# RECAFCO HRMS — Progress Tracker

Update this file after every meaningful implementation change.

## Current Phase

- Phase 1 — Foundation: Unit 1 (database package), Unit 1.1 (local migration verification), Unit 2 (NestJS API foundation), Unit 3 (Auth/RBAC foundation), Unit 4 (Employee/Department/Designation/Work Location API foundation), Unit 5 (Attendance Device Registry API foundation), Unit 6 (Mobile Device Binding API foundation), Unit 7 (Attendance Raw Punch API foundation), Unit 8 (Attendance Basic Validation Engine), Unit 9 (Daily Attendance Summary Calculator), Unit 10 (Attendance Correction Workflow), Unit 11 (Next.js Web Frontend Foundation), Unit 12 (Web Admin Master Data Screens), Unit 13 (Web Admin Device and Mobile Device Screens), Unit 14 (Web Admin Attendance Operations Screens), Unit 15 (Mobile App Foundation), Unit 16 (Mobile Device Registration Screen), Unit 17 (Mobile Attendance Punch Screen), Unit 18 (Mobile Attendance History and Status Screen), Unit 18.1 (Mobile Navigation and Logout UX Fix), Unit 19 (Mobile Attendance Correction Request Screen), Unit 19.2 (Employee User Account Provisioning + Password Change Reminder), Unit 20 (Payroll Lock / Monthly Closing Backend), Unit 19.3 (Employee Web/Mobile Experience Alignment), Unit 19.4 (Mobile Employee App UX Benchmark Alignment), Unit 19.5 (Web Employee Self-Service Benchmark Alignment), Unit 20.1 (Automated Attendance Validation and Daily Auto-Calculation), Unit 19.6 (Mobile Splash Screen and App Branding Polish), Unit 19.6.1 (Mobile Splash Screen Visual Refinement), Unit 19.6.2 (Mobile App Icon Visual Refinement), and Unit 19.6.3 (Mobile Splash and Login Branding Refinement) complete; Monthly Attendance Payroll Basis Calculator (Unit 20.2) is next, then Web Payroll Lock / Monthly Closing Screen (Unit 21).

## Current Goal

Define a spec-driven foundation for building a fast, reliable, payroll-safe internal HRMS with web, Android, and iOS applications.

## Completed

- Initial product direction defined.
- System identified as internal RECAFCO HRMS for 2000+ employees.
- Core stack selected:
  - Next.js web
  - React Native mobile
  - NestJS backend
  - PostgreSQL database
  - Prisma ORM
  - Redis + BullMQ
  - MinIO/S3-compatible storage
  - pnpm monorepo
- Attendance identified as payroll-critical.
- Central attendance engine concept defined.
- Raw punch + validation + daily summary + correction + payroll lock model defined.
- Mixed mobile/device punch support defined.
- Fraud protection requirements identified:
  - Geofence
  - Device binding
  - Fake GPS detection
  - GPS accuracy validation
  - Optional selfie
  - Wi-Fi/QR/site validation
  - Device import audit
  - Manual correction audit
- Spec-driven context file set created.

- UNIT 1 — Database package foundation (`packages/db`) completed:
  - `@recafco/db` package created with Prisma (PostgreSQL provider), pinned to `prisma`/`@prisma/client` 6.19.3.
  - Prisma schema (`packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma`) defines: Department, Designation, WorkLocation, AttendanceDevice, Employee, MobileDevice, Shift, ShiftAssignment, AttendanceRawPunch, AttendanceDailySummary, AttendanceCorrection, AttendancePayrollLock, AuditLog.
  - Enums added: EmployeeStatus, EmploymentType, AttendanceSourceType, PunchType, PunchValidationStatus, DailyAttendanceStatus, CorrectionStatus, PayrollLockStatus.
  - No migration was run against a live database (no Postgres instance available in this environment) — only `prisma generate` and typecheck were verified. `db:migrate` (i.e. `prisma migrate dev`) still needs to run against a real Postgres instance before this schema is considered applied.
  - `packages/db/src/client.ts` exports a singleton `PrismaClient` (dev-mode global caching pattern); `src/index.ts` re-exports it plus all Prisma-generated types.
  - Root `.gitignore` added (previously missing) to keep `.env`, `node_modules`, and build output out of version control, since a real `.env` with a live DB password already existed untracked in the working tree.

- UNIT 1.1 — Local PostgreSQL migration verification completed:
  - Local PostgreSQL 18 server confirmed running and reachable on `localhost:5432` (existing Windows service `postgresql-x64-18`).
  - Database `recafco_hrms_dev` created (did not previously exist).
  - `packages/db/.env` created (gitignored, not committed) by copying the root `.env` value, since Prisma CLI resolves `.env` relative to its own working directory, not the repo root.
  - Migration `20260714054603_init` created and applied via `prisma migrate dev` — applied cleanly against the schema from Unit 1 with **no schema changes needed**.
  - Verified all 13 model tables + `_prisma_migrations` exist in `recafco_hrms_dev` (`\dt` via psql), and confirmed no rows exist in `employees`/`departments`/`attendance_raw_punches` (no demo data was created).
  - `pnpm --filter @recafco/db db:generate`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/db typecheck`, and root `pnpm typecheck` all pass.
  - Operational gotcha found and worth remembering: `pnpm --filter @recafco/db db:migrate -- --name <name>` mis-forwards the `--` separator into the underlying `prisma migrate dev` invocation, causing Prisma to ignore `--name` and drop into an interactive "enter a migration name" prompt (which hangs indefinitely in a non-interactive shell, and can leave an orphaned `node`/`prisma` process holding the Postgres advisory lock — check for and kill it before retrying). Workaround used: run `pnpm exec prisma migrate dev --name <name>` directly from inside `packages/db` instead of through the `db:migrate` package script when a migration name must be supplied non-interactively.

- UNIT 2 — NestJS API foundation + database connection completed:
  - `@recafco/api` created in `apps/api` (NestJS 11.1.28, strict TypeScript, source under `apps/api/src`).
  - **Fixed a real packaging bug in `@recafco/db` discovered while wiring this unit**: the package previously had `"type": "module"` with `"main"`/`"types"` pointing straight at raw `.ts` source. That resolves fine for bundler-based consumers (Next.js) but a CommonJS Node process (`node dist/main.js`, which is exactly how the built API runs) cannot `require()` a `.ts` file — it would have crashed at runtime with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`/syntax errors the first time the API tried to import `@recafco/db`. Fixed by giving `packages/db` a real build step: added `packages/db/tsconfig.build.json` (emits CommonJS to `packages/db/dist`), changed its `build` script to `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json` (previously incorrectly aliased to `tsc --noEmit`, duplicating `typecheck`), and pointed `main`/`types` at `./dist/index.js`/`./dist/index.d.ts`. Verified the compiled output is `require()`-able from plain Node. **`packages/shared` has the same latent issue** (`"type": "module"`, raw-`.ts` `main`) but was left untouched since nothing in this unit consumes it yet — fix it the same way before any CommonJS app (`apps/api`, `apps/mobile`) imports it.
  - `DatabaseModule` (`@Global()`) + `DatabaseService` wrap the existing singleton `prisma` client exported by `@recafco/db` (no second `PrismaClient` instantiated). `onModuleInit` attempts `$connect()` but only logs a warning on failure rather than throwing, so the API still boots and `/health` still works even if PostgreSQL is down; `onModuleDestroy` calls `$disconnect()`, wired up via `app.enableShutdownHooks()` in `main.ts`.
  - `HealthModule`: `GET /health` (always returns `status/service/timestamp`, no DB dependency) and `GET /health/db` (runs `SELECT 1` via Prisma; returns `503 Service Unavailable` with a generic `status/database/timestamp` body — no stack trace or connection string — if the query fails; failure is logged server-side only).
  - Typed env config via a small `zod` schema (`apps/api/src/config/env.schema.ts`) wired into `@nestjs/config`'s `validate` option: `NODE_ENV` (enum, default `development`), `API_PORT` (coerced int, default `4000`), `DATABASE_URL` (required, non-empty). Kept local to `apps/api` rather than creating a `packages/config` env-schema module — `architecture.md` says `packages/config` should eventually own shared env-schema helpers, but nothing else needs env validation yet, so extracting a shared package now would be speculative; flagged as an open question below.
  - `main.ts`: global `ValidationPipe` (`whitelist`, `forbidNonWhitelisted`, `transform`), CORS restricted to `http://localhost:3000` and `http://localhost:19006` (credentials enabled, no wildcard), explicit `1mb` body size limit via `express`'s `json()`/`urlencoded()`, `app.enableShutdownHooks()`.
  - Root `package.json` gained `dev:api` (builds `@recafco/db` then runs `@recafco/api` dev) without touching the existing `dev`/`build`/`lint`/`typecheck`/`db:*` scripts. `turbo.json`'s `dev` task now has `dependsOn: ["^build"]` so `turbo dev` always builds workspace library deps (like `@recafco/db`) before starting persistent app dev tasks — needed for the same reason as the `@recafco/db` packaging fix above.
  - **Second operational gotcha found and fixed at the root cause**: `apps/api/nest-cli.json` initially had `"deleteOutDir": true`, which raced with `nest start --watch`'s incremental compiler on this machine — the CLI wiped `dist/` right as it tried to run the freshly compiled `dist/main.js`, crashing with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on the very first watch-mode boot. Root-caused (not just a stale-cache issue: a clean rebuild reproduced it) and fixed by setting `"deleteOutDir": false` in `nest-cli.json` — `nest build` (one-shot production build) is unaffected. Also added `*.tsbuildinfo` to `.gitignore` (TypeScript's incremental build cache; was untracked but not previously ignored).
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm install`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/api build`, `pnpm typecheck` (root, all 3 packages) all pass. Ran `pnpm --filter @recafco/api dev`, confirmed `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok","service":"recafco-hrms-api","timestamp":...}` and `GET /health/db` → `200 {"status":"ok","database":"connected","timestamp":...}` against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database, confirmed CORS preflight allows `http://localhost:3000` with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`, then cleanly stopped the verification server and confirmed port 4000 was released.
  - `apps/api/.env` created (gitignored, not committed) the same way as `packages/db/.env` in Unit 1.1 — copied `DATABASE_URL`/`API_PORT` from root `.env`, added `NODE_ENV=development`, password never printed.

- UNIT 3 — Auth/RBAC foundation completed:
  - **Schema**: added `UserStatus` enum (`ACTIVE`/`DISABLED`) and six models — `User`, `Role`, `Permission`, `UserRole`, `RolePermission`, `RefreshToken`. `Role`/`Permission` are DB-backed rows with a unique `code` string (not Prisma enums), matching the spec's intent that they be extensible without a migration. Migration `20260714061307_auth_rbac_foundation` created and applied via `pnpm exec prisma migrate dev --name auth_rbac_foundation` from `packages/db` (per the operational note, not through the broken `--filter ... -- --name` path). Verified all 6 new tables (`users`, `roles`, `permissions`, `user_roles`, `role_permissions`, `refresh_tokens`) exist via `psql \dt`.
  - **Converted the 3 named plain-string user-ID fields to real relations**, since `attendance_corrections` and `audit_logs` were both empty (confirmed in Unit 1.1) and this was explicitly safe: `AttendanceCorrection.requestedByUserId` → `requestedBy User` (required, relation name `CorrectionRequestedBy`), `AttendanceCorrection.approvedByUserId` → `approvedBy User?` (relation name `CorrectionApprovedBy`), `AuditLog.actorUserId` → `actor User?`. `AttendancePayrollLock.lockedByUserId`/`reopenedByUserId` were deliberately **not** converted — the task only asked for the three fields above, so those stay plain strings for now (see open questions).
  - **`PasswordResetToken` was deliberately not built** — out of scope per the unit's own exclusions (no email/OTP yet), and the task marked it optional/only-if-needed.
  - **Password hashing**: tested `argon2` directly on this Windows machine first (hash+verify round-trip) before committing to it — it installed and ran with zero native-build friction (prebuilt binary via `node-gyp-build`), so used `argon2id` per the task's stated preference rather than falling back to `bcryptjs`. Added `argon2` to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `onlyBuiltDependencies`/`allowBuilds` (same pattern as Prisma in Unit 1).
  - **JWT design**: access tokens (`JWT_ACCESS_SECRET`, default 15m) carry `{ sub, username, roles, permissions }` so `PermissionsGuard` never needs a DB round-trip per request. Refresh tokens (`JWT_REFRESH_SECRET`, default 7d) are *also* real signed JWTs (`{ sub, jti }`) per the task's explicit env-var design, but the DB (`refresh_tokens.tokenHash`, a SHA-256 hex hash of the full JWT string) is the actual source of truth for validity/revocation — a stolen-but-expired-in-DB JWT is rejected even if its own `exp` claim hasn't elapsed, and vice versa. Refresh tokens are rotated on every use (old row's `revokedAt` set, a new row + token issued) — verified manually that reusing a pre-rotation refresh token is rejected with 401. Raw refresh tokens are never stored, only their hash.
  - **RBAC**: 17 permissions and the 9 system roles from `context/architecture.md`'s suggested permission groups / this task's role list, defined in `apps/api/src/auth/constants/rbac.constants.ts` along with a provisional role→permission default mapping (documented as provisional in an open question below — not confirmed HR policy). `@Permissions(...)` decorator + `PermissionsGuard` (reads `request.user.permissions`, populated by `JwtStrategy` from the access token) is the reusable route-protection mechanism; `JwtAuthGuard` handles authentication. No permanent demo/test route was added — `GET /auth/me` (JWT-only, no specific permission) was sufficient to verify the guard chain.
  - **Endpoints**: `POST /auth/login` (identifier = username or email + password; generic "Invalid credentials" for every failure mode — unknown user, wrong password, disabled, locked — so none are distinguishable from outside), `POST /auth/refresh` (rotates), `POST /auth/logout` (revokes the presented refresh token; idempotent — an already-revoked or unknown token is a silent no-op, not an error), `GET /auth/me` (JWT-protected, returns id/username/email/employeeId/status/mustChangePassword/lastLoginAt/roles/permissions — never `passwordHash`).
  - **Lockout**: 5 failed attempts locks the account for 15 minutes (`lockedUntil`), and `failedLoginAttempts` resets to 0 at the moment of locking (so the next window after expiry starts clean rather than accumulating). A correct password presented while locked is still rejected (verified manually) — the lock check happens before password verification, so no argon2 CPU cost is spent on a locked account.
  - **Audit**: `AuditService` (`@Global()`, in `apps/api/src/audit/`) wraps `auditLog.create` in a try/catch that only logs a warning on failure rather than throwing, so a transient audit-write problem can't itself block a login/logout/refresh. Records `LOGIN_SUCCESS`, `LOGIN_FAILED` (with a `reason` in `afterSnapshot`: `not_found`/`disabled`/`locked`/`invalid_password`), `USER_LOCKED`, `REFRESH_TOKEN_USED`, `LOGOUT`, and `BOOTSTRAP_SUPER_ADMIN_CREATED`. Verified all of these actually appear in `audit_logs` in the correct order during manual testing.
  - **Bootstrap script**: `apps/api/src/bootstrap/create-super-admin.ts`, run via `pnpm --filter @recafco/api create:super-admin` (uses `ts-node`, added as a devDependency). Reads `BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_USERNAME`/`BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD`/`BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAIL` from `.env` (loads it itself via the `dotenv` package, since `ts-node` doesn't auto-load `.env` the way Nest's `ConfigModule` or the Prisma CLI do — a real gap, not just a one-off test issue, so `dotenv` was added as a proper dependency, not worked around). Upserts all 17 permissions and 9 roles (idempotent), skips user creation entirely if the username already exists (verified by running it twice), never touches the `employees` table, never logs the password. Rejects passwords under 12 characters.
  - **Reused `@recafco/db`'s existing CommonJS build fix from Unit 2** — no new packaging work needed since Auth code imports `@recafco/db` (already fixed) and does **not** import `@recafco/shared` (deliberately — see open questions; its enum values were used as a naming reference only, not imported, to avoid re-triggering the same raw-`.ts`-`main` problem in a new package).
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: `pnpm install`, `db:generate`, `@recafco/db typecheck`, `@recafco/api typecheck`, `@recafco/api build`, root `pnpm typecheck` all pass. Ran the bootstrap script (twice, to confirm idempotency), started `pnpm --filter @recafco/api dev`, and manually exercised the full flow: `/health` and `/health/db` still 200, login → 200 with token pair, `/auth/me` → 200 with correct profile/roles/permissions and no password hash, refresh → 200 with rotated tokens + old token now rejected, logout → 200 + the logged-out token now rejected, 5× wrong password → generic 401 each time + correct password rejected once locked, confirmed via direct `psql` query that `lockedUntil`/`failedLoginAttempts` and the full audit trail matched expectations. Cleanly stopped the verification server afterward (had to explicitly `Stop-Process` the two child PIDs, same `TaskStop`-doesn't-kill-grandchildren caveat as Unit 2) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch the unrelated `recafco-maintenance-management-system` Next.js dev processes running on this machine.

- UNIT 4 — Employee/Department/Designation/Work Location API foundation completed:
  - **Schema**: added `WorkLocationType` enum (`OFFICE`/`FACTORY`/`WAREHOUSE`/`PROJECT_SITE`, default `OFFICE`) + `WorkLocation.type` field, and `Employee.civilId` (nullable, unique). `Department`/`Designation`/`WorkLocation.isActive` already existed from Unit 1 — no migration needed for those, contrary to the task's assumption that they might be missing.
  - **Migration `20260714063921_master_data_api_foundation`** applied via a workaround: `prisma migrate dev --create-only` (and even plain `migrate dev`) refused non-interactively because adding the `civilId` unique constraint triggers a data-loss-style warning prompt, and this environment has no TTY to confirm it — piping `y` into stdin didn't help either, since Prisma explicitly detects non-interactive environments and refuses regardless of piped input. Root-caused instead of forced: generated the exact SQL via `prisma migrate diff --from-url "$DATABASE_URL" --to-schema-datamodel ./prisma/schema.prisma --script` (fully non-interactive, no prompts), hand-created the standard `<timestamp>_master_data_api_foundation/migration.sql` folder with that output, then applied it with `prisma migrate deploy` (never prompts, exactly as the CLI's own error message suggested). Verified the generated SQL was correct before applying, and confirmed via `psql` afterward that `civilId`/`type` landed exactly as expected. This is a real, repeatable gotcha for any future migration that would trigger a Prisma warning prompt (a new required column with a default is fine and doesn't prompt; a new unique constraint on an existing column can prompt even on an empty table) — the `migrate diff` + hand-built-folder + `migrate deploy` sequence is the reliable non-interactive workaround.
  - **13 new permissions** added to `apps/api/src/auth/constants/rbac.constants.ts`: `employees.disable` (was missing since Unit 3 despite `employees.read/create/update` existing) plus `departments.*`/`designations.*`/`workLocations.*` (`read`/`create`/`update`/`disable` each). Total permission count: 17 → 30. Role→permission mapping follows the existing Unit 3 pattern (elevated `*.disable` actions reserved for `HR_MANAGER`, not `HR_ADMIN`; read-only master-data visibility given to `DEPARTMENT_MANAGER`/`SUPERVISOR`/`PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER`). `SUPER_ADMIN` needed no manual change since its permission list is derived (`ALL_PERMISSION_CODES`), not hardcoded. Re-ran `pnpm --filter @recafco/api create:super-admin` (idempotent) to upsert the new permissions/role-links onto the existing bootstrapped roles — verified `/auth/me` for the existing `superadmin` user went from 17 to 30 permissions without needing to recreate the user. Also appended the new permission codes to `architecture.md`'s "Suggested Permission Groups" list to keep it in sync (that list was already visibly out of sync with Unit 3's actual implementation before this — not fully reconciled here, see open questions).
  - **Four new feature modules** (`departments`, `designations`, `work-locations`, `employees`), each following an identical Controller → Service → DTO pattern: `JwtAuthGuard` + `PermissionsGuard` on the controller, `@Permissions('<domain>.read'|'create'|'update'|'disable')` per route, paginated `GET` list (page/limit/search + relevant filters), `GET /:id` (404 if missing), `POST` (create), `PATCH /:id` (field update, does not touch `isActive`/`status` directly for the domains where that's a deliberate separate action), and `DELETE /:id` mapped to a **soft** deactivate/terminate — no row is ever physically deleted. Every create/update/deactivate is audited (before/after snapshots, actor, IP, user-agent); read endpoints are not audited, per the task's own instruction.
  - **Naming choices kept the existing schema's field names rather than renaming to match the task's prose exactly**: `Designation.title` (task said "name"), `WorkLocation.geofenceRadiusMeters` (task said "allowedRadiusMeters" — same concept, pre-existing established term from Unit 1's fraud/geofence context), `Employee.hireDate` (task said "joiningDate"), `Employee.firstName`/`lastName` (task said "fullName" — the API response adds a derived, non-persisted `fullName` convenience field instead of renaming the stored columns). Renaming real columns for purely cosmetic reasons wasn't judged worth a migration.
  - **`EmployeesModule` specifics**: `departmentId`/`designationId`/`workLocationId` existence is checked in the service layer before any write (`assertReferencesExist`), returning a clean `400 Bad Request` naming the offending field rather than letting a raw Postgres foreign-key violation surface — verified manually with a nonexistent `departmentId`. `UpdateEmployeeDto.status` deliberately excludes `TERMINATED` (via `@IsIn` against the other four `EmployeeStatus` values) — that transition is only reachable through `DELETE /employees/:id`, which also stamps `terminationDate`, keeping status and termination date from ever going out of sync. `employeeCode` is immutable after creation (not present in `UpdateEmployeeDto`). No `User` row is ever created as a side effect of employee create/update, per the hard requirement. List/detail responses include minimal nested `department`/`designation`/`workLocation` objects (id + name/title + code) to save API consumers a round trip.
  - **Consolidated a small duplication while wiring this unit**: `apps/api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts` had a private `contextFromRequest()` helper; since 4 new controllers now need the identical IP/user-agent extraction, it was extracted to `apps/api/src/common/request-context.util.ts` (`getRequestContext`), and `auth.controller.ts`/`auth.types.ts` were updated to re-use/re-export it instead of keeping a duplicate.
  - **New shared `apps/api/src/common/` utilities** (justified by 4x reuse within this single unit): `PaginationQueryDto` (page ≥1 default 1, limit 1–100 default 20, optional `search`), `paginate()`/`paginationSkip()` helpers (consistent `{ data, meta: { page, limit, total, totalPages } }` envelope), `ParseCuidPipe` (rejects malformed `:id` params with a clean `400` before even hitting the database), `isUniqueConstraintError`/`isForeignKeyConstraintError`/`isRecordNotFoundError` (Prisma `P2002`/`P2003`/`P2025` detection, used to turn raw DB constraint violations into clean `409`/`400` responses instead of leaking Prisma internals), and `toJsonSnapshot()` (normalizes a Prisma model — which contains `Date` objects — into a JSON-safe value before it goes into an audit log's `Json` column; this exact `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` + Prisma-generated-type interaction bit both `AuditService` in Unit 3, now fixed in passing, and `EmployeesService`'s create/update `data` objects in this unit — see open questions for the general pattern).
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/generate/typecheck(×2)/build/root-typecheck suite passed; started the API, confirmed `/health` and `/health/db` still 200; created a department/designation/work location/employee through the real HTTP API as the bootstrapped super admin, confirmed list pagination + search, confirmed PATCH updates, confirmed `DELETE` soft-deactivates (row stays, `isActive`/`status` flips) rather than removing rows, confirmed invalid latitude/longitude (200/−400) is rejected with a clean `400` and a valid one succeeds, confirmed an employee created with a bogus `departmentId` is rejected with `400` and one with real FK ids succeeds and returns nested relation data + `fullName`, confirmed an anonymous request is rejected `401` and a real-but-low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user is rejected `403` on both a `departments.create` and an `employees.read` attempt, and confirmed via direct `psql` query that every create/update/deactivate/terminate action produced exactly the expected `audit_logs` row. **All test/verification data (the test department, designation, work location, employee, and the temporary low-privilege test user) was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are empty again and `users` contains only the one legitimate `superadmin` bootstrap account. Cleanly stopped the verification server afterward (again had to explicitly `Stop-Process` the two child PIDs — the same `TaskStop`-doesn't-kill-grandchildren caveat noted in Units 2 and 3) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 5 — Attendance Device Registry API foundation completed:
  - **Schema**: added `AttendanceDeviceType` enum (`BIOMETRIC`/`FACE_RECOGNITION`/`CARD`/`QR_TERMINAL`/`TURNSTILE`/`OTHER`) and `AttendanceConnectionMode` enum (`NETWORK_PULL`/`API`/`DATABASE_SYNC`/`FILE_IMPORT`/`MANUAL_IMPORT`/`UNKNOWN`, default `UNKNOWN`). Added to `AttendanceDevice`: `deviceType` (required), `vendor`/`model`/`serialNumber` (nullable, `serialNumber` unique), `port` (nullable Int), `connectionMode` (default `UNKNOWN`), `notes` (nullable). Kept the existing `deviceCode` (task said "code") and `sourceType` fields unchanged — see the `deviceType` vs. `sourceType` decision below. **Did not add `syncStatus`** — the task explicitly left it as "nullable if useful," and with no device-sync logic existing yet (out of scope for this unit), nothing would ever set or read it; adding an inert column was judged not worth it. Indexed `deviceType` and `connectionMode` for the new list filters.
  - **Migration `20260714071126_attendance_device_registry`** hit the exact same non-interactive `migrate dev` refusal as Unit 4 (the new `serialNumber` unique constraint triggers the warning prompt even on an empty table) — used the same now-documented workaround: `prisma migrate diff --from-url ... --script` → hand-built `<timestamp>_attendance_device_registry/migration.sql` → `prisma migrate deploy`. Verified the generated SQL before applying, and confirmed the full column/index/FK layout via `psql \d attendance_devices` afterward.
  - **5 new permissions** (`attendanceDevices.read/create/update/disable/testConnection`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, following the task's own explicit suggested role table exactly (not the Unit 3/4 "disable is elevated to HR_MANAGER" pattern) — `HR_ADMIN` gets read/create/update/disable directly, `HR_MANAGER`/`PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get read-only, `testConnection` is `SUPER_ADMIN`-only (derived via `ALL_PERMISSION_CODES`, not hardcoded elsewhere), and `EMPLOYEE`/`SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` get nothing — the task said "if in doubt, keep supervisor access out," and department manager wasn't mentioned in its suggested table at all, so both were left out entirely. Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent) to upsert the new permissions/role-links onto existing roles — `/auth/me` went from 30 to 35 permissions without touching the existing user.
  - **`deviceType` vs. `sourceType`**: rather than repurposing or extending the existing `AttendanceSourceType` enum (which already has device-like values `BIOMETRIC_DEVICE`/`FACE_DEVICE`/`CARD_DEVICE` but serves a different purpose — tagging raw punches for the not-yet-built attendance engine, per Unit 1's architecture, and also covers non-device sources like `MOBILE`/`WEB`), added the new `deviceType` as a separate, user-facing hardware-category field. `sourceType` is derived automatically from `deviceType` via a fixed mapping in `AttendanceDevicesService` (`BIOMETRIC`→`BIOMETRIC_DEVICE`, `FACE_RECOGNITION`→`FACE_DEVICE`, `CARD`→`CARD_DEVICE`, `QR_TERMINAL`/`TURNSTILE`/`OTHER`→`FUTURE_INTEGRATION`, its designated catch-all) and is never exposed in the create/update DTOs — it exists only because the column is a pre-existing required field with no default, not because this unit needed a second type system.
  - **`AttendanceDevicesModule`**: standard `JwtAuthGuard` + `PermissionsGuard` + `@Permissions()` pattern from Units 4. `workLocationId` must reference an existing **and active** work location (`assertActiveWorkLocation`) — stricter than `EmployeesService`'s FK check, per this task's explicit "moving device to another active work location only if valid" requirement — checked on both create and the same-named field on update. `ipAddress` accepts either a valid IPv4 address or a bare LAN hostname (no TLD required, since on-prem devices are commonly addressed as e.g. `biometric-hq` with no domain) via a combined regex, not `class-validator`'s built-in `@IsIP`/`@IsFQDN` (which would reject the hostname case). `POST /attendance-devices/:id/test-connection` returns the exact placeholder response the task specified (`{ status: "not_configured", message: "..." }`) and audits the attempt (`ATTENDANCE_DEVICE_TEST_CONNECTION_ATTEMPTED`) after confirming the device exists (404 if not) — no network call, no vendor SDK, exactly as scoped.
  - No new `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`/Prisma checked-vs-unchecked type errors this time — applied the Unit 4 lesson from the start (explicitly-typed intermediate `data: Prisma.AttendanceDeviceUncheckedCreateInput`/`UncheckedUpdateInput` objects, `?? null` for nullable fields, `if (x !== undefined)` guards for defaulted-but-non-nullable fields like `connectionMode`) — `pnpm --filter @recafco/api typecheck` passed on the first attempt.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/generate/typecheck(×2)/build/root-typecheck suite passed on the first attempt; started the API, confirmed `/health`/`/health/db` still 200; created a test work location, then an attendance device linked to it as the bootstrapped super admin — confirmed an invalid `workLocationId` is rejected `400`, a valid one succeeds and returns the correct auto-derived `sourceType`; confirmed list pagination, `workLocationId` filter, and `deviceType` filter all return the right row; confirmed a duplicate `deviceCode` is rejected `409`; confirmed `PATCH` update (including switching `ipAddress` to a bare hostname) succeeds; confirmed `POST .../test-connection` returns the exact specified placeholder body; confirmed `DELETE` soft-deactivates (`isActive:false`, row retained); confirmed an anonymous request is rejected `401` and a low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user is rejected `403`; confirmed via `psql` that create/update/test-connection/deactivate each produced the expected `audit_logs` row in order. **All verification data (the test work location, attendance device, and temporary low-privilege test user) was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_devices`/`work_locations`/`departments`/`designations`/`employees` are all empty and `users` has only `superadmin`. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Stop-Process`-on-two-child-PIDs pattern as prior units) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 6 — Mobile Device Binding API foundation completed:
  - **Schema**: added `MobilePlatform` enum (`ANDROID`/`IOS`/`UNKNOWN`) and `MobileDeviceStatus` enum (`PENDING_APPROVAL`/`APPROVED`/`BLOCKED`/`DEACTIVATED`, default `PENDING_APPROVAL`). On `MobileDevice`: **dropped** `deviceModel`, `osName`, and `isActive` (all superseded, not just renamed — see decision below); **added** `platform` (required), `deviceName`/`manufacturer`/`model`/`osVersion` (kept)/`appVersion` (kept), `status`, `isPrimary` (default `false`), `approvedAt`/`approvedByUserId`/`blockedAt`/`blockedByUserId`/`blockReason` (all nullable). `approvedByUserId`/`blockedByUserId` are real `User` relations (`approvedBy`/`blockedBy`, via named relations `MobileDeviceApprovedBy`/`MobileDeviceBlockedBy`), following the Unit 3 precedent of converting plain-string actor-ID fields to relations when the target table is provably empty. Kept `pushToken`/`registeredAt` untouched (harmless, not in scope to remove). Indexed `status`/`platform`/`isPrimary` for the new list filters (`employeeId` index already existed).
  - **Migration `20260714073057_mobile_device_binding`** applied via plain `prisma migrate dev` on the very first attempt — no interactive-prompt refusal this time, unlike Units 4/5. Column *drops* (even on an empty table) apparently don't trigger the same data-loss-style warning that new *unique constraints* do; only genuinely new/changed constraints seem to need the `migrate diff`/`migrate deploy` workaround. Verified the generated SQL and the full `\d mobile_devices` layout via `psql` before and after — matched the intended schema exactly.
  - **No new field kept for redundancy**: unlike Unit 5's `deviceType`/`sourceType` split (kept both because `sourceType` had an established downstream consumer), `osName` and `isActive` had zero established consumers anywhere in the codebase or `architecture.md`, so they were dropped outright in favor of `platform` and `status` respectively rather than kept alongside them — avoids two competing sources of truth for the same concept (e.g., `isActive:true` while `status:BLOCKED` would have been a real inconsistent-state risk).
  - **6 new permissions** (`mobileDevices.read/register/approve/block/reset/update`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, following the task's suggested table: `HR_ADMIN` gets all 6, `HR_MANAGER` gets read/approve/block/reset (not register/update), `PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get read-only, `EMPLOYEE` gets `mobileDevices.register` only (not the general `.read`, which is reserved for the HR/admin list endpoint — an employee's own-device visibility is served entirely by the separate JWT-only `/me` endpoint, not by widening `.read`), `SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` get nothing (task said "none unless context requires it"). Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent) — `/auth/me` went from 35 to 41 permissions without touching the existing user.
  - **`MobileDevicesModule`**: `GET /mobile-devices/me` is JWT-only (no `@Permissions()`), matching `GET /auth/me`'s established precedent — an employee's own devices aren't gated behind the admin `mobileDevices.read` permission at all, they're inherently scoped to the caller. Route order matters: `@Get('me')` is declared before `@Get(':id')` in the controller so Nest doesn't try to resolve `me` as a `:id` value. `POST /mobile-devices/register` resolves the employee via `User.employeeId` looked up from the JWT's `userId` (the JWT payload itself has no `employeeId`) — returns a clean `400` if the current user isn't linked to an employee. Re-registering an already-known `deviceIdentifier` for the *same* employee updates metadata + `lastSeenAt` in place (verified: same row `id`, no duplicate) rather than creating a new row; a **different** employee registering an already-claimed `deviceIdentifier` is rejected `409` (verified with two real test employees/users). Registration does a **full replace** of the optional metadata fields on refresh, not a merge — deliberate, since a real mobile client always resends its complete current state, not partial fields (see open questions).
  - **Primary-device handling on `POST /:id/approve`**: only `isPrimary` is flipped on any other approved device for the same employee (via `$transaction` + `updateMany`), not its `status` — the previous primary stays `APPROVED`, just non-primary. Chosen deliberately over auto-blocking/deactivating the old device as a side effect, since the task's own principle ("every status change must go through explicit endpoint") reads as applying to *status* transitions specifically; `isPrimary` is a lower-stakes attribute flip, not a security-status change. Verified manually: approving a second device for the same employee correctly demoted the first (still `APPROVED`, `isPrimary:false`) without touching its `status`. A `BLOCKED` device cannot be approved directly (`400`, must go through `/reset` first) — no such restriction exists for a `DEACTIVATED`/`PENDING_APPROVAL` device, since re-approving a previously-reset device is a legitimate, expected flow (e.g., re-approving a returned device).
  - **`POST /:id/block` requires `reason`** (verified: omitting it returns a clean `400` from DTO validation before the handler even runs) and additionally clears `isPrimary` for defense-in-depth, so no future code path can trust a blocked device by checking `isPrimary` alone without also checking `status`. **`POST /:id/reset`** only touches `status`→`DEACTIVATED` and `isPrimary`→`false` per the task's own "recommended" minimal change — deliberately does **not** clear `approvedAt`/`approvedByUserId`/`blockedAt`/etc., preserving them as historical record (the audit log's before/after snapshots already capture the full transition, so clearing these fields would only lose information for no benefit).
  - **`PATCH /:id` (admin metadata update) has no `status`/`isPrimary`/`approved*`/`blocked*` fields in its DTO at all** — combined with the global `ValidationPipe`'s `forbidNonWhitelisted` (from Unit 2), attempting to sneak a `status` field into the request body is actively rejected with a `400` (`"property status should not exist"`), not silently dropped — verified manually. This structurally enforces "do not allow patching security status through the generic update endpoint" rather than relying on service-layer discipline alone.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/generate/typecheck(×2)/build/root-typecheck suite passed on the first attempt (no new `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` issues — same explicit-intermediate-`data`-object pattern from Units 4–5 applied from the start); started the API; created two test employees + two test users (one `EMPLOYEE`-role each, linked via `User.employeeId`) using a throwaway script (same pattern as Units 4/5, since no admin user-creation endpoint exists yet); as employee 1: registered a device (→ `PENDING_APPROVAL`), confirmed `/me` returns only that one device, re-registered the same identifier and confirmed the same row updated (not duplicated); as super admin: listed devices, approved device 1 as primary, registered+approved a second device for employee 1 and confirmed device 1 was correctly demoted (still `APPROVED`, `isPrimary:false`), blocked device 1 with a reason, reset device 2; as employee 2: confirmed registering employee 1's `deviceIdentifier` is rejected `409`; confirmed anonymous access is `401` and employee-role access to admin-only actions (`approve`, list) is `403`; confirmed via `psql` that all 8 expected `audit_logs` rows (`MOBILE_DEVICE_REGISTERED` ×2, `_REGISTRATION_REFRESHED`, `_APPROVED` ×2, `_BLOCKED`, `_RESET`, `_UPDATED`) appeared in the correct order. **All verification data (2 departments/designations/work-locations/employees worth of shared test master data, 2 mobile devices, 2 temporary test users) was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that every relevant table is back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Stop-Process`-on-two-child-PIDs pattern as prior units) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 7 — Attendance Raw Punch API foundation completed:
  - **Schema: no migration needed.** `AttendanceRawPunch` already had every field this unit required, going all the way back to Unit 1 — confirmed by reading the current schema before writing any code, per the task's own explicit instruction to only add fields "if genuinely needed." First unit since Unit 3 with zero schema changes.
  - **5 new permissions** (`attendancePunches.read/createMobile/createDeviceImport/createManual/readOwn`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, following the task's suggested table: `HR_ADMIN` gets all 5 (including `createMobile`/`readOwn`, which read oddly for an admin role but match the task's literal "HR_ADMIN: all" instruction, same pattern as Unit 6), `HR_MANAGER` gets read + createManual, `PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get read-only, `EMPLOYEE` gets createMobile + readOwn, `SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` get nothing (task left both as "if needed"/"if context requires it" — deferred, see open questions). Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent) — `/auth/me` went from 41 to 46 permissions without touching the existing user. **Note**: this creates a second, differently-named permission covering similar ground to Unit 3's pre-existing `attendance.punch.read` (dot-separated, singular) — not reconciled, see open questions.
  - **Validation-status decision (explicitly requested by the task): `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`**, chosen over `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`, uniformly across all three intake paths (mobile, device-import, manual) when nothing else disqualifies the punch. Reasoning: no real GPS/geofence/fraud validation engine exists yet (that's Unit 8), so labeling anything "ACCEPTED" — even "with warning" — would falsely imply some check actually ran and merely flagged a minor issue. `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` honestly represents "received, not yet validated by any engine, needs human review before being trusted" — the safer, more payroll-conservative choice per `CLAUDE.md`'s "treat attendance like a financial transaction" principle. `isOfficial` is always `false` at intake regardless of path, per the task's explicit instruction, until Unit 8/9 exist.
  - **Mobile punch device-approval handling (the other core design decision this unit required)**: every one of the four possible outcomes (device not found / device belongs to a different employee / device found but not `APPROVED` / device found, correct employee, `APPROVED`) **always stores a raw punch** — the only case that returns a plain `400` with nothing stored is when the *current user has no linked employee at all* (no identifiable actor). This follows `CLAUDE.md`'s non-negotiable "every punch attempt must be stored, including rejected and suspicious attempts" literally: an unknown/mismatched/unapproved device is itself exactly the kind of suspicious attempt that must be recorded, not silently discarded. All three rejection cases use `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` (the closest existing enum value — there's no separate "not yet approved" value) with a `fraudFlags` entry distinguishing the specific reason (`DEVICE_NOT_REGISTERED`/`DEVICE_BELONGS_TO_DIFFERENT_EMPLOYEE`/`DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED`); `mobileDeviceId` is only set on the raw punch when we actually know which device row it is (i.e. not for the "device not found" case). Verified manually: an unapproved-device attempt returned `201` (not an error) with `validationStatus:REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE`, and the identical request succeeded with `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` immediately after the device was approved.
  - **`fraudScore` is never accepted from the client** — always hardcoded to `0` at creation, per the task's explicit "prefer server-calculated default" guidance and `architecture.md`'s invariant that mobile-reported signals are inputs to backend validation, not authoritative themselves. A client-supplied `fraudSignals` object *is* accepted and stored verbatim into `fraudFlags` (tagged `CLIENT_REPORTED_SIGNALS`) for the future validation engine to read — the raw signal is preserved, but scoring it is explicitly deferred, not faked.
  - **Device-import idempotency reuses the existing `@@unique([attendanceDeviceId, sourceId])` constraint from Unit 1** (no schema change needed) — a pre-check `findFirst` returns the already-imported record directly (verified: identical `id` returned on a duplicate `POST`, no new row), with a unique-constraint-violation catch as a race-condition fallback for concurrent duplicate imports. Chose "return the existing record" over "clean 409 conflict" since real device-sync retries (network hiccups re-sending the same log entry) should be transparent and idempotent, not error-producing — matches `code-standards.md`'s "device imports must be idempotent" requirement, and mirrors the exact reasoning already used for the analogous `attendanceDeviceId`/`serialNumber` uniqueness decisions in Unit 5. `sourceType` for device-import punches is read directly from `AttendanceDevice.sourceType` (already correctly derived from `deviceType` back in Unit 5) — no new source-mapping logic needed in this unit at all, since the task's own "basic source mapping" instruction only applies when a device lacks a `sourceType`, which none of ours do.
  - **`GET /attendance-punches/me` returns a clean `400` (not an empty list) when the current user has no linked employee** — different from Unit 6's `/me` (which returns an empty array in the same situation) because this task explicitly specified the `400` behavior for this endpoint, unlike Unit 6's task. The differing precedent between the two `/me` endpoints is intentional, not an inconsistency — each unit's own task spec was followed literally.
  - **Extracted `resolveEmployeeIdForUser(prisma, userId)` to `apps/api/src/common/current-employee.util.ts`**, reused by both `MobileDevicesService.register` (refactored from its own private copy — small consolidation, matching the Unit 4 precedent) and this unit's `createMobile`/`findMine`. `MobileDevicesService.findMine` was deliberately **not** refactored onto this shared helper, since it needs the empty-list behavior, not the throwing one — kept as its own inline check.
  - **Root-caused a real TypeScript error hit for the first time in this unit**: `apps/api/tsconfig.json` had `"declaration": true` (inherited from the original Nest scaffold), which made `tsc` fail with `TS2742` ("inferred type ... cannot be named without a reference to .../@prisma/client/runtime/library") on any controller method returning a Prisma model containing a `Json`-typed field (`AttendanceRawPunch.fraudFlags`/`rawPayload` are the first `Json` fields any controller in this codebase has returned). `apps/api` is a standalone deployable application — nothing imports type declarations from it — so declaration emission was serving no purpose and was simply disabled, not worked around with type assertions. Confirmed the fix doesn't affect `nest build`'s output or any other package.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/generate/typecheck(×2)/build/root-typecheck suite passed after the `declaration` fix; started the API; built one full set of test master data (department/designation/work-location/employee/attendance-device) plus one `EMPLOYEE`-role test user via the established throwaway-script pattern; as the employee: registered a mobile device, attempted a mobile punch while the device was still `PENDING_APPROVAL` (→ `201`, `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE`, stored not rejected-outright), then — after the super admin approved the device — the identical punch succeeded (→ `201`, `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`), and `GET /me` correctly returned both; as the super admin: a device-import punch created correctly with the device's own `sourceType`, an immediate duplicate import (same `attendanceDeviceId`+`sourceId`) returned the exact same record (verified identical `id`, no new row), a manual punch without `reason` was cleanly rejected `400` and with `reason` succeeded (`MANUAL_CORRECTION`, `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`), the admin list correctly filtered by `employeeId`/`validationStatus`/`sourceType`, and the detail endpoint returned relation data; confirmed an invalid `workLocationId` on a mobile punch is rejected `400`; confirmed anonymous access is `401` and the low-privilege employee is `403` on list/manual/device-import; confirmed via `psql` that exactly the 4 expected `audit_logs` rows appeared (`ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_MOBILE_CREATED` ×2, `_DEVICE_IMPORT_CREATED` ×1 — correctly not duplicated for the idempotent re-import — `_MANUAL_CREATED` ×1). **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that every relevant table (`attendance_raw_punches`, `mobile_devices`, `employees`, `attendance_devices`, `departments`, `designations`, `work_locations`) is back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Stop-Process`-on-two-child-PIDs pattern as prior units) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 8 — Attendance Basic Validation Engine completed:
  - **Schema**: added `AttendanceRawPunch.validatedAt` (`DateTime?`) and `validatedByUserId` (`String?`, real `User` relation `validatedBy`/`PunchValidatedBy`, mirroring the `MobileDevice.approvedByUserId`/`blockedByUserId` precedent from Unit 6). Migration `20260714082307_attendance_punch_validation_metadata` applied cleanly via plain `prisma migrate dev` (no interactive-prompt refusal — adding nullable columns + an FK to an already-populated-pattern table doesn't trigger the warning the way new unique constraints do). **Decision**: kept these fields rather than relying on `AuditLog` alone, since "who validated this punch and when" is a natural query directly against the punch row itself (e.g., for a future HR dashboard), not just something buried in an audit trail — consistent with how every other "reviewed/approved" entity in this schema (`MobileDevice`) already exposes its own actor/timestamp metadata.
  - **`AttendanceValidationService`** added inside the existing `AttendancePunchesModule` (not a separate module — tightly coupled to the same table/permission prefix, per the task's own "keep inside if cleaner" option) in `apps/api/src/attendance-punches/attendance-validation.service.ts`. Recommended method shape followed closely: a private `computeValidationResult`/`computeAndApplyValidation` core, plus `validateMobilePunch`/`validateDevicePunch`/`validateManualPunch`/`resolveDuplicateOriginal`/`hasFraudSignal` and a standalone `calculateDistanceMeters` (Haversine) helper.
  - **Common rules (all sources), checked first, in order**: (1) employee is re-fetched fresh (not trusted from intake time) and must be `ACTIVE` → `REJECTED_EMPLOYEE_INACTIVE` (fraudScore 100) and short-circuits everything else if not; (2) duplicate detection — same `employeeId`+`punchType`+`sourceType`, and either the same `sourceId` or (when no `sourceId`, e.g. mobile/manual) a `punchTimeSource` within a 2-minute window of an *earlier-created* candidate row → `REJECTED_DUPLICATE` (fraudScore 80) + `duplicateOfPunchId` set to the earliest matching row, short-circuits source-specific checks. A punch that already has `duplicateOfPunchId` set is re-confirmed against that same original on every re-validation rather than re-searched, keeping duplicate status stable across repeated validation runs.
  - **Mobile rules**: employee's own `isMobilePunchAllowed`/`isDeviceOnly` flags are checked first (an addition beyond the task's literal rule list, since the schema already carries these two employee-level flags from Unit 1 with no consumer anywhere yet — a validation engine is exactly the layer that should enforce them) → `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` (fraudScore 50) if punching isn't allowed for this employee. Then: device must exist, belong to the same employee, and be `APPROVED` (not missing/wrong-employee/`BLOCKED`/`DEACTIVATED`/`PENDING_APPROVAL`) → `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` (fraudScore 80, reusing Unit 7's overloaded enum value, distinguished via `fraudFlags`) if not, short-circuits. If the device is approved: work location (if given) must be active → `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` (fraudScore 40) if not; missing lat/lng → `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` (fraudScore 20); geofence distance computed via Haversine and stored into `distanceFromLocationMeters` whenever both lat/lng and an active work location are present, `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_LOCATION` (fraudScore 70) if beyond `geofenceRadiusMeters`; `gpsAccuracy > 50m` → `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` (fraudScore 30, conservative choice per the task's own hedge, not `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`); any `CLIENT_REPORTED_SIGNALS` flag with a truthy mock-location/root/jailbreak-shaped key → `REJECTED_FAKE_GPS` (fraudScore 90). Multiple conditions can all fire at once (all accumulate into `fraudScore`/`fraudFlags`); the single most severe candidate status wins via a fixed severity ranking (`REJECTED_FAKE_GPS` > `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_LOCATION` > `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` > `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` > `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`). If nothing fires at all → `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` (never plain `ACCEPTED`), `isOfficial` stays `false`.
  - **Device-import rules**: device must exist and be active → `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` (fraudScore 80) if not, short-circuits (no GPS checks of any kind for this path); device's work location must be active → `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` (fraudScore 40) if not; otherwise `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`.
  - **Manual rules**: `rawPayload.reason` must be a non-empty string (defensive re-check; Unit 7's intake DTO already requires it, so this is effectively unreachable in practice) → `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` (fraudScore 50) if somehow missing. Otherwise **always** `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` — chosen deliberately over `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` (the task explicitly left this as a choice) since a manually-typed correction is exactly the kind of exception that should route to a manager's judgment call, not just a generic HR queue; manual punches are never auto-accepted in this unit, per the task's explicit instruction.
  - **`isOfficial` remains `false` for every punch this engine touches, with no exception** — confirmed by manual testing across every rule path (in-geofence accept, out-of-geofence reject, unapproved-device reject, device-import accept, manual pending, duplicate reject). Deciding which specific IN/OUT punch becomes the "official" pair for a working day is explicitly deferred to Unit 9's daily summary calculator, per `architecture.md`'s layering.
  - **`fraudScore`/`fraudFlags` are fully recomputed from scratch on every validation run, not accumulated across repeated runs** — re-validating an unchanged punch twice produces the identical result (idempotent), rather than a score that grows every time someone re-runs validation. Engine-authored `fraudFlags` entries are tagged `{source: "VALIDATION_ENGINE", ...}` and are replaced wholesale on each run; intake-time flags from Unit 7 (e.g. `CLIENT_REPORTED_SIGNALS`, `DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED` at intake) are preserved untouched alongside them, so the full history of both intake-time and validation-time signals stays visible on the row.
  - **Two new endpoints** on the existing `AttendancePunchesController`: `POST /attendance-punches/:id/validate` (single punch, returns the updated row, audits `ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_VALIDATED` with before/after snapshots) and `POST /attendance-punches/validate-pending` (batch; optional `employeeId`/`from`/`to`/`limit` — max 100, default 20 — filters to only currently `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`/`PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` rows; returns `{totalProcessed, accepted, rejected, pending}`; audits **one** `ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_BATCH_VALIDATED` summary entry per call, not one per processed punch — verified via `psql` that a 1-punch batch call produced exactly one batch-summary audit row, not a duplicate of the per-item action). Both require the new `attendancePunches.validate` permission.
  - **1 new permission** (`attendancePunches.validate`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, granted to `HR_ADMIN` and `HR_MANAGER` only, per the task's suggested table (`PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER`/`EMPLOYEE`/`SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` all excluded). Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent) to upsert it onto existing roles.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/`db:generate`/`@recafco/db typecheck`/`@recafco/api typecheck`/`@recafco/api build`/root `pnpm typecheck` suite passed (two `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` issues hit and fixed the same way as prior units — an explicitly-typed intermediate result object instead of assigning `undefined` to an optional property, and casting a Prisma `JsonValue` array to `unknown[]` before a type-predicate `.filter()` to avoid a confusing `JsonArray`-vs-element type inference error). Built one full set of test master data (department/designation/geofenced work location/employee/attendance-device), one temporary `EMPLOYEE`-role test user, and two mobile devices (one approved, one left pending); exercised every rule path as the super admin: an in-geofence mobile punch validated to `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` with `distanceFromLocationMeters:0` and `isOfficial:false`; an out-of-geofence mobile punch (~13.2km away) validated to `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_LOCATION` with the correct Haversine distance and `fraudScore:70`; a punch from the still-`PENDING_APPROVAL` second device validated to `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` with `fraudScore:80` (had to backdate its `punchTimeSource` by an hour to isolate this case from the duplicate-detection rule, which correctly fired first on an initial attempt made close in time to an earlier same-type punch — confirming duplicate detection's precedence works as designed, not a bug); a device-import punch validated to `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` with no GPS involved; a manual punch moved from `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` (intake default) to `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` (validation outcome), never auto-accepted; a genuine duplicate (same employee/punchType/sourceType, ~90 seconds after the original) validated to `REJECTED_DUPLICATE` with `duplicateOfPunchId` correctly pointing at the original row; `validate-pending` with an `employeeId` filter and `limit:10` correctly picked up only the still-pending manual punch and reprocessed it idempotently; confirmed anonymous requests are `401` and the low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user is `403` on both the single and batch validate endpoints; confirmed via `psql` that exactly 7 `ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_VALIDATED` rows and exactly 1 `ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_BATCH_VALIDATED` row exist, in the correct order. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_raw_punches`/`mobile_devices`/`attendance_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script used to link the test user (no admin user-creation endpoint exists yet, same gap noted since Unit 6) was deleted, not left in the repo. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Get-CimInstance`/`Stop-Process`-on-specific-PIDs pattern as prior units, filtered by command line to avoid touching the unrelated `recafco-maintenance-management-system` Next.js dev processes running on this machine) and confirmed port 4000 was released.

- UNIT 9 — Daily Attendance Summary Calculator completed:
  - **Schema: no migration needed.** `AttendanceDailySummary` already had every field this unit required (`officialInPunchId`/`officialOutPunchId`/`officialInTime`/`officialOutTime`/`totalWorkedMinutes`/`lateMinutes`/`earlyLeaveMinutes`/`overtimeMinutes`/`status`/`calculationVersion`/`calculatedAt`/`lockedAt`) plus a pre-existing `@@unique([employeeId, workDate])` constraint, all the way back to Unit 1 — confirmed by reading the schema before writing any code, same "no speculative migration" discipline as Unit 7. Second unit (after Unit 7) with zero schema changes.
  - **New `AttendanceSummariesModule`** (`apps/api/src/attendance-summaries/`), split the same way Units 7/8 split intake vs. validation: `AttendanceSummariesService` (read-only: `findAll`/`findOne`/`findMine`) and `AttendanceSummaryCalculatorService` (the actual derivation/write logic: `calculateForEmployeeDate`/`recalculateRange` plus private `getEligiblePunches`/`chooseOfficialInOut`/`findActiveShift`/`calculateMinutes`/`determineStatus`/`updateOfficialFlags`/`upsertDailySummary`).
  - **Timezone handling**: added `apps/api/src/attendance-summaries/kuwait-date.util.ts` implementing Asia/Kuwait's calendar-day math as a fixed UTC+3 offset (no IANA tz database dependency) — Kuwait has observed no DST since 1968, so a constant offset is fully correct, not a shortcut. `toKuwaitWorkDate(utcInstant)` derives the calendar-day `workDate` from a punch's `punchTimeSource`; `kuwaitWorkDateRange(workDate)` returns the `[start, end)` UTC instant window for querying punches on that day; `parseWorkDateInput` turns a plain `"YYYY-MM-DD"` request field into the same canonical UTC-midnight `workDate` representation used everywhere else, ignoring any stray time-of-day component a caller might send.
  - **Eligible-punch rule**: a raw punch is usable for official selection only if `validationStatus` is `ACCEPTED` or `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` — every `PENDING_*`/`REJECTED_*` value (including `REJECTED_DUPLICATE`) is excluded by construction, which alone satisfies "not rejected"/"not duplicate"/"not manual pending" without needing extra special-case checks: manual punches always land in `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` per Unit 8 and are therefore never eligible in this unit (documented as an open question — this is correct only until Unit 10's correction-approval workflow gives manual entries a path to become eligible).
  - **Official IN/OUT selection**: earliest eligible `IN` by `punchTimeSource` (not `punchTimeServer` — the real-world event time, not server-receipt time, is what "earliest/latest" should reflect) and latest eligible `OUT`, computed independently per employee/workDate, fully agnostic to source — verified all four mixed-source combinations manually (mobile IN + device OUT, device IN + mobile OUT, plus extra same-type punches at other times that correctly stayed non-official).
  - **`updateOfficialFlags`** demotes any previously-`isOfficial:true` punch for that employee/day that isn't this run's chosen IN/OUT (so recalculation correctly un-flags a punch that's no longer the best candidate), then sets the two newly-chosen ones to `true` — scoped only to punches already known to be eligible/official, never touches rejected/pending rows.
  - **Shift-aware late/early/overtime**: looks up the employee's active `ShiftAssignment` for the workDate (overlap check against `effectiveFrom`/`effectiveTo` using the full Kuwait day window, not just an exact date match, since no shift-assignment admin API exists yet to guarantee clean midnight-aligned values) and, if found, computes `lateMinutes`/`earlyLeaveMinutes`/`overtimeMinutes` from the shift's `startTimeMinutes`/`endTimeMinutes` (handling `isOvernight` by rolling the end time past midnight). If no shift is assigned, all three are `0`, exactly as scoped. `totalWorkedMinutes` is clamped to a minimum of `0` (defends against a data anomaly where a same-day OUT is chronologically before the selected IN).
  - **Status logic**: `PRESENT`/`LATE` only when both official IN and OUT exist (`LATE` iff `lateMinutes > 0`), `MISSING_PUNCH` when exactly one side exists, `ABSENT` only when neither exists **and** the day was explicitly requested (a single `POST /calculate` call, or a day that already had raw punch activity picked up by `POST /recalculate-range`) — no bulk "mark everyone absent" behavior exists anywhere in this unit, per the explicit exclusion. No `HALF_DAY`/`LEAVE`/`WEEK_OFF`/`HOLIDAY` logic was implemented, per the explicit exclusion.
  - **`recalculate-range`'s candidate-day discovery** queries raw punches (any `validationStatus`, minimal `employeeId`/`punchTimeSource` projection) within the requested date range, groups them in application code by `employeeId` + computed Kuwait `workDate`, sorts, and caps at `limit` (default 20, max 100) — this only ever touches employee/days that already have at least one raw punch, never manufacturing new absent-employee/day pairs, per the explicit "existing punch days only" instruction. Each candidate is then run through the exact same `calculateForEmployeeDate` used by the single-item endpoint (not a separate bulk code path), so a day with only rejected punches legitimately resolves to `ABSENT`/`MISSING_PUNCH` — that's a genuine result for a day with real (if all-rejected) punch activity, not the forbidden bulk-absent case.
  - **Locked-summary protection**: `calculateForEmployeeDate` checks `existing.lockedAt` before doing any work and throws a `409 Conflict` with a clear message if set — no recalculation, no official-flag changes, no audit write. `recalculateRange` catches exactly this `ConflictException` per-candidate and counts it under `skippedLocked` rather than aborting the whole batch or silently overwriting the locked day — verified manually: a locked day was correctly rejected via the single endpoint and correctly skipped (not recalculated, not errored) inside a batch call that also covered two other, unlocked days.
  - **4 new permissions** (`attendanceSummaries.read`/`readOwn`/`calculate`/`recalculate`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, following the task's suggested table: `HR_ADMIN` gets all 4, `HR_MANAGER` gets read/calculate/recalculate (no `readOwn`, per the task's literal list), `PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get read-only, `EMPLOYEE` gets `readOwn` only, `SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` get none. Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent) to upsert the new permissions onto existing roles. **Note**: this creates a fourth, differently-named permission set covering ground already touched by Unit 3's pre-existing `attendance.read` — the same naming-duplication pattern flagged since Unit 7, not reconciled here either (task didn't ask for it).
  - **`GET /attendance-summaries/me` requires `attendanceSummaries.readOwn` explicitly** (not JWT-only like Units 6/7's `/me` endpoints) — a deliberate difference from those precedents, because this unit's own task text explicitly asked for a dedicated own-read permission on this endpoint rather than treating it as inherently scoped-by-JWT.
  - **Audit granularity is per employee/day, not per HTTP call** — a deliberate departure from Unit 8's "one summary audit row per batch endpoint call" design. Reasoning: Unit 8's two audit actions (`_VALIDATED`/`_BATCH_VALIDATED`) both represented the *same* operation, differing only by which endpoint triggered it; here, `ATTENDANCE_SUMMARY_CALCULATED` vs. `_RECALCULATED` are a genuine semantic distinction (first-time creation vs. an update to an existing payroll-relevant record) that exists per employee/day regardless of which endpoint triggered it. Every call to `calculateForEmployeeDate` — whether from `POST /calculate` directly or from inside `POST /recalculate-range`'s loop — writes its own audit row with a real before/after snapshot, since a daily attendance summary is itself a payroll input and `code-standards.md` requires auditing every payroll-critical mutation individually, not as an opaque batch count. Verified via `psql`: a 2-employee-day recalculate-range call produced exactly 2 individual `ATTENDANCE_SUMMARY_RECALCULATED` rows (correctly excluding the locked third day), not one combined batch entry.
  - **Extracted `dateRangeFilter` from `attendance-punches.service.ts` to `apps/api/src/common/date-range.util.ts`** — it was already being reused by Unit 8's `AttendanceValidationService` within the same module, and this unit needed the identical generic from/to-date-range-to-Prisma-filter logic in a third, unrelated module (`AttendanceSummariesService`'s `workDate` filtering), crossing the "reused by 2+ modules" threshold the project has used consistently to decide when a helper belongs in `common/`. `attendance-punches.service.ts` and `attendance-validation.service.ts` both now import it from `common/` instead of one re-exporting it to the other.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/`db:generate`/`@recafco/db typecheck`/`@recafco/api typecheck`(one `TS18048` possibly-undefined fix in `kuwait-date.util.ts`, root-caused not worked around)/`@recafco/api build`/root `pnpm typecheck` suite passed. Built one set of test master data (department/designation/geofenced work location/employee/attendance-device), one temporary `EMPLOYEE`-role test user, and one mobile device; exercised: mobile IN (08:00 UTC) + device-import OUT (09:00 UTC) on one day → `officialInPunchId`/`officialOutPunchId` correctly cross-source, both raw punches flipped to `isOfficial:true`, `totalWorkedMinutes:60`, `status:PRESENT`; reverse mixed source (device IN + mobile OUT) on a second day, with an extra later IN and an extra earlier OUT also present → earliest IN and latest OUT correctly chosen as official, both extras correctly stayed `isOfficial:false`; a third day with only an IN punch → `MISSING_PUNCH`, `totalWorkedMinutes:0`; `recalculate-range` across all 3 days → `totalCandidateDays:3, calculated:3`, confirmed `calculationVersion` incremented on the re-run; inserted a real `Shift`(08:00–16:00 Kuwait)/`ShiftAssignment` via `psql` (no shift-assignment admin API exists yet) and recalculated the first day → `lateMinutes:180`, `earlyLeaveMinutes:240`, `overtimeMinutes:0`, `status:LATE`, confirming the shift-based math end-to-end, not just by code review; manually set `lockedAt` on one summary → `POST /calculate` correctly returned `409` with a clear message, and a subsequent `recalculate-range` call correctly reported `skippedLocked:1` while still recalculating the other 2 unlocked days; `GET /attendance-summaries` (admin, `employeeId` filter) and `GET /attendance-summaries/me` (employee) both returned exactly the 3 expected rows, correctly scoped; confirmed anonymous → `401` and the low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user → `403` on both `calculate` and the admin list endpoint; confirmed via `psql` exactly 9 audit rows (3 `_CALCULATED` + 6 `_RECALCULATED`) in the correct order, with the locked day correctly absent from the final recalculate-range's audit rows. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — the locked summary was unlocked first (so it could be deleted like any other test row, not because unlocking is a real feature of this unit), then `attendance_daily_summaries`/`attendance_raw_punches`/`shift_assignments`/`shifts`/`mobile_devices`/`attendance_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` were all confirmed back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Get-CimInstance`/`Stop-Process`-on-specific-PIDs pattern as prior units) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 10 — Attendance Correction Workflow completed:
  - **Schema**: added `CorrectionType` enum (`MISSED_IN`/`MISSED_OUT`/`WRONG_IN`/`WRONG_OUT`/`MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT`/`OTHER`) and, to `AttendanceCorrection`: `correctionType` (required), `requestedPunchType`/`requestedPunchTime` (nullable), `targetPunchId` (nullable, a real `AttendanceRawPunch` relation named `CorrectionTargetPunch`, not a loose string — matches the project's established pattern of modeling nullable target-row references as real relations, e.g. `duplicateOfPunchId`), `approvedAt`/`rejectedAt` (nullable). Migration `20260714090752_attendance_correction_workflow` applied cleanly via plain `prisma migrate dev` on the first attempt (confirmed `attendance_corrections` was empty beforehand, same as Unit 3 verified). **Deliberately did not add a `rejectedByUserId` field** — the task's own wording ("Use rejectedBy if field exists; if not, store in snapshot/audit") was read as explicit permission to rely on the existing audit log's `actorUserId` for "who rejected" rather than adding a field that would just duplicate what `ATTENDANCE_CORRECTION_REJECTED`'s audit row already captures.
  - **New `AttendanceCorrectionsModule`** (`apps/api/src/attendance-corrections/`), following the same read-service/write-service split precedent as Units 7–9: `AttendanceCorrectionsService` handles both reads (`findAll`/`findOne`/`findMine`) and the full request/approve/reject workflow (no separate calculator service was needed here — the actual official-selection recalculation is delegated entirely to Unit 9's existing `AttendanceSummaryCalculatorService`, imported cross-module by adding it to `AttendanceSummariesModule`'s `exports` array).
  - **Two request paths funnel through one shared private `createCorrection` core**: `requestForSelf(dto, userId, context)` resolves `employeeId` via `resolveEmployeeIdForUser` (the employee endpoint's `RequestCorrectionDto` has **no** `employeeId` field at all, so a client-supplied one is rejected by the global `ValidationPipe`'s `forbidNonWhitelisted` before the handler even runs — structurally impossible, not just policy); `requestForEmployee(dto, actorUserId, context)` (admin-request) takes `employeeId` directly from `AdminRequestCorrectionDto extends RequestCorrectionDto`. Both create a `PENDING` correction with a `beforeSnapshot` (existing summary + all raw punches for that employee/workDate) and an `afterSnapshot` (the requested change) — no manual punch and no summary recalculation happen at request time, exactly as scoped.
  - **`requestedPunchType` is always server-derived, never trusted from the client, for `MISSED_IN`/`MISSED_OUT`/`WRONG_IN`/`WRONG_OUT`** (`IN`/`OUT`/`IN`/`OUT` respectively via a fixed lookup) — the client is only asked for it (and only validated as required) for `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT`, where it's genuinely ambiguous. This closes off an entire class of "client says `WRONG_IN` but sends `requestedPunchType: OUT`" inconsistency. For `OTHER`, `requestedPunchType`/`requestedPunchTime` are always forced to `null` server-side regardless of what a client sends, since `OTHER` is a free-form category with no structured "requested change" to ever turn into a punch.
  - **Business validation implemented**: employee must exist and be `ACTIVE` (both request paths); `requestedPunchTime`'s Kuwait calendar day (via Unit 9's `toKuwaitWorkDate`) must match `workDate`, or a clean `400`; `targetPunchId`, if provided, must reference an existing punch belonging to the *same* employee and the *same* Kuwait workDate, or a clean `400`; a correction can only be approved or rejected while `status === PENDING` (enforced with a `409 Conflict`, covering "approved twice," "rejected after approval," "approved after rejection," and "rejected twice" — all four transitions verified manually).
  - **Approval behavior**: creates a manual `AttendanceRawPunch` (`sourceType: MANUAL_CORRECTION`, `punchType`/`punchTimeSource` from the correction, `validationStatus: ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`, `isOfficial: false` initially, `rawPayload` carrying `correctionId`/`reason`/`approvedByUserId`/`targetPunchId`) only when `correctionType !== OTHER` and both `requestedPunchType`/`requestedPunchTime` are set — verified `OTHER`-type corrections approve cleanly with `manualPunchId: null` and no new punch row. The manual-punch-create + correction-status-update happen inside one `$transaction`; **the subsequent summary recalculation is a separate, sequential call into Unit 9's existing `calculateForEmployeeDate`**, not folded into the same atomic transaction (see Architecture Decisions below for why). This is exactly what lets the new manual punch become an official IN/OUT through the normal earliest-IN/latest-OUT selection rules — verified end-to-end: a `MISSED_OUT` correction's approval produced a manual `OUT` punch that summary recalculation then selected as `officialOutPunchId`, flipping the day from `MISSING_PUNCH` to `PRESENT` with the correct `totalWorkedMinutes`.
  - **Locked-summary behavior (explicitly decided per the task's own "safer" option)**: a correction **request** is always allowed to be created even if the target day's summary is already locked (so HR still has a record that a correction was asked for, e.g. for a post-lock dispute) — but **approval** checks `lockedAt` before any mutation and throws a `409` with zero writes (no punch created, no status change, no audit row) if locked. Verified manually: created a correction, locked the day's summary directly via `psql`, attempted approval → clean `409`, correction stayed `PENDING`, raw-punch count unchanged; unlocked and re-approved successfully afterward.
  - **5 new permissions** (`attendanceCorrections.read`/`readOwn`/`request`/`approve`/`reject`) added to `rbac.constants.ts` and `architecture.md`'s permission list, following the task's suggested table: `HR_ADMIN` gets all 5, `HR_MANAGER` gets read/approve/reject, `PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get read-only, `EMPLOYEE` gets request/readOwn, `SUPERVISOR` gets read/approve/reject (`project-overview.md`'s own Manager/Supervisor role description explicitly says "Approves or rejects attendance correction requests" — this is exactly the "if context says supervisor approves corrections" condition the task hedged on), `DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` gets none (no equivalent explicit instruction found). Re-ran `create:super-admin` (idempotent).
  - **A real permission-escalation bug was found and fixed during manual verification, not just during code review**: `POST /attendance-corrections/admin-request` was initially gated on `attendanceCorrections.request` (per the task's first-offered option), but since `EMPLOYEE` legitimately holds that same permission for the self-service `/request` endpoint, a plain employee could call `/admin-request` and supply *any other employee's* `employeeId` in the body — confirmed live with a `201` before the fix. Changed the guard to `attendanceCorrections.approve` (the task's own explicitly-offered "or a stronger permission if preferred" alternative) — re-verified live: the same employee-role call now returns `403`, while a `SUPER_ADMIN`/`HR_ADMIN`/`HR_MANAGER`/`SUPERVISOR` call (all of whom hold `.approve`) still succeeds. This is a deliberate security fix made during this unit, not deferred to an open question.
