  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database: full install/`db:generate`/`@recafco/db typecheck`/`@recafco/api typecheck` (one fix — TS narrowed `CorrectionType[]` array literals to a literal-union type that broke `.includes()`, fixed with an explicit type annotation, not a cast)/`@recafco/api build`/root `pnpm typecheck` suite passed, re-verified again after the admin-request permission fix. Built one set of test master data (department/designation/geofenced work location/employee), one temporary `EMPLOYEE`-role test user, and one mobile device; as the employee: an `IN`-only day (summary `MISSING_PUNCH`) got a `MISSED_OUT` correction request → admin approved it → manual `OUT` punch created (`MANUAL_CORRECTION`, `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`, `isOfficial:true` after recalculation) → summary correctly became `PRESENT` with `totalWorkedMinutes:240`; a `WRONG_IN` correction targeting the original `IN` punch → admin rejected it with a reason → confirmed `REJECTED` status, `rejectedReason`/`rejectedAt` set, and zero new raw punches created; confirmed approving-twice/rejecting-twice/approving-after-reject/rejecting-after-approve all cleanly `409`; confirmed the locked-summary approval rejection (`409`, zero writes, verified via unchanged punch count and unchanged `PENDING` status); confirmed an `OTHER`-type correction approves with no manual punch created; confirmed `GET /attendance-corrections/me` returns only the employee's own 3 corrections and the admin `GET /attendance-corrections?employeeId=...` returns the same set; confirmed anonymous → `401` and the low-privilege employee → `403` on admin list/approve/reject and (after the fix) on `admin-request`; confirmed via `psql` the full expected audit trail (`_REQUESTED` ×5, `_MANUAL_PUNCH_CREATED` ×1, `_APPROVED` ×2, `_REJECTED` ×1) plus the summary-recalculation audit entry firing correctly as a side effect of correction approval. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_corrections`/`attendance_daily_summaries`/`attendance_raw_punches`/`mobile_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo. Cleanly stopped the verification server (same `Get-CimInstance`/`Stop-Process`-on-specific-PIDs pattern as prior units) and confirmed port 4000 was released; did not touch any unrelated Node process.

- UNIT 11 — Next.js Web Frontend Foundation completed:
  - **New `apps/web` package (`@recafco/web`)**: Next.js 16.2.10 (App Router, Turbopack), React 19.2.7, TypeScript (extends the shared `tsconfig.base.json`), Tailwind CSS v4 (via `@tailwindcss/postcss`, CSS-first config — no `tailwind.config.js` needed), `@tanstack/react-query` 5, `react-hook-form` 7 + `@hookform/resolvers` + `zod` 4.1.13 (same pinned version already used by `packages/shared`), `lucide-react`, and `class-variance-authority`/`clsx`/`tailwind-merge` for the hand-authored shadcn-style UI primitives. All dependency versions were resolved live via `pnpm add` (not hand-typed), then pinned into the lockfile — consistent with how every prior unit avoided guessing version numbers.
  - **`sharp` and `unrs-resolver` build scripts approved** in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `allowBuilds`/`onlyBuiltDependencies` (same mechanism already used for `prisma`/`argon2`) — both are legitimate, well-known transitive dependencies of Next.js/ESLint tooling, not new supply-chain risk.
  - **ESLint pinned to `^9`** (not left floating to the newly-released `10.x`) after `pnpm peers check` showed `eslint-config-next`'s underlying plugins (`eslint-plugin-import`/`-jsx-a11y`/`-react`) don't yet support ESLint 10 — a real, verified compatibility gap, not a hypothetical one.
  - **Hand-authored shadcn-style UI primitives** (`components/ui/button.tsx`, `input.tsx`, `label.tsx`, `card.tsx`) rather than running the `shadcn` CLI — shadcn/ui is explicitly designed as "code to copy into your project," and this foundation unit only needs 4 non-interactive primitives (no Radix-dependent dropdown/modal/popover is used anywhere yet), so hand-writing them in the exact same `cva`+`cn()` convention avoids both a network-dependent interactive CLI step and unnecessary Radix dependencies for components not yet needed.
  - **Design tokens wired to `context/ui-context.md` literally**: `app/globals.css` defines the exact CSS custom properties named in `ui-context.md`'s color table (`--bg-base`, `--bg-surface`, `--text-primary`, `--accent-primary`, etc.) as the single source of truth, then a Tailwind v4 `@theme inline` block maps them to clean utility-generating names (`--color-background`, `--color-foreground`, `--color-accent`, `--color-border`, etc., following the shadcn `background`/`foreground` naming convention) — so every component uses `bg-surface`/`text-foreground`/`border-border`-style utilities, never a hardcoded hex value, per `ui-context.md`'s explicit rule.
  - **Auth session (`lib/auth/`)**: `token-storage.ts` (thin localStorage read/write/clear wrapper — the only file that knows the storage mechanism, isolating the documented future migration to httpOnly cookies to one file), `permissions.ts` (`hasPermission`/`hasAnyPermission`), `auth-context.tsx` (a plain React Context + `useState`/`useEffect` — deliberately *not* a state-management library like zustand/jotai, since the target stack list didn't name one and React Context is sufficient for this foundation's scope). On mount, if a stored access token exists, calls `GET /auth/me`; on any failure (expired/invalid/network) it clears the stored tokens and lets `ProtectedLayout` redirect to `/login` — exactly the literal behavior the task specified, not a more elaborate silent-refresh-then-retry flow (flagged as a natural, deferred follow-up in Open Questions).
  - **API client (`lib/api/client.ts`)**: a single `request<T>()` wrapper (sets `Content-Type`/`Authorization`, parses NestJS's `{message, error, statusCode}` error shape into a typed `ApiError`, no console logging of tokens/bodies anywhere) backing four typed functions: `login`, `refreshTokens`, `logout`, `getCurrentUser`. Response types (`TokenPair`, `CurrentUserProfile`) were written by reading the actual `AuthController`/`AuthService`/`auth.types.ts` source first, not guessed — confirmed field-for-field via live `curl` against the real backend (see verification below).
  - **Permission-aware navigation (`lib/routes.ts` + `components/layout/app-sidebar.tsx`)**: a single `NAV_ITEMS` array pairs each nav entry with the exact backend permission code that gates it (`employees.read`, `departments.read`, ..., `attendanceCorrections.read`, `audit.read` — cross-checked one-by-one against the current `rbac.constants.ts`), filtered client-side via `hasPermission(user, item.permission)`. Dashboard has no `permission` field (visible to any authenticated session). All 10 non-dashboard nav targets are `ComingSoonPage` placeholders (a single shared component parameterized by title/description/icon) — no CRUD tables, per the explicit "do not build full CRUD screens yet" instruction.
  - **Route protection**: `components/layout/protected-layout.tsx` is a client component that redirects to `/login` once loading settles and no session exists (used by the `(app)` route group's layout, wrapping the sidebar+header+`{children}` shell); `/login` itself redirects the *other* direction (to `/dashboard`) if a session already exists; the root `/` page redirects to whichever of the two is appropriate once the initial auth check resolves. All client-side, exactly as scoped — the task explicitly acknowledges this is acceptable for a localStorage-token foundation and that a future unit may move to server-side/cookie-based protection.
  - **Deliberately did not import `@recafco/shared`** for enums/permission constants — same reasoning maintained since Unit 2/3 for the backend: the package still ships raw `.ts` source with `"type": "module"`, and while Next.js *can* consume it via `transpilePackages`, permission codes are already dynamic runtime strings returned by `/auth/me` (not a fixed enum the frontend needs to import), so there was no genuine need to touch the still-unresolved packaging issue in this unit either.
  - **Root scripts**: added `dev:web` (`pnpm --filter @recafco/web dev`) to root `package.json`. Did **not** need to touch `turbo.json` — `apps/web`'s own `build`/`typecheck`/`lint` scripts are automatically picked up by Turbo's existing `build`/`typecheck`/`lint` task definitions across all workspace packages, confirmed live (`pnpm build`/`pnpm typecheck` both show `@recafco/web` in "Packages in scope" and complete successfully alongside `@recafco/db`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/shared`).
  - **A real ESLint flat-config bug was found and fixed, not worked around**: the initial `eslint.config.mjs` used the legacy `FlatCompat` bridge (`compat.extends('next/core-web-vitals', 'next/typescript')`) — the pattern still shown in most Next.js docs/tutorials — but this crashed with `TypeError: Converting circular structure to JSON` at lint time. Root-caused: `eslint-config-next@16.2.10` now ships its configs as *already-flat* arrays (`eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals`, `eslint-config-next/typescript`), and routing an already-flat config back through the legacy-config JSON-schema validator is what triggered the circular-reference crash. Fixed by importing the flat configs directly (`import nextCoreWebVitals from 'eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals'`, spread into the exported array) and removing the now-unneeded `@eslint/eslintrc` dependency entirely, rather than pinning to an older `eslint-config-next` version to keep the legacy pattern working.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`, `build`, and `lint` all pass cleanly (zero errors, zero warnings after also fixing a minor `import/no-anonymous-default-export` lint warning in `postcss.config.mjs`); root `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm build` both correctly include `@recafco/web` alongside the three existing packages. Started the real backend (`/health`/`/health/db` both `200`) and, since port 3000 was already occupied by the unrelated pre-existing `recafco-maintenance-management-system` Next.js dev server on this machine (confirmed via `Get-NetTCPConnection`/process inspection before touching anything), ran the web app's dev server on port 3001 *for this verification session only* — the package's actual configured `dev` script still targets port 3000 as specified. Confirmed via `curl`: `/`, `/login`, and `/dashboard` all return `200` with no server-side crash; `/login`'s and `/dashboard`'s initial server-rendered HTML correctly shows nothing/a loading state (not the real form or dashboard content) since both are client components gated on the auth check completing — this is expected, correct behavior for a client-rendered auth guard, not a bug. Verified CORS preflight from `Origin: http://localhost:3000` succeeds against the real backend. **Independently re-verified the exact backend contract the frontend's `lib/api/client.ts` depends on**, live, end-to-end: login → tokens; `/auth/me` with the access token → correct profile/roles/56 permissions; `/auth/refresh` → new token pair, old refresh token subsequently rejected `401` (rotation still works); `/auth/logout` → `{status:"ok"}`; anonymous `/auth/me` → `401`; garbage/invalid access token → clean `401` (not a crash) — this is exactly the failure path `auth-context.tsx`'s `restoreSession` catches to clear the session and let `ProtectedLayout` redirect to `/login`. **Explicitly not performed: interactive browser click-through verification** (actual login submission, visual sidebar permission filtering, an actual reload-keeps-session check, watching the redirect happen) — no browser-automation tool was available in this environment to drive a real browser. What was verified instead: full backend contract correctness (above), server-render smoke tests for every route, and direct code review of the auth/redirect logic paths. This gap is called out explicitly rather than silently claimed as tested, per this project's own verification standard — recommend the user (or a future unit with browser-automation tooling) perform one real click-through pass before this foundation is built upon further. No database rows were created or need cleanup in this unit — only existing `/auth/*` endpoints were exercised against the already-bootstrapped `superadmin` account. Cleanly stopped only this session's own web (port 3001) and API (port 4000) processes afterward (confirmed both ports released) — confirmed via process listing that the unrelated `recafco-maintenance-management-system` processes (port 3000) were never touched.

- UNIT 12 — Web Admin Master Data Screens completed:
  - **API client extended** (`apps/web/lib/api/`): `client.ts` gained `PaginatedResult<T>`/`PaginationMeta` types, a `buildQueryString(params: object)` helper, and `authorizedRequest<T>()` — a thin wrapper that reads the current access token directly from `token-storage.ts` (the single source of truth from Unit 11) so every new CRUD function doesn't need the token threaded through manually. Four new resource modules — `departments.ts`, `designations.ts`, `work-locations.ts`, `employees.ts` — each exporting typed `list*`/`get*`/`create*`/`update*`/`deactivate*` (or `terminate*` for employees) functions. **Every field name and shape was confirmed by reading the actual Unit 4 controllers/DTOs/service response construction first** (not guessed from the task's prose): `Designation.title` (not "name"), `WorkLocation.geofenceRadiusMeters` (not "allowedRadiusMeters"), `Employee.firstName`/`lastName` plus a service-derived `fullName` (not a stored "fullName" field), `EmployeeStatus`'s 4 admin-updatable values excluding `TERMINATED` (matching `UpdateEmployeeDto`'s own `UPDATABLE_STATUSES` exclusion), and the exact nested `department`/`designation`/`workLocation` relation shapes returned by each list/detail endpoint.
  - **Reusable admin components** (`apps/web/components/admin/`): `DataTable` (generic, column-defs based — a hand-rolled `<table>`, not `@tanstack/react-table`, since none of these 4 screens need sorting/complex filtering yet), `PaginationControls`, `SearchInput` (350ms debounced), `FormField` (label+error wrapper), `FormModal` and `ConfirmDialog` (both lightweight custom overlays — no Radix — with Escape-key handling), `TableSkeleton`, `Spinner`, `SuccessBanner` (self-dismissing, no toast library), `ErrorAlert`, and `AccessDenied`. Plus two new low-level `components/ui/` primitives (`Select`, `Textarea`) matching the existing hand-authored shadcn-style convention from Unit 11.
  - **Four functional pages** replacing Unit 11's placeholders: `/departments`, `/designations`, `/work-locations`, `/employees`. Each uses `@tanstack/react-query`'s `useQuery` (keyed by page/search/filters, `placeholderData` keeps the previous page visible during refetch) and `useMutation` (invalidating the list query on success) directly in the page component — no extra custom data-fetching hook layer, since TanStack Query already *is* that layer and wrapping it again would be a pointless abstraction for this unit's scope.
  - **Departments/Designations pages are structurally identical** (name/title + code + description + isActive), confirming the shared-component design paid off — the only real differences are the field name (`name` vs `title`) and copy text.
  - **Work Locations page** validates latitude (−90..90), longitude (−180..180), and a positive integer geofence radius via Zod, using a `.refine()`-based `requiredNumberInRange()` helper (not bare `.min()/.max()` on `z.number()`, because an empty/NaN input from `valueAsNumber` doesn't reliably fail a plain range check — see Architecture Decisions) and a `setValueAs` transform (not `zod.preprocess`) for the optional radius field, since `z.preprocess` breaks `zodResolver`'s type inference against a plain `z.infer`-derived form type (also in Architecture Decisions). The type dropdown is a real `<select>` sourced from the 4 backend enum values (`OFFICE`/`FACTORY`/`WAREHOUSE`/`PROJECT_SITE`), also usable as a list filter.
  - **Employees page uses two separate forms (`CreateEmployeeModal`/`EditEmployeeModal`), not one shared form component** — `CreateEmployeeDto` and `UpdateEmployeeDto` are genuinely different shapes on the backend (`employeeCode`/`hireDate` are create-only and immutable thereafter; `status` is edit-only, restricted to 4 of the 5 `EmployeeStatus` values), so forcing one Zod schema/RHF instance to cover both via conditional-requiredness would have been more complex than just writing two small, clear components. Department/designation/work-location dropdowns are populated by three `isActive:true, limit:100`-filtered queries fetched once at the page level and passed down — a `RelationSelects` sub-component renders all three `<select>`s, taking already-resolved `register()` return objects as props (not the `form` instance itself) specifically to sidestep a real TypeScript limitation: a union of two different `UseFormRegister<T>` generic instances doesn't produce a callable function (`This expression is not callable` — confirmed, not assumed). No salary field, no login-user auto-creation, and no document upload were added, exactly as scoped.
  - **Permission-aware UI** on all 4 pages: `*.read` gates the entire page (`AccessDenied` shown otherwise, matching this unit's own "show access denied" instruction over a silent redirect), `*.create` gates the "New" button, `*.update` gates each row's "Edit" button, `*.disable` gates each row's "Deactivate"/"Terminate" button — and every mutation still goes through the real backend endpoint, so a stale/forged client-side permission check is never the actual security boundary (matches this unit's explicit "still rely on backend 403 for real security" instruction and `architecture.md`'s "UI permissions are only for presentation" invariant).
  - **Two real `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` issues were hit and fixed at the source**, consistent with every backend unit's established pattern rather than being patched per call-site: (1) `buildQueryString`'s parameter type had to become a bare `object` (not `Record<string, string|number|boolean|undefined>`) because passing a *named interface* with optional properties to a `Record<K,V>`-typed parameter triggers a distinct "index signature is missing" error in this TS configuration — object literals structurally satisfy `Record` types, named interfaces do not, a real and non-obvious TS quirk; (2) every `List*Params`/`Create*Input`/`Update*Input` optional field had to be typed `field?: T | undefined` (not just `field?: T`) to accept the extremely common `value || undefined` idiom used throughout the forms' submit handlers, and `FormFieldProps.error` needed the same treatment to accept `errors.field?.message` directly.
  - **A real `z.preprocess` + `zodResolver` type-inference bug was found and fixed, not routed around**: wrapping the optional `geofenceRadiusMeters` field in `z.preprocess(...)` (to convert an empty/NaN input into `undefined`) made `z.infer`'s *output* type diverge from the schema's *input* type in a way that broke `zodResolver`'s generic `Resolver<TFieldValues>` match against the `useForm<WorkLocationFormValues>()` instance — confirmed via the exact TS error, not assumed. Fixed by dropping `z.preprocess` entirely and instead using React Hook Form's own `setValueAs` option on `register()` to do the empty-string-to-undefined conversion *before* Zod ever sees the value, keeping the schema's input and output types identical.
  - **Two real newer-ESLint (`eslint-config-next`'s React Compiler rules) errors were found and fixed, not suppressed**: `SearchInput` assigned `ref.current = onChange` directly in the render body (`Cannot access refs during render` — refs must only be written in effects/handlers) and separately called `setLocalValue(value)` synchronously inside a `useEffect` to sync from the `value` prop (`Calling setState synchronously within an effect can trigger cascading renders`). Fixed by moving the ref assignment into its own dependency-less `useEffect`, and — since nothing in this unit's actual page usage ever changes the `search` state from outside `SearchInput` itself — removing the prop-to-state sync effect entirely rather than silencing the rule, seeding `useState(value)` once as the initial value instead.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`/`build`/`lint` all pass with zero errors/warnings; root `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm build` both include `@recafco/web` alongside the three existing packages. Started the real backend and, this time, port 3000 was free (the unrelated `recafco-maintenance-management-system` app was not running this session — confirmed via process listing before starting), so the web dev server ran on its actual configured default port 3000, not a fallback port. **Independently re-verified the full backend contract for all 4 resources**, live, using the exact request/response shapes the new API client functions send: created/listed/searched/updated/deactivated a department, a designation, and a work location (including confirming the backend itself rejects an out-of-range latitude with a clean `400`, the authoritative validation layer behind the form's client-side Zod check); created supporting master data then created/listed/searched/updated (status transition)/terminated an employee, confirming the nested `department`/`designation`/`workLocation` relation objects and derived `fullName` match the `Employee` TypeScript interface field-for-field; confirmed the active-only dropdown-source query (`isActive=true&limit=100`) returns the expected filtered set; confirmed anonymous access is still `401` and `/health`/`/health/db` remain unaffected. **Explicitly not performed: interactive browser click-through** — no browser-automation tool was available in this environment (checked again at the start of this unit); all 4 new routes were confirmed to render (`200`, correct SSR shell) and the full backend contract was independently verified, but actual form submission, dropdown selection, pagination clicking, and visual permission-based button hiding were not driven through a real browser. This gap is disclosed rather than glossed over, same standard as Unit 11. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`. Cleanly stopped this session's own API and web dev processes; confirmed via process listing that no unrelated Node process needed to be touched this time (the maintenance app wasn't running).

- UNIT 13 — Web Admin Device and Mobile Device Screens completed:
  - **A real, previously-undiscovered backend bug affecting 5 list endpoints was found and fixed at the root, not routed around**: `ListDepartmentsQueryDto`/`ListDesignationsQueryDto`/`ListWorkLocationsQueryDto`/`ListAttendanceDevicesQueryDto`/`ListMobileDevicesQueryDto` all used `@Type(() => Boolean)` (from `class-transformer`) to parse their `isActive`/`isPrimary` query-string filters — but `Type(() => Boolean)` calls the `Boolean()` constructor on the raw string, and `Boolean("false")` evaluates to `true` in JavaScript (any non-empty string is truthy). This meant `?isActive=false` and `?isPrimary=false` **silently returned the wrong (opposite) result on every one of these 5 endpoints since the units that built them (4, 5, 6)** — `@IsBoolean()` still validated the coerced `true` as a legitimate boolean, so no validation error ever surfaced the bug. Confirmed live via `curl` before AND after the fix (`isPrimary=false` on a device that was primary returned it anyway, pre-fix; correctly excluded it, post-fix — same reproduction for `isActive=false` on work locations). Root-caused with a new shared `apps/api/src/common/query-boolean.transform.ts` (`parseQueryBoolean`) used via `@Transform(({value}) => parseQueryBoolean(value))` in all 5 DTOs, replacing the unsafe `@Type(() => Boolean)`. This was found and fixed specifically because this unit's own task explicitly required building working `isActive`/`isPrimary` filter dropdowns — building them correctly required first discovering they'd have been silently broken.
  - **API client extended** with two new resource modules: `lib/api/attendance-devices.ts` and `lib/api/mobile-devices.ts`, following the exact Unit 12 conventions (typed `list*`/`get*`/`create*`/`update*` functions via `authorizedRequest`). Every field name confirmed against the actual Unit 5/6 controllers/DTOs/Prisma models first, not guessed — including discovering that **neither `AttendanceDevice` nor `MobileDevice` list/detail endpoints include their `workLocation`/`employee` relation objects** (no `include` in either service, unlike `EmployeesService`'s `RELATIONS_INCLUDE` from Unit 4) — both screens resolve the "show linked work location"/"view employee relation" requirements client-side instead (see below), not by inventing a backend field that doesn't exist.
  - **`/attendance-devices` screen**: list with search + 4 filters (work location, device type, connection mode, active/inactive — the last one is exactly what surfaced the boolean-filter bug above), create/edit form (deviceCode, name, deviceType, vendor, model, serialNumber, workLocationId, ipAddress, port, connectionMode, notes — `sourceType` is correctly never shown or sent, since it's server-derived from `deviceType` per Unit 5's own design), a "Test Connection" action showing the real placeholder response (`{status:"not_configured", message:"Device connection testing is not implemented yet"}`), and soft deactivate. The work-location name shown in the table is resolved via a client-side lookup map built from an **unfiltered** work-locations query (not the active-only one used for the create/edit dropdown) specifically so a device linked to a since-deactivated location still displays a real name instead of a raw ID.
  - **`/mobile-devices` screen**: list with search + 4 filters (status, platform, employee ID as a plain text filter — not a picker, see Open Questions — and primary/non-primary), status badges for all 4 `MobileDeviceStatus` values with distinct tones, a star icon for the primary-device marker, Approve (confirm dialog, no `isPrimary` override — relies on the backend's own documented default of `true` when omitted, per Unit 6's `approve()` logic), Block (new `ReasonDialog` component, required reason, server still authoritative — confirmed a reason-less block attempt returns `400`), Reset (confirm dialog), and an Edit-metadata form restricted to exactly the 5 fields `UpdateMobileDeviceDto` accepts (`deviceName`/`manufacturer`/`model`/`osVersion`/`appVersion`) — confirmed live that attempting to sneak a `status` field into that same PATCH is rejected `400` by the backend's `forbidNonWhitelisted`, exactly matching Unit 6's documented "security-status transitions must go through /approve, /block, or /reset" invariant. **No employee self-registration UI was built** — explicitly out of scope per this unit's own instruction; this remains purely HR/admin device management.
  - **Employee-relation display uses `@tanstack/react-query`'s `useQueries`**, not a bulk-fetch — one `getEmployee(id)` query per *unique* `employeeId` appearing on the current page of mobile-device results (deduplicated, cached by React Query across pages/re-renders). This is the more correct choice than the Unit 12 "fetch up to 100 for a dropdown" pattern, since a mobile-devices list could in principle span far more than 100 distinct employees across pages at a 2000-employee company — fetching only what's actually displayed scales regardless of total employee count.
  - **New shared component**: `components/admin/reason-dialog.tsx` (confirm dialog + a required reason textarea, client-side "reason required" check plus server-error passthrough) for the Block action — everything else reused Unit 12's existing `DataTable`/`PaginationControls`/`SearchInput`/`ConfirmDialog`/`FormModal`/`ErrorAlert`/`StatusBadge`/`FormField`/`SuccessBanner`/`Spinner`/`AccessDenied`/`Select`/`Input`/`Textarea` components unchanged.
  - **Two more real newer-ESLint (React Compiler) violations were found and fixed, not suppressed** — matching the same class of issues hit in Unit 12: (1) a `useCallback` dependency array containing a computed expression (`uniqueEmployeeIds.join(',')`) is rejected by the stricter lint rule requiring "simple" dependency expressions; fixed by dropping the `useCallback`/memoization entirely in favor of building a plain `Map` directly in the render body (cheap enough — at most 20 rows per page — that memoization wasn't buying anything anyway). (2) `ReasonDialog` reset its `reason`/`touched` state via a `useEffect` keyed on the `open` prop (the same anti-pattern flagged in Unit 12's `SearchInput`) — fixed the same way: removed the reset effect, and instead the calling page now passes `key={blockTarget?.id ?? 'closed'}` to `ReasonDialog`, so React's own remount-on-key-change resets the component's internal state naturally instead of syncing it through an effect.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`/`build`/`lint` all pass cleanly (two rounds of `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` fixes — `isActive`/`isPrimary` needed the same `field?: T | undefined` widening every prior unit's optional filter fields needed); root `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm build` both pass, including after the backend bugfix. **A messy multi-process situation was discovered and cleanly resolved during this unit's own verification**: port 3000 was occupied by a *leftover `@recafco/web` dev server from earlier in this same session* (not the unrelated `recafco-maintenance-management-system` app) — confirmed by process start time and command line before touching it, then stopped and a single fresh instance restarted on its real default port once the ambiguity was resolved; a `nest start --watch` hot-reload also transiently left two duplicate watcher processes after the backend fix, both stopped and replaced with one clean instance. **Independently re-verified the full backend contract for both resources live**, exactly matching what the new API client functions send: created a work location + attendance device, listed/filtered by all 4 filter dimensions, updated, tested connection (confirmed exact placeholder response text), deactivated; created a test employee + mobile device via the existing register endpoint, listed/filtered by all 4 filter dimensions (including the specific `isPrimary=false`/`isActive=false` reproduction of the bug above, both before and after the fix), approved, confirmed a reason-less block is rejected `400`, blocked with a reason, reset, edited metadata, and confirmed a `status` field in the edit PATCH is rejected `400`; confirmed anonymous access is `401` and the low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user is `403` on both resources' admin list/mutation endpoints; confirmed `/health`/`/health/db` remained unaffected throughout. **Explicitly not performed: interactive browser click-through** — no browser-automation tool was available in this environment (checked again at the start of this unit); disclosed rather than glossed over, same standard as Units 11–12. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `mobile_devices`/`attendance_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo. Cleanly stopped every one of this session's own API/web processes afterward; confirmed both ports released.

- UNIT 14 — Web Admin Attendance Operations Screens completed:

Every field name and nested-relation shape was confirmed by reading the actual Unit 7/8/9/10 controllers/DTOs/services/Prisma schema first (including each `RELATIONS_INCLUDE`), not guessed from the task's prose — unlike Unit 13's devices, all three of these resources' backend services *do* already include their relevant relations, so no client-side relation-lookup workaround was needed this time.

  - **`/attendance-summaries` screen**: list + pagination, filters (employeeId, departmentId, status, date range), detail modal showing official IN/OUT times, worked/late/early/overtime minutes, and a locked indicator (padlock icon) when `lockedAt` is set, Calculate (single employee/date) and Recalculate Range (optional employeeId — blank means all employees with punch activity in range — plus required from/to and optional limit) forms. No salary/deduction/payroll-lock UI anywhere, exactly as scoped.
  - **`/attendance-corrections` screen**: list + pagination, filters (employeeId, status, correctionType, date range), detail modal including the full `beforeSnapshot`/`afterSnapshot` JSON (rendered as scrollable `<pre>` blocks, since these are potentially large nested objects with no fixed display shape worth hand-formatting), an Admin Request Correction form whose visible fields (`requestedPunchType`, `requestedPunchTime`, `targetPunchId`) conditionally show/hide and become required based on the selected `correctionType`, exactly mirroring the backend's own `@ValidateIf` rules from `request-correction.dto.ts` (punch type only for `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT`; punch time required for everything except `OTHER`; target punch required only for `WRONG_IN`/`WRONG_OUT`) — enforced client-side via a Zod `.superRefine()`, not just left to the backend to reject. Approve (`ConfirmDialog`, success message distinguishes whether a manual punch/summary recalculation actually happened via `manualPunchId`/`summaryId` in the response) and Reject (`ReasonDialog`, required reason, `key={rejectTarget?.id ?? 'closed'}` for state reset) actions, both only shown while `status === PENDING`. No employee self-service correction UI, per the task's explicit exclusion — `requestAttendanceCorrection` exists in the API client only for future reuse.
  - **A real backend-vs-task-text permission discrepancy was resolved by matching the actual backend, not the task's literal wording**: the task described gating "Admin Request Correction" on `attendanceCorrections.request`, but Unit 10's own already-shipped security fix requires `attendanceCorrections.approve` instead (`.request` is also held by plain `EMPLOYEE`-role accounts for self-service, which is exactly the escalation Unit 10 closed). The UI's "Request Correction" button is gated on `.approve`, matching live, re-verified backend behavior — documented here rather than silently deviating from the task text without explanation.
  - **`react-hook-form`'s `watch()` was avoided in favor of local `useState`+`setValue`** for the correction-request form's conditional-field logic — an initial `requestForm.watch('correctionType')` implementation triggered a real ESLint React-Compiler warning ("Use of incompatible library... React Hook Form's `watch()` function cannot be memoized safely"), the first time `watch()` had been used anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via a repo-wide grep — no prior unit had used it). Replaced with a plain `useState<CorrectionType>` driven by the select's `onChange` (via `register(name, {onChange})`, not a second uncontrolled input), which cleared the warning entirely and is more consistent with this codebase's established preference (Units 12/13) for local state over form-library reactivity hooks in render-affecting logic.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`/`build`/`lint` all pass cleanly (zero errors, zero warnings, after the `watch()` fix above); root `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm build` both pass, including `@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared` unaffected. Started the real backend and web dev server (port 3000 was free this session — confirmed via process listing before starting). **Independently re-verified the full backend contract for all three resources live**, exactly matching what the new API client functions send: built one full set of test master data (department/designation/geofenced work location/employee/attendance-device); created a manual `IN` punch and a device-import `OUT` punch (both landed `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` as designed), listed/filtered by employeeId/validationStatus/sourceType, validated the manual punch individually (→ `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL`), ran validate-pending for the remaining punch (`{totalProcessed:2, accepted:1, rejected:0, pending:1}`, matching the `ValidatePendingResult` type exactly); calculated a summary (→ `MISSING_PUNCH`, only the OUT punch had validated by then), confirmed the `departmentId` filter and nested `employee`/`officialInPunch`/`officialOutPunch` relation shapes, ran recalculate-range (`{totalCandidateDays:1, calculated:1, skippedLocked:0, failed:0}`); admin-requested a `MISSED_IN` correction, approved it and confirmed **live** that approval created a real manual punch and recalculated the summary through the existing Unit 10 backend behavior (status flipped `MISSING_PUNCH` → `PRESENT`, `totalWorkedMinutes` became `540`, `calculationVersion` incremented) — exactly the behavior the corrections screen's success-message logic depends on; admin-requested a second `OTHER`-type correction and rejected it with a reason, confirmed omitting the reason is cleanly rejected `400`. **Since no payroll-lock API endpoint exists yet** (out of scope for this unit, confirmed by reading `AttendanceSummariesController` — only `calculate`/`recalculate-range` exist, no `lock`/`unlock` routes), the locked-summary `409` error paths were exercised by directly flipping `lockedAt` on the test summary via `psql` (simulating a state the UI must someday handle, not fabricating fake business data) — confirmed `POST /calculate` on the locked day returns a clean `409 "Cannot recalculate a locked attendance summary"`, `recalculate-range` gracefully reports `skippedLocked:1` rather than erroring, and approving a correction against the locked day returns a clean `409 "Cannot approve a correction for a locked attendance summary"` — all three error shapes match what the pages' `ErrorAlert`/`errorMessage()` helpers already display correctly (`ApiError.message` surfaces the backend's message verbatim); the simulated lock was removed immediately afterward. Confirmed anonymous access is `401` and a genuine low-privilege `EMPLOYEE`-role test user (created via the same established throwaway-`tmp-create-test-user.ts`-script pattern used since Unit 6, deleted after use) is `403` on every admin action across all three resources (manual punch creation, admin-request correction, calculate summary). Confirmed `/health`/`/health/db` remained unaffected throughout, and all three new routes (`/attendance-punches`, `/attendance-summaries`, `/attendance-corrections`) server-render `200`. **Explicitly not performed: interactive browser click-through** — no browser-automation tool was available in this environment (checked again at the start of this unit); this is now a 4-unit-deep gap (Units 11–14), disclosed rather than glossed over, same standard as every prior frontend unit. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_corrections`/`attendance_daily_summaries`/`attendance_raw_punches`/`attendance_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo. Cleanly stopped this session's own API and web dev processes afterward (confirmed via `Get-CimInstance` process identity/start-time before stopping — no unrelated Node process needed to be touched this time).

- UNIT 15 — Mobile App Foundation completed:
  - **New `apps/mobile` package (`@recafco/mobile`)**: Expo SDK 57.0.4 (React Native 0.86.0, React 19.2.3 — a different React patch than web's 19.2.7 since Expo SDK 57 pins its own compatible React version; no conflict, pnpm isolates each app's own dependency tree), TypeScript 5.9.3 extending the monorepo's `tsconfig.base.json`. All dependency versions were resolved live via `pnpm add`/`npx expo install` (which cross-checks SDK-57 compatibility per package), not hand-typed, matching the precedent set in Unit 11 for `apps/web`.
  - **Router choice: Expo Router, not React Navigation directly** — chosen and documented per the task's own explicit request for a choice with reasoning. Reason: Expo Router's file-based routing maps directly onto this unit's tiny, flat screen set (Login, Home, Profile, Device placeholder, Attendance placeholder) with near-zero manual wiring, and its `(app)` route-group convention is a structural match for the *exact* protected-layout pattern already established in `apps/web` (Unit 11's `(app)` route group + `ProtectedLayout` component) — reusing the same mental model across web and mobile was judged more valuable than React Navigation's more manual (but more explicit) `NavigationContainer`/`Stack.Navigator` setup for a 5-screen shell.
  - **Auth (`src/lib/auth/`)**: `token-storage.ts` wraps `expo-secure-store` (iOS Keychain / Android Keystore) — per the task's explicit preference and `architecture.md`'s "mobile app uses secure storage for tokens" requirement — with every function async (SecureStore has no sync API, unlike web's synchronous `localStorage` wrapper from Unit 11). No AsyncStorage fallback was needed; SecureStore installed and typechecked without any native-module friction on this Windows/Expo setup, so the task's documented fallback path was not triggered. `auth-context.tsx` mirrors `apps/web`'s `AuthProvider`/`useAuth` almost line-for-line (session restore on mount via `/auth/me`, `login`/`logout` methods, `isAuthenticated`/`isLoading` state), swapping Next's `useRouter` for `expo-router`'s (same hook name, compatible `.replace()`/`.push()` API) and awaiting every token-storage call.
  - **API client (`src/lib/api/client.ts`)**: a single file mirroring `apps/web/lib/api/client.ts`'s exact shape (`ApiError`, `TokenPair`, `CurrentUserProfile` — field-for-field matching the real `AuthService.getProfile()`/`login()`/`refresh()` return shapes, confirmed by re-reading `apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` and `auth.controller.ts` directly rather than trusting memory from Units 11–14), plus `login`/`refreshTokens`/`logout`/`getCurrentUser`, and `authorizedRequest<T>()` for future (Unit 16+) authenticated calls. **Discovered while re-verifying the contract**: the login endpoint's field is `identifier` (not `username` — re-confirmed directly against `LoginDto`), and `/auth/refresh`/`/auth/logout` both take `{ refreshToken }` (re-confirmed against `RefreshTokenDto`) — matches Units 11–14's client exactly, no drift. `authorizedRequest` catches a `401` specifically and clears the stored session (the task's explicit "handles 401 by clearing session" requirement) rather than clearing on every error class.
  - **Confirmed a real, honest scope limit in `/auth/me`'s actual response**: `AuthService.getProfile()` returns `employeeId: string | null` only — **no nested `employee` object** (no `employeeCode`/`firstName`/`lastName`), confirmed by reading the service directly, not assumed. The task conditionally asked for "employee code/name if `/auth/me` returns employee relation" — since it doesn't, the Home/Profile screens show only the raw `employeeId` (or "not linked to an employee record"). This is a real, pre-existing backend gap (not something this unit broke), left as-is per "no backend changes unless a real blocking issue is found" — not blocking, since the screen degrades cleanly without it.
  - **Device info foundation (`src/lib/device/device-info.ts`)**: `getDeviceInfo()` using `expo-device` (`deviceName`/`manufacturer`/`modelName`/`osVersion`), `expo-application` (`nativeApplicationVersion`), and `expo-constants` (version fallback) — returns a typed `DeviceInfo` object. `deviceIdentifier` is hardcoded `null` with an explicit doc comment that Unit 16 must decide the real, stable device-identifier strategy (a client-generated hardware ID is not safe/stable enough for attendance trust on its own, per `architecture.md`'s "mobile fraud checks are signals; backend validation is authoritative" invariant) — no device registration, binding, or attendance-device API calls were made, exactly as scoped.
  - **Screens**: `app/login.tsx` (RHF `Controller`-based form using RN `TextInput`, RHF's built-in `rules` for required-field validation — see the Zod decision below —, loading/error states, redirects to `/` if already authenticated), `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` (the auth gate: loading spinner while `isLoading`, `<Redirect href="/login" />` if unauthenticated, else a `Stack` with headers for the 4 protected screens — structurally identical in intent to `apps/web`'s `ProtectedLayout`), `app/(app)/index.tsx` (Home: welcome + username, employeeId or "not linked" fallback, roles list, permission count, 4 cards — Today Attendance/My Attendance route to the Attendance placeholder, Register Device routes to the Device placeholder, Corrections is non-interactive since no dedicated route was in scope), `app/(app)/profile.tsx` (full profile fields + the actual Sign Out action), `app/(app)/device.tsx` and `app/(app)/attendance.tsx` (both render a small shared `ComingSoon` component — mirroring `apps/web`'s Unit 11 `ComingSoonPage` pattern). No punch button, no GPS prompt, and no leave/payslip/document UI exist anywhere in this unit.
  - **A real cross-package dependency conflict was found and resolved by removing the dependency, not forcing a version**: wiring `react-hook-form` + `zod` + `@hookform/resolvers/zod` (as the task suggested "if useful") for the login form's validation produced a genuine `tsc` error — `@hookform/resolvers`'s zod-resolver type declarations resolved a *different* physical zod copy inside `apps/mobile`'s dependency graph than inside `apps/web`'s (a transitive `zod@3.25.76` carrying an internal v4-compatibility shim, vs. the explicitly-installed `zod@4.1.13` — reproduced and confirmed via `require.resolve` probing, not guessed), tripping zod4's own internal version-brand check (`_zod.version.minor: 1` not assignable to `0`). Rather than chase a monorepo-wide pnpm dependency-resolution fix for a two-field form, `zod`/`@hookform/resolvers` were removed from `apps/mobile` entirely and the login form uses RHF's native `rules={{ required: '...' }}` instead — simpler, and sidesteps the conflict. Documented as an open question below for when a more complex mobile form (Unit 16+) might make solving the underlying conflict worthwhile.
  - **A real, reproduced Metro/pnpm monorepo bundling bug was found and fixed at the root cause, not routed around**: following Expo's generic monorepo guide verbatim (`metro.config.js` with `watchFolders`, custom `nodeModulesPaths`, **and** `disableHierarchicalLookup: true`) caused the Metro bundler to fail resolving `expo-router`'s own transitive dependencies (`@expo/metro-runtime`, then `@expo/log-box`) once actual bundle requests were attempted — confirmed live via direct HTTP requests to the running Metro dev server, not assumed from reading docs. Root cause: `disableHierarchicalLookup: true` disables Metro's normal walk-up-the-tree resolution, which is exactly the mechanism pnpm's nested `.pnpm` virtual-store symlink structure depends on for a package to find *its own* transitive dependencies. Removing that one setting (keeping `watchFolders`/`nodeModulesPaths` as supplementary, not exclusive, lookup locations) fixed it — confirmed by re-requesting the same bundle URL after the fix and getting a real, complete, 200 OK bundle. This is a genuinely useful finding for Unit 16+ and any future Metro config changes in this repo: pnpm monorepos need Metro's hierarchical lookup left **on**.
  - **`@expo/metro-runtime` was added as an explicit dependency** (not left as an implicit transitive one) after the live bundle-request test above surfaced it as a genuinely missing module `expo-router`'s entry file requires at runtime — the same "don't rely on pnpm hoisting accidents" discipline already applied to `babel-preset-expo` (added explicitly up front, after confirming via `require.resolve` probing that it would NOT reliably resolve from `apps/mobile`'s own `node_modules` as a bare transitive dependency of `expo`/`@expo/cli`).
  - **`@babel/core` was pinned to `^7.29.7`, not left to resolve to the newly-released `^8.0.1`** — `pnpm peers check` immediately surfaced a real, concrete conflict (the entire Expo/Metro babel-plugin ecosystem still declares `@babel/core: ^7.x` peer ranges; none support the `8.x` major yet). Caught and fixed before it could cause a runtime babel-transform failure, the same "verify peer dependencies, don't just accept whatever resolves" discipline used throughout every backend unit's dependency additions.
  - **No TypeScript path alias (`@/*`) was wired up**, despite `apps/web` having one — a `"paths": {"@/*": ["./*"]}` entry was added to `tsconfig.json` initially, but removed once recognized that TypeScript path mappings are a `tsc`-only construct: Metro does not honor `tsconfig.json` `paths` without additional Babel resolver configuration (`babel-plugin-module-resolver` or equivalent), which could not be runtime-verified in this environment (no emulator/device to click through). Rather than ship an alias that type-checks but could plausibly fail to *bundle* at runtime, every mobile source file uses plain relative imports (`../../src/lib/...`) — confirmed working end-to-end via the live bundle-compile test below. Revisit once a real device/emulator is available to verify a Babel-resolver-based alias actually works before relying on it.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck` and `pnpm typecheck` (root, all 5 packages including `@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared`) pass cleanly; `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile lint` and root `pnpm lint` pass with zero errors; root `pnpm build` confirms `@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared` are all unaffected (mobile has no `build` script — not required by this unit's scope, since Expo apps ship via `expo start`/EAS build, not a local `tsc`/bundler build step). Started the Expo dev server (`npx expo start`) and, after the two real fixes above, **directly requested compiled JS bundles from the running Metro server for both `platform=android` and `platform=ios`** — both returned a complete `200 OK` bundle (~7MB/~6.3MB), and the Android bundle was grepped to confirm it actually contains this unit's own source strings (`"RECAFCO HRMS"`, `"Welcome, "`, the `recafco.accessToken` SecureStore key, `expo-router/entry`) — i.e. the entire dependency graph, every screen, the auth context, and the device-info module genuinely compile end-to-end for both target platforms, not just `tsc`-level type-correctness. **No Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** to actually install and click through the running app (login submission, session-restore-on-reload, logout, live network calls to a running backend) — disclosed per the task's own explicit fallback instruction, matching the same "browser automation unavailable" transparency standard already established for every web unit (11–14). The exact commands a developer with a real emulator/device needs are: `pnpm --filter @recafco/api dev` (backend) plus, in a second terminal, `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile start` then press `a` for an Android emulator or `i` for an iOS simulator (Mac only), or scan the QR code in Expo Go on a physical device on the same LAN after setting `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` in `apps/mobile/.env` to the PC's LAN IP (see `.env.example`'s inline documentation for the three scenarios). All test/cache artifacts from this verification (`.expo/`, `.turbo/` inside `apps/mobile`) are build-time caches, not committed data — added to `.gitignore` (see below). No database rows were created or need cleanup — this unit made no backend calls beyond the dev server startup/bundle-compile checks above, and never exercised `/auth/login` against a live backend session (no client existed to drive it). Existing `@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api` dev servers were not started or touched during this unit's verification; confirmed via `Get-CimInstance` process listing before and after that only this unit's own `expo start` processes were running, and all were cleanly stopped afterward (confirmed port 8081 released).
  - **`.gitignore` updated**: added `.expo/`, `apps/mobile/android/`, `apps/mobile/ios/`, `apps/mobile/web-build/`, and `*.orig.*` — none of these existed before this unit (no native `android/`/`ios/` folders were ever generated, since this is a managed-workflow Expo app with no `expo prebuild` run), added preemptively since Expo's dev server already created a `.expo/` cache folder during this unit's own verification.

- UNIT 16 — Mobile Device Registration Screen completed:
  - **Device identifier strategy implemented in a dedicated module** (`src/lib/device/device-identifier.ts`, kept separate from the existing synchronous `device-info.ts` per the task's own offered option, since resolving/persisting an identifier is fundamentally async and stateful while `getDeviceInfo()` stays a pure sync read): Android uses `Application.getAndroidId()` (`Settings.Secure.ANDROID_ID`, stable per app-signing-key+user+device, resets on factory reset or signing-key change); iOS uses `Application.getIosIdForVendorAsync()` (IDFV, stable across launches, changes only if every app from the vendor is uninstalled, can transiently return `null` right after a restart before first unlock); a fallback (web, or iOS returning `null`) generates a random UUID via the newly-added `expo-crypto` package and persists it in SecureStore so it survives app restarts but not reinstalls — matching how ANDROID_ID/IDFV themselves reset on their own trigger conditions, not a weaker guarantee. Every value is prefixed by source (`android:`/`ios:`/`fallback:`) per the task's "prefix with platform if useful" suggestion, both for support/debugging clarity and to structurally rule out a same-string cross-strategy collision. `device-info.ts`'s now-obsolete `deviceIdentifier: null` placeholder field (from Unit 15) was removed entirely rather than left as dead weight alongside the new real resolver.
  - **API client extended** with `src/lib/api/mobile-devices.ts`: `MobilePlatform`/`MobileDeviceStatus` types, a `MobileDevice` interface, `registerMobileDevice(input)`, and `getMyMobileDevices()` — both via the existing `authorizedRequest` helper from Unit 15. Every field name was confirmed by re-reading the actual Unit 6 `RegisterMobileDeviceDto`/`MobileDevicesController`/`MobileDevicesService` directly in this unit (not carried over from memory), including the exact business rules: `employeeId` is never sent by the client (backend resolves it from the authenticated user via `resolveEmployeeIdForUser`, confirmed by reading that helper directly), a device identifier already registered to a *different* employee returns `409 Conflict` with a specific message, the *same* employee re-registering an already-known identifier updates metadata in place without touching `status` (verified live, not assumed), and a user with no linked employee gets a `400` with a specific message from that same shared helper (also confirmed by reading it directly) rather than a generic validation error.
  - **`/device` screen fully replaces the Unit 15 placeholder**: always shows this device's info (platform, name, manufacturer, model, OS version, app version, and a masked device identifier — first 6 + `••••` + last 4 characters, never the full value) and, only when `user.employeeId` is set, a Registration Status section (loading/error/pending/approved/blocked/deactivated/not-registered states, each with the exact message text the task specified) plus a "Register Device"/"Update Registration" button (label switches based on whether a matching device was found in `GET /mobile-devices/me`) and a "Refresh Status" button, plus pull-to-refresh via `RefreshControl` on the containing `ScrollView`. When `employeeId` is `null`, the screen shows "Your user account is not linked to an employee profile. Contact HR." instead of the registration section entirely and never calls `/mobile-devices/register` or `/me` at all — not merely "avoids repeating the call," genuinely never issues it in that state, checked client-side from the already-loaded `/auth/me` profile rather than discovering the gap via a failed request.
  - **Error handling matches the task's exact required messages**: a `409` on register is mapped to "This device appears to be registered to another employee. Contact HR."; a `400` (no employee linked, reachable only via a theoretical race since the button is already hidden in that state) surfaces the backend's own message directly; a network-unreachable `ApiError` (status `0`, from Unit 15's `client.ts`) falls through to a generic "could not register/could not load" message; a `401` on either the status-load or the register call triggers `useAuth()`'s existing `logout()` (not a new session-management mechanism) — since Unit 15's `authorizedRequest` already clears the stored tokens on `401`, calling the existing `logout()` afterward is a no-op for its own best-effort server-side revoke (no refresh token remains to revoke) but still correctly flips `isAuthenticated` to `false` and navigates to `/login`, closing the real gap noted in Unit 15 where a background `401` cleared storage but never updated in-memory auth state.
  - **A real React Compiler `set-state-in-effect` lint violation was found and fixed by restructuring, not suppressing** — mirroring the exact fix already used for `auth-context.tsx`'s `restoreSession` in Unit 15: the mount-time status fetch is a function declared *inside* the `useEffect` body (not a call to the outer `loadStatus` `useCallback`, and not a direct `setState` call sitting in the effect's synchronous top level either — an initial attempt that left the `!hasEmployee` early-return's `setIsLoadingStatus(false)` directly in the effect body was itself flagged and had to move inside the nested function too). `loadStatus` remains a separate, reusable `useCallback` for the refresh button and the post-registration reload, both triggered from event handlers, which this lint rule does not flag at all.
  - **No new dependency-resolution conflicts this time** — `expo-crypto` (the one new dependency, for `Crypto.randomUUID()`) installed and typechecked cleanly via `npx expo install`, unlike Unit 15's `zod`/`@hookform/resolvers` collision; this screen needed no form-schema validation at all, since every registration field is device-derived, not user-typed (structurally satisfying the task's "do not allow manual editing of deviceIdentifier" requirement — there is no input field for it to edit).
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly (zero errors, zero warnings; `@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared` all unaffected). **The backend (`pnpm --filter @recafco/api dev`) and the mobile Expo dev server (`pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile start`) were already running when this unit began** (both started roughly one minute before this session's first action, matching the task's own verification instructions) — left running rather than restarted, since they were not started by this session's own actions and may have been left running intentionally for the user's own concurrent device/emulator testing; both were confirmed healthy (`/health`, `/health/db`, Metro `/status`) before use. **Independently re-verified the full backend contract live via curl**, exactly matching what the new API client functions send: registered a device for a real test employee (→ `PENDING_APPROVAL`, response shape matching `MobileDevice` field-for-field), confirmed `GET /mobile-devices/me` reflected it; approved it as the super admin (→ `APPROVED`, reflected on `/me`), blocked it with a reason (→ `BLOCKED`, reflected), reset it (→ `DEACTIVATED`, reflected) — the exact status sequence the screen's `STATUS_COPY` messages must handle; registered a second device for a second test employee, then confirmed the first employee attempting to register that same `deviceIdentifier` gets a clean `409` with the exact expected message; confirmed the second employee re-registering their own already-known identifier updates metadata in place (`deviceName`/`appVersion` changed, `status` unchanged) rather than erroring; confirmed a user with no linked employee gets a clean `400` with the exact expected message from `POST /register`, while `GET /me` for that same user correctly returns `[]` (not an error); confirmed anonymous `POST /register` is `401`. **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (same disclosed gap as Unit 15, now also true for Unit 16 — a 2-unit-deep mobile-specific verification gap), directly requested compiled JS bundles from the already-running Metro dev server for both `platform=android` and `platform=ios` — both returned a complete `200 OK` bundle, and the Android bundle was grepped to confirm it contains this unit's new source strings (`"Register Device"`×3, `"Update Registration"`, the exact pending/409 message text, `mobile-devices/register`, the fallback-identifier SecureStore key) — the entire new screen, API client, and identifier resolver genuinely compile end-to-end for both target platforms. The exact commands for real device/emulator testing are unchanged from Unit 15's documented ones (`pnpm --filter @recafco/api dev` plus `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile start`, then `a`/`i`/Expo Go QR scan). **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `mobile_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo. The already-running backend/Expo dev server processes (not started by this session) were deliberately left running, not stopped, unlike every prior unit's own self-started verification servers.

- UNIT 17 — Mobile Attendance Punch Screen completed:
  - **New dependency**: `expo-location` (`~57.0.2`, resolved via `npx expo install` — the same live-resolution discipline used for every prior mobile dependency). `app.json`'s `plugins` array gained a config-plugin entry with a custom `locationWhenInUsePermission` iOS message; `isIosBackgroundLocationEnabled`/`isAndroidBackgroundLocationEnabled` were deliberately left at their default `false` — this unit is explicitly foreground-only, per the task's own scope and `architecture.md`'s "GPS... device info" ownership under `apps/mobile` (background location is a distinct future capability, not silently enabled here).
  - **Location service (`src/lib/location/location-service.ts`)**: `getForegroundPermissionStatus()` (read-only, never prompts — safe to call on screen mount), `requestForegroundPermission()` (prompts; only ever called from the punch button handler, not on mount), and `getCurrentLocation()` (wraps `Location.getCurrentPositionAsync({ accuracy: Location.Accuracy.High })`, returning `{ latitude, longitude, accuracy, mockLocationDetected }` — `mockLocationDetected` reads `LocationObject.mocked` directly, a real typed field confirmed by reading `expo-location`'s own `.d.ts`, not a cast). `MAX_ACCEPTABLE_ACCURACY_METERS = 100` is exported from here as the single source of truth for the client-side pre-check threshold.
  - **API client extended** with `src/lib/api/attendance-punches.ts`: `PunchType`/`AttendanceSourceType`/`PunchValidationStatus` types, a trimmed `AttendanceRawPunch` interface (no nested relation fields, since neither `POST /mobile` nor `GET /me` include them — confirmed by reading `AttendancePunchesService.createMobile`/`findMine` directly, not assumed from the web client's richer version built in Unit 14), `createMobilePunch(input)`, and `listMyAttendancePunches(params)`. `CreateMobilePunchInput.punchType` is narrowed to `'IN' | 'OUT'` at the client boundary (this screen only ever produces those two), while the response-side `PunchType` keeps the full 4-value union matching the real backend enum, since a punch returned by `GET /me` could in principle be any type from a non-mobile source appearing in the same employee's punch history.
  - **Shared status-copy modules extracted** (used by both this unit's screen and Unit 16's, and set up for Unit 18's history screen to reuse too): `src/lib/device/device-status-copy.ts` (pulled out of Unit 16's `device.tsx`, which now imports it instead of duplicating `MOBILE_DEVICE_STATUS_COPY`/`maskDeviceIdentifier`) and `src/lib/attendance/punch-status-copy.ts` (new — `PUNCH_VALIDATION_STATUS_COPY` for all 10 `PunchValidationStatus` values, `SOURCE_TYPE_LABELS` for all 8 `AttendanceSourceType` values). Crossing the "reused by 2+ modules" threshold this project has applied consistently since Unit 4's `common/` extractions.
  - **`/attendance` screen fully replaces the Unit 15 placeholder**: Device Status card (reusing the exact same status copy/tone/messages as the Device screen, plus a "Not Registered" state with a link to `/device` via `expo-router`'s `useRouter().push()`), Location Status card (permission-not-requested / denied-with-an-"Open Settings"-button via `Linking.openSettings()` / ready-with-accuracy-and-a-qualitative-good/poor-tag — deliberately **never shows raw latitude/longitude**, per `ui-context.md`'s explicit "Employee Punch Screen... do not show raw fields" rule), two large Check In/Check Out buttons, a punch-result banner, a permanent disclaimer ("Final attendance status is determined by HR/backend validation, not by this screen"), and a Latest Punches list (last 10, punch type + formatted time + validation-status badge + source-type label) with both pull-to-refresh and an explicit "Refresh" text link. When `employeeId` is `null`, the screen shows the same "not linked" message as the Device screen and never calls any punch/device/location endpoint.
  - **Location permission is requested only when a punch is attempted, never proactively on screen open** — the task explicitly asked for one approach with documented reasoning. Chosen because: (a) opening the Attendance tab to merely check status/history shouldn't force a native permission dialog before the user has decided to act; (b) this matches Apple/Google's own recommended "contextual permission request" guidance (request access at the moment it's actually needed); (c) the screen still shows an accurate, non-intrusive permission status on load via the read-only `getForegroundPermissionStatus()` check, so nothing is hidden from the user — only the actual OS prompt is deferred.
  - **The 100-meter GPS accuracy pre-check blocks submission client-side**, per the task's explicit "safer for this unit" instruction — if `accuracy` is `null` or exceeds `MAX_ACCEPTABLE_ACCURACY_METERS`, the punch is never sent and the user sees "GPS accuracy is low. Move to an open area and try again." This is a UX courtesy only, not a security boundary — the backend's own `AttendanceValidationService` (Unit 8) independently re-evaluates geofence/accuracy server-side regardless of what the client pre-checked, so a bypassed/compromised client gains nothing by skipping this check.
  - **The backend does not reject a punch with an HTTP error just because the device is unregistered/pending/blocked/deactivated — it still stores the attempt** (re-confirmed live via curl, not assumed: all four non-approved device states return `201` with `validationStatus: REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` and a specific `fraudFlags` reason, never a `4xx`), exactly matching `CLAUDE.md`'s "every punch attempt must be stored, including rejected" principle from Unit 8's original design. This means the mobile screen's device-approval gate (disabling the Check In/Out buttons unless `currentDevice?.status === 'APPROVED'`) is a **UX safeguard that prevents a doomed submission**, not something the backend would otherwise refuse — if that client-side gate were ever bypassed, the punch would still succeed at the HTTP layer but land as a rejected, non-official record, and the screen's result banner reads the punch's *actual returned* `validationStatus` rather than assuming success from a `201`, so this is handled correctly either way.
  - **`wifiSsid`/`wifiBssid` are never sent** — reading Wi-Fi SSID/BSSID from React Native requires an additional native module (e.g. `react-native-wifi-reborn`), which the task explicitly said not to add ("Do not add heavy libraries"); both fields are optional on the backend DTO and were simply omitted, not stubbed with fake values.
  - **`workLocationId` is never sent** — resolving the employee's assigned work location isn't available from `/auth/me` (which only returns `employeeId`, not the full employee record) and the task explicitly scoped geofence-distance logic out of this unit ("Do not implement geofence distance locally yet unless workLocation data is already available"); the backend already handles a `null` `workLocationId` gracefully (skips the geofence check, per Unit 8's validation engine).
  - **No new React Compiler lint violations** — the exact nested-function-inside-`useEffect` pattern from Units 15/16 was applied proactively from the start for all three initial-load fetches (location permission status, device status, punches), so `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile lint` passed clean on the very first attempt, with no fix-and-retry cycle needed this time.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly on the first attempt (zero errors, zero warnings; `@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared` all unaffected). **The backend, web dev server, and mobile Expo dev server were all already running when this unit began** (confirmed healthy — `/health`, `/health/db`, web `200`, Metro `/status` — before use) — left running rather than restarted, consistent with the Unit 16 precedent of not touching processes this session didn't start. **Independently re-verified the full backend contract live via curl**, exactly matching what the new API client sends: a mobile punch against a genuinely unregistered `deviceIdentifier` stored as `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` with a `DEVICE_NOT_REGISTERED` fraud flag; registering the device then punching while still `PENDING_APPROVAL` stored as `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` with a `DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED` flag; approving the device then punching IN and OUT both landed `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` with the correct `mobileDeviceId` populated; `GET /attendance-punches/me` returned all four punches in the correct newest-first order with exactly the fields the mobile `AttendanceRawPunch` interface expects; blocking then resetting the device reproduced `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` with `DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED`/`BLOCKED` and `DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED`/`DEACTIVATED` flags respectively; a user with no linked employee got a clean `400` from both `GET /me` and `POST /mobile` (matching `resolveEmployeeIdForUser`'s shared error message); an anonymous `POST /mobile` was `401`. **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from Units 15–16, now 3 units deep), directly requested compiled JS bundles from the running Metro dev server for both `platform=android` (200 OK, ~7.2MB) and `platform=ios` (200 OK, ~6.5MB) — the Android bundle was grepped to confirm it contains this unit's new source strings (`"Check In"`×3, `"Check Out"`×2, the GPS-accuracy-warning text, `attendance-punches/mobile`, the backend-validation disclaimer text, the location-permission-required message). **A genuine mid-verification incident, investigated rather than assumed**: the first attempt to fetch the iOS bundle timed out and the Metro server appeared to have exited (port 8081 unresponsive) — a restart was attempted, which then failed with `EADDRINUSE`, revealing the original server was still alive the whole time and had simply been slow/busy serving the large Android bundle compile immediately after the new `expo-location` native module was added (a legitimate one-time cold-rebuild cost, not a real crash); the iOS bundle request was retried against the still-running original server and succeeded. The server exited on its own sometime after that (confirmed gone, not stopped by any action here) — not restarted again, since both platform bundle checks had already succeeded by that point. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_raw_punches`/`mobile_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo.

- UNIT 18 — Mobile Attendance History and Status Screen completed:
  - **A real, blocking regression in `@recafco/web`'s build was found and fixed at the root cause, not routed around, before this unit's own feature work could even be verified**: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web build` failed with `Module not found: Can't resolve 'zod/v4/core'` from inside `@hookform/resolvers/zod`. Root cause, confirmed via direct `require.resolve` probing (not guessed): `@hookform/resolvers` has never declared `zod` as a real dependency or peerDependency in its own `package.json` — every prior unit's successful web build (Units 12–17) worked only because pnpm happened to phantom-link some zod copy into its resolution scope as an accidental side effect of the broader lockfile's shape. Installing `expo-location` into `apps/mobile` this unit triggered a workspace-wide re-resolution that broke that lucky accident for `apps/web` specifically (pnpm's dependency resolution is one shared graph across the whole monorepo, not per-package). Fixed by adding a `packageExtensions` entry to `pnpm-workspace.yaml` declaring the dependency pnpm should have inferred (`'@hookform/resolvers': { dependencies: { zod: '*' } }`), then re-running `pnpm install` — this makes the link deterministic and immune to unrelated lockfile churn anywhere else in the workspace, rather than hoping the phantom-hoisting accident keeps recurring. Re-verified: `zod` now resolves correctly from `@hookform/resolvers`'s own scope, and `pnpm --filter @recafco/web build` passes cleanly again. This closes the exact risk flagged as an open question back in Unit 15 ("investigate why `apps/web` doesn't hit the same collision").
