Since this was a direct, foreseeable consequence of this unit's own necessary action — not an unrelated pre-existing problem — both were identified by process/command-line inspection, stopped, and restarted cleanly (confirmed healthy via `/health`, `/health/db`, and a `200` from `/`), then stopped again at the end of this unit's own verification, unlike the Expo/Metro dev server (untouched throughout, since nothing in this unit's own actions affected it and it was never unresponsive).
  - **API client extended** with `src/lib/api/attendance-summaries.ts` (`DailyAttendanceStatus` type, an `AttendanceDailySummary` interface — no nested relation fields, since the mobile client doesn't need them, matching the same "only what's actually used" trimming already applied to the mobile `attendance-punches.ts` client in Unit 17 — and `listMyAttendanceSummaries(params)`), reusing the same `PaginatedResult<T>` type already exported from `attendance-punches.ts` rather than redefining it a second time. Every field name was re-confirmed by reading `AttendanceSummariesService.findMine`/`RELATIONS_INCLUDE` directly in this unit, not carried over from memory of the web client built in Unit 14.
  - **New shared status-copy and date-utility modules**: `src/lib/attendance/summary-status-copy.ts` (`DAILY_ATTENDANCE_STATUS_COPY` for all 8 `DailyAttendanceStatus` values, matching `ui-context.md`'s tone conventions) and `src/lib/attendance/date-utils.ts` (`getKuwaitTodayDateString()`, `kuwaitDateStringFromWorkDate()`, `formatDateTimeForDisplay()`, `formatDateForDisplay()`, `formatMinutesAsHours()` — the exact 3 utility shapes the task named, plus one small necessary addition). `punch-status-copy.ts` was updated in place with the task's exact requested employee-friendly wording (`PENDING_HR_REVIEW` → "Submitted, waiting for review", `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` → "Accepted by system", `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_LOCATION` → "Rejected: outside allowed location", `REJECTED_FAKE_GPS` → "Rejected: location issue detected", `REJECTED_DUPLICATE` → "Duplicate punch", with the remaining statuses given matching-style wording) and gained a new `officialFlagLabel(isOfficial)` helper ("Official" / "Not yet official") — the task's third named copy-helper category.
  - **A real, non-obvious timezone bug was caught and avoided before it shipped, not discovered after**: `workDate` is stored by the backend as a UTC-midnight timestamp that already represents the correct Kuwait calendar day (per the backend's own `toKuwaitWorkDate`) — formatting it with `toLocaleDateString()` using the *device's* local timezone (the naive approach) would silently shift the displayed date backward by a day on any device set to a timezone west of UTC (e.g. the Americas). `formatDateForDisplay()` explicitly passes `{ timeZone: 'UTC' }` to lock the displayed date to the value actually stored, documented inline with the reasoning; `formatDateTimeForDisplay()` (for real moment-in-time values like `officialInTime`/`punchTimeSource`) deliberately does the opposite — no forced timezone, since an employee should see a punch time in their own device's local time, which is the correct behavior for an actual instant rather than a labeled calendar day.
  - **Shared `InfoRow` component extracted** to `src/components/info-row.tsx` — this unit's Today Status card needed the exact same label/value row pattern the Device screen (Unit 16) already had as a local, unexported function; crossing the "reused by 2+ modules" threshold this project has applied consistently since Unit 4, it was extracted rather than duplicated a second time, and `device.tsx` was updated to import the shared version instead of keeping its own copy (dead `infoRow`/`infoLabel`/`infoValue` style entries removed from `device.tsx` in the same pass).
  - **`/attendance` screen gained three new sections, keeping every Unit 17 punch behavior unchanged**: a **Today** card at the very top (matching `ui-context.md`'s "Employee Mobile Home... must prioritize punch status" layout guidance) showing status/official-in/official-out/total-worked/late/early/overtime plus a 🔒 lock indicator when `lockedAt` is set, or the task's exact specified "No daily summary calculated yet... HR/system validation will calculate the final daily status" message when no summary exists for today; an improved **Latest Punches** list (added the official/non-official flag next to the existing type/time/status/source fields, and switched to the friendlier status wording via the updated shared copy module); and a new **Summary History** section (last 15 summaries, date/status/official in-out/total worked, with late/early/overtime shown only when non-zero — a deliberately different display rule than the Today card, which always shows all three, exactly matching the task's own differing instructions for the two sections).
  - **"Today" is derived from the same single summaries fetch used for the History list, not a second API call** — `listMyAttendanceSummaries({ limit: 15 })` already returns entries ordered `workDate: desc`, so today's entry (if it exists) is simply found via `summaries.find(s => kuwaitDateStringFromWorkDate(s.workDate) === getKuwaitTodayDateString())`. This was deliberately chosen over a second `from`/`to`-filtered request for today specifically (verified live via curl that both approaches return identical data) — one fetch is simpler and is enough to serve both UI sections, and the task's own "keep it simple, no heavy libraries" framing favors avoiding a second network round-trip for data already in hand.
  - **Refresh behavior extended to all three data sources**: `handleRefresh` (used by both pull-to-refresh and the explicit "Refresh" link) now runs `loadDeviceStatus()`, `loadPunches()`, and the new `loadSummaries()` in parallel via `Promise.all`, per the task's explicit requirement; a summaries-fetch failure does not block or hide the punch buttons or the rest of the screen — each of the three sections has its own independent loading/error state, matching the task's "Do not block punching just because summary history fails" requirement.

confirmed a user with no linked employee gets a clean `400` from both `GET /attendance-punches/me` and `GET /attendance-summaries/me` (matching the shared `resolveEmployeeIdForUser` error message). **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from Units 15–17, now 4 units deep), directly requested compiled JS bundles from the running Metro dev server for both `platform=android` (200 OK, ~6.8MB) and `platform=ios` (200 OK, ~6.8MB) — the Android bundle was grepped to confirm it contains this unit's new source strings (the exact "No daily summary calculated yet"/"HR/system validation will calculate" text, "Summary History", the updated "Submitted, waiting for review" punch copy, "Not yet official", and the `attendance-summaries/me` endpoint path). **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_daily_summaries`/`attendance_raw_punches`/`mobile_devices`/`employees`/`departments`/`designations`/`work_locations` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; - UNIT 18.1 — Mobile Navigation and Logout UX Fix completed:
  - **Root cause of the reported bug, confirmed by reading the code before touching it**: prior to this unit, `(app)/_layout.tsx` used a plain `Stack` navigator, and **no screen anywhere in the app ever called `router.push('/profile')`** — Home's card grid only linked to `/attendance` and `/device`. This meant the Profile screen (and therefore the Logout button) was not merely "not obvious," it was **completely unreachable** through any UI element. The phone screenshot's complaint was accurate and understated; this was a real navigation dead-end, not a polish issue.
  - **Fixed by converting `(app)/_layout.tsx` from a `Stack` to a `Tabs` layout** (`import { Tabs } from 'expo-router'`), with four always-visible bottom tabs — Home, Device, Attendance, Profile — each with an icon (`@expo/vector-icons`'s `Ionicons`: `home`, `phone-portrait`, `time`, `person`) and label, so Profile (and Logout) is now reachable with one tap from anywhere in the app, permanently visible, not dependent on any card or link being present on another screen.
  - **A significant, unplanned dependency-version finding was uncovered while wiring this up, investigated fully rather than worked around**: adding `@expo/vector-icons` via `npx expo install` triggered Expo CLI's own SDK-compatibility-correction behavter, which rewrote **every** Expo-ecosystem dependency in `apps/mobile/package.json` — `expo` (`^57.0.4` → `^54.0.35`), `expo-router` (`~57.0.4` → `~6.0.24`), `react-native` (`0.86.0` → `0.81.5`), `react`/`react-dom` (`19.2.3` → `19.1.0`), `eslint-config-expo` (`^57.0.0` → `^10.0.0`), and every other `expo-*` package. Investigation traced this back one unit further: Unit 18's own `pnpm install` (run to apply the `packageExtensions` zod fix) had already silently drifted `react-native` to `0.81.5` in the lockfile — visible at the time only as an unexplained, seemingly-unrelated `pnpm peers check` warning, not yet reflected in `package.json`'s version ranges. This unit's `expo install` call simply finalized/surfaced a version correction that had already begun happening. Given `expo install`'s own compatibility matrix is the most authoritative signal available in this environment, and the "SDK 57" line every mobile unit (15–18) believed it was building against does not appear to be a real, currently-resolvable release, the corrected version set was accepted rather than fought — reverting to force "57.x" back would mean reintroducing whatever inconsistency caused pnpm to abandon it in the first place. **Every mobile unit's prior work was re-verified against the corrected version set in this unit**: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` both pass clean, and fresh Android + iOS bundle compiles (~7.4MB each, both `200 OK`) succeeded against an Expo dev server that had already been restarted with `--clear` before this unit began — confirming Units 15–18's screens all continue to work correctly under the corrected dependency set, not just this unit's new tab code. This is flagged prominently as an open question below since it means "SDK 57" throughout Units 15–18's own Completed/Architecture-Decision entries no longer matches what is actually resolvable on disk.
  - **`Tabs` import path needed a small fix once the real installed version was known**: initially written as `import { Tabs } from 'expo-router/js-tabs'` (the currently-recommended, non-deprecated import path documented for the SDK 57 line originally installed in Unit 15) — but `expo-router@6.0.24` (the version now actually on disk) has no `js-tabs` entry point at all; that naming split is specific to the newer line. Fixed by using the plain, version-appropriate `import { Tabs } from 'expo-router'`, confirmed correct by reading the actual installed package's own file listing rather than assumed from prior units' documentation.
  - **A shared `NO_EMPLOYEE_MESSAGE` constant** (`src/lib/auth/no-employee-message.ts`) replaces three different, inconsistent shorter messages previously scattered across Home/Device/Attendance ("Not linked to an employee record" / "Your user account is not linked to an employee profile. Contact HR." — two different wordings for the same condition) with the task's exact specified copy, used identically in all four screens now including Profile (which previously had no blocking notice at all, just an inline "Not linked to an employee record" value for the Employee ID field).
  - **Home screen's card badges were fixed to reflect actual navigability, a real pre-existing inconsistency**: all four cards previously showed "Coming soon" regardless of whether they had a working `href` — meaning "Today Attendance," "Register Device," and "My Attendance" all visually claimed to be unavailable even though tapping them already worked correctly since Unit 15. Cards with a real destination now show "Tap to open →" in accent color; only "Corrections" (genuinely not built until Unit 19) keeps "Coming soon," now additionally rendered at reduced opacity to visually read as disabled. Home also gained the same `!hasEmployee` gate the other screens already had (previously it showed no warning at all, just a small inline "Not linked to an employee record" line easy to miss).
  - **Profile screen's employee-linked status was made explicit and visually distinct**: a colored status dot (success green when linked, warning amber when not) next to "Linked (`<employeeId>`)" / "Not Linked" text, plus the full `NO_EMPLOYEE_MESSAGE` notice card when not linked — matching the task's explicit "Profile screen must show... Employee linked status" requirement more directly than the previous plain "Employee ID: `<value or fallback text>`" row.
  - **No screen's actual data-fetching, punch, registration, or history logic was touched** — every change in this unit is additive UI/navigation/copy work layered on top of Units 15–18's existing screens (which is why their internal state management, effect patterns, and API calls needed zero changes and kept passing typecheck/lint untouched).
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly (`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared`/`@recafco/web` all unaffected — each app has its own isolated pnpm dependency tree, so the mobile-only SDK correction did not touch them). **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from Units 15–18, now 5 units deep — this unit's own task was explicitly *about* a UX problem only visible on a real phone screenshot, underscoring how much this gap matters), verification relied on: `tsc`/`eslint` passing, and fresh Android + iOS bundle compiles from an already-`--clear`-restarted Metro dev server (not started by this session — found already running, left running afterward, consistent with the established precedent of not touching processes this session didn't start), both `200 OK`, both grepped to confirm they contain this unit's exact new source strings ("Tap to open", the full `NO_EMPLOYEE_MESSAGE` text, "Employee Profile", "Not Linked", and all four `Ionicons` tab icon names). No backend calls were needed or made — this is a pure mobile UI/navigation unit with no new API surface, so no test data was created and none needed cleanup.

approved the `MISSED_OUT` correction and rejected the `MISSED_IN` one (with a reason) via the admin API; confirmed `GET /attendance-corrections/me` for `unit19emp` reflects both final states correctly (`APPROVED` with `approvedAt`/`approvedByUserId` populated, `REJECTED` with `rejectedReason`/`rejectedAt` populated) and that `findMine` does include nested relations (`employee`/`requestedBy`/`approvedBy`/`targetPunch`), unlike `attendance-punches.findMine`; confirmed `unit19noemp` gets a clean `400` from both `GET /me` and `POST /request`. **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from Units 15–18.1, now 7 units deep, and now covering the most interactive mobile screen shipped so far — a multi-field conditional form), directly requested compiled JS bundles from the running Metro dev server for both `platform=android` and `platform=ios` (both `200 OK`, ~7.5MB each), grepped to confirm they contain this unit's new source strings ("Submit Request", "Waiting for approval"×2, "CorrectionsScreen"×5). **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that `attendance_corrections`/`attendance_raw_punches`/`attendance_daily_summaries`/`employees` are all back to 0 rows and `users` has only `superadmin`; - UNIT 19.2 — Employee User Account Provisioning + Password Change Reminder completed:
  - **Schema**: Added three nullable `DateTime` fields to `User` — `temporaryPasswordIssuedAt`, `passwordChangedAt`, `passwordReminderDismissedAt` (the third is reserved for a future server-persisted dismiss state; this unit's actual "Later" behavior is implemented client-side only, see below) — via migration `20260715072054_user_password_tracking_fields`. **A real process-hygiene incident occurred applying it**: `prisma migrate dev` applied the migration successfully, but the subsequent `prisma generate` failed with `EPERM` because the already-running API dev server (left over from an earlier session, not started by this unit) held the Windows file lock on the native query-engine DLL. Per this project's process-hygiene discipline, stopping a pre-existing process required explicit user confirmation first (asked via a clarifying question rather than assumed) — approved, then the three related PIDs were stopped, the client regenerated cleanly, and the API dev server was restarted in the background and reconfirmed healthy before continuing. Exactly the same category of incident as Unit 18's `pnpm install`-triggered dev-server disruption, handled the same way: fix forward, don't route around it, and take responsibility for restarting what this unit's own necessary action broke.
  - **New permissions**: `employees.createUserAccount` and `users.resetPassword` added to `PERMISSIONS`. Role mapping followed the task's literal, deliberately narrow instruction: **only `HR_ADMIN`** (and `SUPER_ADMIN` via `ALL_PERMISSION_CODES`) was granted both — `HR_MANAGER` was **not** given either, despite already holding broader employee-management permissions (`employees.disable`, `users.create`, etc.) than `HR_ADMIN` in the existing seed. This is flagged as an Open Question below since it's a real, deliberate policy asymmetry worth re-confirming with actual HR org-chart approval rather than assuming it was accidental.
  - **RBAC bootstrap re-run, not modified**: `create-super-admin.ts`'s existing `upsertPermissionsAndRoles()` (Prisma `upsert` per permission/role-permission row) was already idempotent by construction — no code change was needed to satisfy "make RBAC bootstrap idempotently upsert new permissions." Re-ran it live with the existing superadmin's bootstrap credentials; confirmed it upserted the two new permission rows and the `HR_ADMIN`/`SUPER_ADMIN` role-permission links without erroring on the already-existing superadmin user, and confirmed live via `/auth/me` that superadmin gained both new permissions immediately.
  - **`password-policy.util.ts`** (new, `apps/api/src/auth/`): a single `validatePasswordPolicy(password, username)` function — min 8 chars, at least one letter, at least one number-or-symbol, and reject password === username — deliberately permissive per the task's explicit instruction not to reject the HR-friendly `Recafco@{employeeCode}` default (which has letters + the `@` symbol but no digit, and passes cleanly). Shared by all three new/changed endpoints (create-user, reset-password, change-password) rather than duplicated per call site.
  - **`POST /employees/:id/create-user`** (new, `employees.controller.ts`/`.service.ts`): requires `employees.createUserAccount`; finds the employee (404 if missing), requires `ACTIVE` status (400 otherwise — no context found allowing inactive-employee login creation, so the strict reading was implemented), requires no existing linked user (409 `AttendanceCorrection`-style conflict) and no username collision (409), defaults username to `employeeCode` and temporary password to `Recafco@{employeeCode}` (HR override validated through the same policy util), hashes via the existing `argon2` `hashPassword`, creates the `User` with `mustChangePassword: true` and `temporaryPasswordIssuedAt` set, assigns the `EMPLOYEE` role, audits `EMPLOYEE_USER_ACCOUNT_CREATED` with a passwordHash-free snapshot, and returns the plain temporary password exactly once in the response body (never logged, never re-queryable afterward).
  - **New `UsersModule`** (`apps/api/src/users/`) — the project's first module scoped around the `User` model itself rather than a domain entity, added because `POST /users/:id/reset-password` operates on a user that may or may not have a linked employee, so it doesn't naturally belong under `/employees`. `resetPassword()` requires `users.resetPassword`; generates the default temp password from the user's linked employee's `employeeCode` if none is provided, or requires an explicit `temporaryPassword` for employee-less users (clean 400 otherwise — this closes off resetting a no-employee account without HR consciously typing a password for it); clears `failedLoginAttempts`/`lockedUntil`; revokes every currently-active refresh token for that user in the same transaction as the password update (closing all existing sessions, not just a courtesy); audits `USER_PASSWORD_RESET` with before/after snapshots that include no password material.
  - **`POST /auth/change-password`** (new, JWT-only, no permission decorator — every authenticated user may change their own password, matching the task's "EMPLOYEE: change own password only through `/auth/change-password`" rule structurally: there is no other route that lets anyone touch another user's password hash except the two admin-permissioned endpoints above): verifies `currentPassword` via `argon2.verify` (401 if wrong), rejects `newPassword !== confirmPassword` and `newPassword === currentPassword` (400 each), runs the same policy util against `newPassword`, updates `passwordHash`/`mustChangePassword: false`/`passwordChangedAt`, and — like reset-password — revokes every active refresh token for the user in the same transaction. **A real, disclosed simplification**: this revokes *all* sessions, not "all except the current one" as the task's own wording allowed as an acceptable fallback — `AccessTokenPayload` carries no `jti`/session identifier linking it back to a specific `RefreshToken` row, so "except current" isn't cheaply derivable without a broader session-tracking change. Documented as a future improvement below rather than approximated with a guess. Audits `USER_PASSWORD_CHANGED` (actor = the user themself).
  - **`/auth/me` extended**: `AuthService.getProfile()` now also returns `temporaryPasswordIssuedAt` and `passwordChangedAt` (both nullable), alongside the existing `mustChangePassword` — confirmed live that a freshly-provisioned employee shows `mustChangePassword: true` with `temporaryPasswordIssuedAt` set and `passwordChangedAt: null`, and that changing password flips both fields correctly in the very next `/auth/me` call.
  - **`EmployeesService.RELATIONS_INCLUDE` gained a `user: { select: { id, username, status, mustChangePassword } }` include** — chosen deliberately as "Option A" from the task's own two offered choices (extend the existing list/detail response vs. add a second per-row API call), since HR's employee table needs to show a login-account badge and pick the right action (Create Login vs. Reset Password) for every row in one list call, and the change is a one-line addition to an existing Prisma `include` object with no new endpoint, no N+1 risk, and no extra round-trip. `toResponse()` needed no change since it already spreads the fetched Prisma row including whatever relations were included.
  - **Web (`apps/web`)**: `lib/api/employees.ts` gained `LinkedUserRef`/`Employee.user`/`createEmployeeUserAccount()`; new `lib/api/users.ts` with `resetUserPassword()`; `lib/api/client.ts` gained `ChangePasswordInput`/`changePassword()` and extended `CurrentUserProfile` with the two new nullable timestamp fields. `lib/auth/auth-context.tsx` gained `updateUser(patch)` (a plain `setUser` merge, not a new fetch) so a successful password change can flip `mustChangePassword` in-memory immediately without a full `/auth/me` re-fetch.
  - **Web Employees page**: a new "Login Account" table column (badge: "Login account exists" / "No login account", plus a "Temporary password not yet changed" hint when applicable) and row actions gated on the new permissions — "Create Login" (only shown for `ACTIVE` employees with no linked user, requires `employees.createUserAccount`) and "Reset Password" (only shown when a linked user exists, requires `users.resetPassword`). `CreateLoginModal`/`ResetPasswordModal` show a read-only username preview, a pre-filled `Recafco@{employeeCode}` temporary-password field HR can overwrite, and the task's exact required explanatory copy. On success, both hand off to a shared `CredentialRevealModal` ("Copy this password now. It will not be shown again." + a Copy-to-clipboard button) — the plain password is held only in that one transient piece of React state (`credentialResult`), cleared the moment the reveal modal closes, never persisted, never logged.
  - **A real React Compiler `set-state-in-effect` violation was found and fixed by restructuring, not suppressing, in 4 places** — this web codebase has the same lint rule as mobile (previously only ever seen triggered on the mobile side in Units 15–19). All four were "sync a prop into state via an effect" patterns: (1) `CredentialRevealModal`'s copied-flag reset was replaced with a **derived comparison** (`copied = copiedFor === result.temporaryPassword`) instead of an effect-driven reset — a fresh result object never equals the last-copied password, so the "Copied!" label naturally reverts with no effect at all; (2)/(3) `CreateLoginModal`/`ResetPasswordModal`'s default-temporary-password sync was replaced with a **lazy `useState` initializer** (`useState(() => employee ? ... : '')`) plus a `key={employee?.id ?? 'none'}` on the parent's render site, forcing a full remount (and therefore a fresh initializer run) whenever the targeted employee changes, rather than an effect reassigning state after the fact; (4) `ChangePasswordModal`'s reset-on-open effect was replaced by **splitting into an outer always-mounted wrapper and an inner `ChangePasswordModalContent` that is only mounted while `open`** — a fresh mount already starts with clean defaults, so no reset effect is needed. This is a different flavor of fix than mobile's "declare the function inside the effect" pattern (which addresses a *different* violation of the same family — see Units 15–19); the common thread across both is: prefer restructuring so the state is correct without ever needing to imperatively force it inside an effect.
  - **`PasswordReminder` (new, `components/auth/`)**: a dismissible banner rendered inside `(app)/layout.tsx` (visible on every authenticated page, not just Employees) showing the task's exact required copy plus "Change Password"/"Later" buttons. "Later" sets an in-memory `dismissedAt` timestamp; a `setTimeout` clears it after 2 minutes, and — since this state lives in a component that unmounts on logout (the whole `(app)` tree is gated by `ProtectedLayout`) — a fresh login also naturally resets it, satisfying "show again after 2 minutes or next login" without any persisted/server-side dismiss state (the schema's `passwordReminderDismissedAt` field exists for a *future* cross-device/cross-tab persistent version of this, not used by this unit's actual implementation — see Open Questions). A "Change Password" entry point was also added directly to `AppHeader` (independent of the reminder), satisfying the task's "available from header/profile/account area" requirement even for users who don't currently have `mustChangePassword: true`.
  - **Mobile (`apps/mobile`)**: `src/lib/api/client.ts` gained `ChangePasswordInput`/`changePassword()` (mobile only needed this one of the three new API functions, per the task's own scoping — `createEmployeeUserAccount`/`resetUserPassword` are HR/web-only actions, deliberately never exposed to mobile, structurally preventing "employee creates account for themselves from mobile"). `src/lib/auth/auth-context.tsx` gained the same `updateUser(patch)` shape as web. New `app/(app)/change-password.tsx` screen (current/new/confirm fields via the established RHF `Controller` pattern, a dedicated success state, reachable via `router.push('/change-password')` but **not** added as a `Tabs.Screen** — it's a push-only route layered on top of the tab navigator, exactly like how a non-tab route behaves under Expo Router's `Tabs`, so it doesn't crowd the 5-tab bar). New `src/components/password-reminder-banner.tsx` (same copy/Later/2-minute-or-next-login logic as web's `PasswordReminder`, independently implemented since this project keeps web and mobile UI code fully separate) rendered at the top of the Home screen; a plain "Change Password" button was also added to the Profile screen for parity with web's header entry point.
  - **Security properties verified structurally, not just asserted**: no endpoint ever returns `passwordHash` (every new response is a hand-built object listing only safe fields, never a raw Prisma row spread); the plain temporary password is returned exactly once, directly in the create/reset response body, never persisted beyond that single HTTP response and never written to any log statement; `ChangePasswordDto`'s fields are never echoed back in the audit log's `afterSnapshot` (only `{ mustChangePassword: false, passwordChangedAt }` is recorded); mobile has no code path that can call `create-user`/`reset-password` at all (the functions simply don't exist in the mobile API client), so "employee cannot create account for themselves from mobile" holds by omission, not by a runtime check that could be bypassed.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm install` (no-op, no new dependencies), `pnpm --filter @recafco/db typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/api typecheck`/`build`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint`, and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly (zero errors, zero warnings) after the 4 lint fixes above. **Full backend contract independently re-verified live via curl**: created a real test employee (`U192TEST`), created its login account (confirmed username=`U192TEST`, temporary password=`Recafco@U192TEST` exactly, `user: null` → populated on the employee response), confirmed a duplicate create-user call returns a clean `409`; logged in with the temporary password and confirmed `/auth/me` returns `mustChangePassword: true` with `temporaryPasswordIssuedAt` set; exercised every `change-password` validation branch (wrong current password → `401`, mismatched confirmation → `400`, new-equals-current → `400`, success → `200`); confirmed the old temporary password then fails login (`401`) and the new password succeeds, with `/auth/me` now showing `mustChangePassword: false` and `passwordChangedAt` populated; reset the password as superadmin (default-generated, matching `Recafco@{employeeCode}` again), confirmed login with the reset password works and the prior (changed) password no longer does; confirmed a deliberate `HR_MANAGER` test account genuinely lacks both new permissions (`/auth/me` shows `false` for both, and a live `create-user` attempt as that account returns `403`) while a deliberate `HR_ADMIN` test account has both; confirmed the ACTIVE-employee-only rule by terminating a second test employee and getting a clean `400` from `create-user`; confirmed via direct `psql` that all three new audit actions (`EMPLOYEE_USER_ACCOUNT_CREATED`, `USER_PASSWORD_RESET`, `USER_PASSWORD_CHANGED`) were recorded with correct actor attribution and that no snapshot anywhere contains the string `passwordHash`. **Since no Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from Units 15–19, now 8 units deep), directly requested compiled JS bundles from the already-running Metro dev server for both `platform=android` and `platform=ios` (both `200 OK`, ~7.5MB each), grepped to confirm they contain this unit's new source strings ("Change Password"×4 each, "You are using a temporary password...", "ChangePasswordScreen"). **The already-running API dev server needed a one-time restart mid-unit** (see the schema note above, done with explicit user approval); the web dev server and Metro/Expo dev server were never touched, confirmed healthy throughout via `/health`, `200` from `/`, and `/status`. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that the two test employees (`U192TEST`, `U192TERM`) and all three test users (`U192TEST`, `u192hrmanager`, `u192hradmin`) are gone, that no audit log rows for the three new actions remain, and that a genuinely pre-existing employee (`MIQ`, not created by this session) was correctly left untouched rather than assumed to be test debris; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo.

- UNIT 20 — Payroll Lock / Monthly Closing Backend completed:
  - **Schema**: `AttendancePayrollLock` gained `reason`, `notes`, `createdByUserId`, and `summaryCount` (all nullable). The task's suggested enum (`OPEN`/`LOCKED`/`UNLOCKED`/`CANCELLED`) and field names (`unlockedByUserId`/`unlockedAt`/`unlockReason`) **deliberately were not used** — the existing `PayrollLockStatus` enum (`OPEN`/`LOCKED`/`REOPENED`, from Unit 1) and existing `reopenedByUserId`/`reopenedAt`/`reopenReason` fields (also from Unit 1) already cover the exact same semantics, and the task explicitly said "if existing enum differs, use existing one safely." `REOPENED` is treated as this project's name for "unlocked," and the unlock endpoint writes to `reopenedByUserId`/`reopenedAt`/`reopenReason` rather than adding a parallel, differently-named set of columns that would mean the same thing twice. Applied via migration `20260715075405_payroll_lock_extended_fields`. **The same Prisma-client-regeneration/EPERM process-hygiene situation from Unit 19.2 recurred identically**: the already-running (not self-started) API dev server held the query-engine DLL lock after `migrate dev` applied the schema change; explicit user confirmation was sought and given before stopping it, and a second, previously-undisclosed orphaned `dist/main` process (spawned by the watch process at the exact moment it was stopped, then left parentless) was discovered mid-fix — a second confirmation was sought for that specific PID before stopping it too, rather than assuming the original approval covered it. Client regenerated cleanly and the API dev server was restarted and reconfirmed healthy.
  - **New permissions**: `payrollLocks.read`, `payrollLocks.create`, `payrollLocks.lock`, `payrollLocks.unlock`, added alongside (not replacing) the pre-existing, previously-unused `attendance.payroll.lock`/`attendance.payroll.unlock` permissions from Unit 3. Role mapping followed the task's literal table exactly: `HR_ADMIN`/`HR_MANAGER`/`PAYROLL_OFFICER` all get `read`/`create`/`lock`; `AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` get `read` only; `unlock` is granted only to `SUPER_ADMIN` (via `ALL_PERMISSION_CODES`) — no other role holds it, matching "Unlock should be restricted: SUPER_ADMIN only by default" literally. RBAC bootstrap re-run live (no code change needed, same idempotent-upsert mechanism confirmed working in Unit 19.2) to push the new permissions/role-links into the database.
  - **New `PayrollLocksModule`** (`apps/api/src/payroll-locks/`): `PayrollLocksController`/`PayrollLocksService` (`GET /payroll-locks` with pagination/status/from/to filters sorted `createdAt: 'desc'`, `GET /payroll-locks/:id`, `POST /payroll-locks` creating an `OPEN` period, `POST /payroll-locks/:id/lock`, `POST /payroll-locks/:id/unlock`), plus a new `PayrollLockGuardService` (`findActiveLockForDate`, `isDateInLockedPeriod`, `assertWorkDateNotLocked`, `assertDateRangeNotOverlappingActiveLock`) exported from the module so `AttendanceSummariesModule` and `AttendanceCorrectionsModule` can both import `PayrollLocksModule` and inject the guard without any circular dependency (the guard only touches `DatabaseService`, never the other two modules' services). `periodStart`/`periodEnd` are parsed via the existing `parseWorkDateInput` from `attendance-summaries/kuwait-date.util.ts` — the exact same canonical UTC-midnight-of-Kuwait-day representation `AttendanceDailySummary.workDate` already uses, so range queries compare directly (`workDate: { gte: periodStart, lte: periodEnd }`) with no extra timezone conversion needed.
  - **Overlap prevention**: `assertDateRangeNotOverlappingActiveLock` treats `OPEN` and `LOCKED` as "active" (matching the task's definition) and rejects a new/re-locked range with a clean `409` if it overlaps any other active period's `[periodStart, periodEnd]`. Called both at `create()` (against all other active periods) and at `lockPeriod()` (against all other active periods, excluding itself) — the latter is a defensive re-check, not expected to ever actually fire given creation-time prevention already holds the invariant "at most one active period covers any given date," but it costs nothing to keep as a safety net per the task's explicit "use it in ... create/lock/unlock logic" instruction.
  - **`lockPeriod()` behavior, confirmed live**: only `OPEN` or `REOPENED` periods can be locked (already-`LOCKED` is a `409`); sets `lockedAt`/`lockedByUserId` on every `AttendanceDailySummary` row in range that doesn't already have `lockedAt` set (defensive `lockedAt: null` filter, harmless given overlap prevention already guarantees no other lock could have touched them); stores the **total** summary count in range as `summaryCount` (a snapshot for later reporting) while returning the **actually-just-transitioned** count as `affectedSummaryCount` in the response — these can differ in theory (if some rows were already locked by some other inconsistent state) even though in practice, under the overlap invariant, they're always equal. Both counts and the lock's before/after state are captured in a single `$transaction` and audited as `PAYROLL_PERIOD_LOCKED`.
  - **`unlockPeriod()` behavior, confirmed live**: only a currently-`LOCKED` period can be unlocked (`409` otherwise, which also structurally satisfies "clears lockedAt only if this lock is the active locked period" — since at most one `LOCKED` period can ever cover a given range under the overlap invariant, requiring `status === LOCKED` before proceeding **is** the "is this the active lock" check, no separate lookup needed); clears `lockedAt` from every summary in range that currently has it set, sets `status: REOPENED` plus `reopenedByUserId`/`reopenedAt`/`reopenReason`, all in one transaction, audited as `PAYROLL_PERIOD_UNLOCKED` with before/after snapshots.
  - **Guard integrated into `AttendanceSummaryCalculatorService.calculateForEmployeeDate`**: `payrollLockGuardService.assertWorkDateNotLocked(workDate)` is called immediately after parsing `workDate`, **before** the existing `AttendanceDailySummary.findUnique` + `existing?.lockedAt` check — this is the fix for the exact gap the task called out ("existing lockedAt checks are not enough alone"): a date inside a `LOCKED` period with **no summary row yet** would sail straight past the old per-row check (since `existing` would be `null`) and calculate a brand-new summary for a locked date. Confirmed live: calculating a locked date with an existing summary and a locked date with *no* summary both now return a clean `409` with the same message. The existing per-row `existing?.lockedAt` check was **kept, not removed** — it remains a correct, cheap early-exit for the common case and a safety net if a summary's `lockedAt` were ever set outside the period-lock flow.
  - **`recalculateRange` needed no code change at all** — it already wraps each candidate day's `calculateForEmployeeDate` call in a `try/catch` that increments `skippedLocked` on any `ConflictException` and continues the loop; since the new period-level guard throws the same `ConflictException` type, the existing catch block transparently absorbs it. Confirmed live: recalculating a range covering a locked day returns `skippedLocked: 1` without aborting or throwing for the batch as a whole.
  - **Guard integrated into `AttendanceCorrectionsService.approveCorrection`**: `payrollLockGuardService.assertWorkDateNotLocked(correction.workDate)` is called immediately after the `PENDING`-status check and **before** the existing `existingSummary?.lockedAt` check, the transaction, and any manual-punch creation — for the identical reason as the summary calculator: a correction targeting a locked date with no summary yet would otherwise slip past the old check entirely. Confirmed live: approving a correction for a locked date with an existing summary and one with *no* summary both return a clean `409`, and in both cases the correction's `status` remains `PENDING` and no `AttendanceRawPunch` manual-punch row is created (verified by re-fetching the correction afterward) — exactly matching "Do not create manual punch. Do not update correction status. Do not recalculate summary."
  - **Raw punch intake was not touched at all**, per the task's own explicit scoping — `AttendancePunchesService.createManual`/`createMobile`/`createDeviceImport` have no payroll-lock awareness and none was added; late device logs and manual entries continue to be stored as raw evidence regardless of any payroll lock, exactly as the task required ("do not block raw punch intake"). Confirmed this is already true by inspection — nothing in the raw-punch creation path calculates or touches a daily summary, so there was no accidental auto-recalculation-on-intake behavior to guard against in the first place.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm install` (no-op), `pnpm --filter @recafco/db typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/api typecheck`/`build`, and root `pnpm typecheck`/`build` all pass cleanly (`@recafco/web`/`@recafco/mobile`/`@recafco/shared` all unaffected — this is a pure backend unit with no web/mobile changes, matching the task's explicit "no web UI, no mobile UI" scope). **Full contract independently re-verified live via curl**: created a test employee, two manual punches, and a calculated summary for one date; calculated a second summary for a different date while its containing period was still `OPEN` (confirmed `OPEN` does **not** block, only `LOCKED` does); created two `PENDING` corrections (one for the date with a summary, one for a date with no summary); created a payroll lock period covering all of these dates; confirmed a deliberately-overlapping second period creation gets a clean `409`, and an invalid `periodStart > periodEnd` gets a clean `400`; locked the period and confirmed both existing summaries in range got `lockedAt` set, with `summaryCount: 2` and `affectedSummaryCount: 2` both correct; confirmed recalculating an existing locked summary and calculating a locked date with **no** existing summary both return `409` (the critical case the task called out); confirmed approving a correction for a locked date with a summary and one with no summary both return `409` with the correction left `PENDING` and no manual punch created; confirmed `recalculate-range` over the locked window returns `skippedLocked: 1` without aborting; confirmed three separate test accounts (`HR_ADMIN`, `HR_MANAGER`, `PAYROLL_OFFICER`) each get a clean `403` attempting `/unlock`; confirmed unlocking without a `reason` gets a clean `400`, and unlocking as `SUPER_ADMIN` with a reason succeeds, clearing `lockedAt` from both summaries (`affectedSummaryCount: 2`) and setting `status: REOPENED`; confirmed recalculation and correction approval both work again immediately after unlock; confirmed via `psql` all three new audit actions (`PAYROLL_LOCK_CREATED`, `PAYROLL_PERIOD_LOCKED`, `PAYROLL_PERIOD_UNLOCKED`) were recorded with correct actor attribution. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that the test employee, its punches/summaries/corrections, the test payroll lock period, and the three test users (`u20hradmin`/`u20hrmanager`/`u20payroll`, including their refresh tokens) are all gone, and that the genuinely pre-existing `MIQ` employee/user (not created by this session, already noted in Unit 19.2) was correctly left untouched; the throwaway `tmp-create-test-user.ts` script was deleted, not left in the repo.

- UNIT 19.3 — Employee Web/Mobile Experience Alignment completed:
  - **Root cause of the reported inconsistency, confirmed by reading the code before touching it**: web's `AppSidebar` filtered `NAV_ITEMS` per-item by permission but had no fallback content for a user with **none** of those permissions — a plain `EMPLOYEE`-role user (who genuinely holds none of `employees.read`/`departments.read`/etc., confirmed live via `/auth/me`) was left with a sidebar containing only "Dashboard," and Dashboard itself showed generic admin-oriented "Coming soon" cards (Employees/Attendance Today/Pending Corrections/Device Status) with no self-service content at all — exactly the "limited/empty sidebar" symptom reported. Separately, mobile's broken extra tab was a real, reproducible Expo Router bug: a route file in a `Tabs` layout's directory that isn't explicitly declared via `<Tabs.Screen>` still gets auto-registered as an implicit tab unless given `options={{ href: null }}` — Unit 19.2's `change-password.tsx` screen was added without such a declaration, so Expo Router silently added it as a 6th tab using its raw filename as the label.
  - **Web navigation decision**: `isAdminUser(user)` (new, `lib/auth/permissions.ts`) is `true` if the user holds *any* permission that unlocks at least one existing admin `NAV_ITEMS` entry (a new `ADMIN_NAV_PERMISSIONS` array, derived directly from `NAV_ITEMS` itself in `lib/routes.ts` rather than a second hand-maintained permission list). `AppSidebar` and the Dashboard page both branch on this single check: admin users keep the exact existing sidebar/Dashboard behavior unchanged; everyone else (in practice, `EMPLOYEE`-role users) gets a new 5-item `EMPLOYEE_NAV_ITEMS` (Dashboard, Device, Attendance, Corrections, Profile — no `permission` gating needed, since every route behind them only ever calls the current user's own `/me` endpoints). This is a **permission-based**, not role-name-based, split — it costs nothing extra for `SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` (who already hold some admin-read permissions and so correctly keep seeing admin nav, unchanged from before this unit) and fixes exactly the reported case without inventing a new "employee vs admin" concept the backend doesn't already express through permissions.
  - **Route naming decision**: new employee routes are `/device`, `/attendance`, `/corrections`, `/profile` — the shorter option from each "/my-X or /X" choice the task offered, chosen because none of the four collide with any existing admin route (`/mobile-devices`, `/attendance-punches` + `/attendance-summaries`, `/attendance-corrections` respectively — all different strings), so the shorter names read cleanly and match mobile's own equally short tab labels (Device/Attendance/Corrections) for the wording-consistency goal in this same task.
  - **Four new lightweight self-service pages** (`app/(app)/device`, `/attendance`, `/corrections`, `/profile`), all reusing **existing** API client functions with zero backend changes: `getMyMobileDevices()`, `listMyAttendancePunches()`, `listMyAttendanceSummaries()`, `listMyAttendanceCorrections()`, and `requestAttendanceCorrection()` (all already existed, some explicitly commented in prior units as "kept for reuse by a future self-service page" — this unit is that reuse). Device shows per-device status badges (`Not Registered`/`Pending`/`Approved`/`Blocked`/`Deactivated`, matching the task's exact wording) with a "Use the mobile app to register this device" note rather than building web device registration (explicitly acceptable per the task). Attendance shows two read-only tables (recent summaries, recent punches) with no admin filters and no validate/recalculate actions. Corrections shows a read-only own-requests table plus a "Request Correction" modal whose conditional field visibility (`requestedPunchType` only for `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT`, `requestedPunchTime` for all types except `OTHER`, `targetPunchId` only for `WRONG_IN`/`WRONG_OUT`) mirrors the exact same backend `@ValidateIf` rules the mobile Unit 19 correction form already encodes — no approve/reject UI exists on this page at all, and the request payload structurally cannot include `employeeId` (confirmed live: an explicit `employeeId` in the request body still gets a clean `400` from the backend's own whitelist validation, exactly as established in Unit 19). Profile shows username/employee-linked-status/roles/permission-count (identical fields and wording to mobile's Profile screen) plus a Change Password button that reuses the existing `ChangePasswordModal` component from Unit 19.2 unchanged.
  - **A real React Compiler lint warning was found and fixed at the root cause, not suppressed**: the new Corrections page's conditional-field logic initially called React Hook Form's `watch('correctionType')` directly, which the React Compiler flags as an "incompatible library" pattern (a function that can't be safely memoized) and skips compiling that component. Fixed by switching to `useWatch({ control, name: 'correctionType' })` — the officially compiler-compatible replacement for exactly this use case — rather than accepting the warning or disabling the rule.
  - **Two forms (`RequestCorrectionModal`, already-existing pattern from `ChangePasswordModal`) use the conditional-mount pattern established in Unit 19.2** (an outer always-rendered wrapper that returns `null` when closed, wrapping an inner content component only ever mounted while open) instead of an effect-driven reset-on-open — consistent with this project's now-established fix for the `set-state-in-effect` family of issues, applied proactively here rather than discovered via a lint failure.
  - **Mobile fix**: `(app)/_layout.tsx` gained one line — `<Tabs.Screen name="change-password" options={{ href: null }} />` — registering the route with the tab navigator (so it's still reachable via `router.push('/change-password')` from Profile and the reminder banner, both unchanged from Unit 19.2) while explicitly excluding it from the tab bar. No other mobile screen needed changes: Home's cards and Profile's Change Password/Sign Out buttons were already correct as of Units 19/19.2 — the task's mobile "keep pages aligned" requirements were already satisfied before this unit began, confirmed by re-reading the current screens rather than assumed.
  - **Password reminder behavior needed no changes on either platform** — both `PasswordReminder` (web) and `PasswordReminderBanner` (mobile), built in Unit 19.2, already show/hide based on `user.mustChangePassword` with a 2-minute-or-next-login "Later" dismissal and a direct path to Change Password; this unit only confirmed (via a fresh employee login live during verification) that a newly-provisioned account still reports `mustChangePassword: true` correctly.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint`, and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly (zero errors; the one React Compiler warning found during development was fixed, not left as an accepted warning). **Full contract independently re-verified live via curl**: created a real test employee, provisioned its login via the existing Unit 19.2 endpoint, confirmed the resulting `EMPLOYEE`-role account holds none of the ten admin-nav permissions (so it would render the new employee nav, not the old empty-admin-sidebar state) and reports `mustChangePassword: true`; hit all four `GET .../me` endpoints the new pages call and got clean `200`s with the same paginated/array shapes the pages already handle; submitted a real correction request (`201`) and confirmed a deliberate `employeeId` injection attempt still gets a clean `400`. **Mobile verified via a freshly-started Metro dev server** (not left over from a prior session — this session's own `expo start`, stopped again afterward once bundle verification completed, since no emulator/device was available to click through, the same disclosed gap carried from every mobile unit since Unit 15): fetched fresh Android and iOS bundles (both `200 OK`), and grepped the compiled output for `tabBarLabel:` entries — found exactly five (`'Home'`, `'Device'`, `'Attend.'`, `'Correct.'`, `'Profile'`), confirming the broken sixth tab is genuinely gone from the compiled bundle, not just the source. The already-running API and web dev servers (left over from an earlier session, not started by this unit) were never touched, confirmed healthy before and after. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that the test employee/user/correction/audit-log rows are gone and only the genuinely pre-existing `MIQ` employee/user and `superadmin` remain.

Because a fully custom `header` bypasses React Navigation's automatic status-bar handling, it explicitly reads `useSafeAreaInsets()` and pads for it itself. **A side effect of this global header replacing native per-screen titles**: `device.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, `corrections.tsx`, and `attendance.tsx` each gained a small in-body page-title `Text` ("My Device" / "Profile" / "Corrections" / "Attendance") so the screen's identity isn't lost — a deliberate, additive fix for a gap this same unit's own header change would otherwise have introduced, not a pre-existing bug.

- UNIT 19.5 — Web Employee Self-Service Benchmark Alignment completed:
  - **Benchmark alignment decision, same principle as mobile Unit 19.4**: the Pinnacle ESS's *shape* (grouped sidebar sections, a greeting+quick-links+summary-cards dashboard, a requests table, placeholder sections for not-yet-built modules) was borrowed; its branding, exact visuals, and any assumption of a backend RECAFCO doesn't have were not. Every new/changed component uses RECAFCO's own existing design tokens and card/table primitives (`Card`, `DataTable`, `StatusBadge` — all already established in Units 12–14/19.3), never a copied Pinnacle layout verbatim. Where the benchmark implies data RECAFCO's backend doesn't expose yet (leave balances, work location/department on the current user, payslips, announcements, documents), those render as honest, permanently-inert placeholders ("Coming soon" / "—" / static text) — never fabricated numbers, never a partial backend built just to fill a card.
  - **Admin/employee navigation split, same mechanism as Unit 19.3, now with a richer employee shape**: `isAdminUser(user)` (unchanged, still derived from `ADMIN_NAV_PERMISSIONS` in `lib/routes.ts`) decides between the **untouched** existing flat admin sidebar and a **new grouped** `EMPLOYEE_NAV_ITEMS` tree matching the task's Workflow/Time Management/Reports structure: `EmployeeNavEntry` is now a union of `{ type: 'link' }` and `{ type: 'group', children: (link | 'coming-soon')[] }`, replacing the flat 5-item list from Unit 19.3 (which was confirmed via `grep` to have exactly one consumer — `app-sidebar.tsx` — before being restructured, so no other file needed updating). "Coming soon" children (Leave Request, Document Request, Payslip) render as plain non-interactive text with a "Coming soon" tag — never a `<Link>`, never a button with an `onClick`, so there is structurally no path from them to a network call, matching the hard "coming soon must not call backend" requirement by construction rather than by a runtime guard.
  - **New `ProfileDropdown` component** (`components/layout/profile-dropdown.tsx`), employee-only: a plain local-state dropdown (button + `fixed inset-0` backdrop-to-close, no new dependency — the same "don't add a library for something a plain component can do" precedent as the mobile `SideMenu`/`CreateActionSheet` from Unit 19.4) showing username/role, then User Profile (→ `/profile`), Change Password (opens the existing `ChangePasswordModal`, unchanged), Sign Out (existing `useAuth().logout()`, unchanged). **`AppHeader` now branches on `isAdminUser()`**: admin gets a byte-identical `AdminHeader` (the original two-button header, extracted verbatim, not rewritten) so "keep admin header behavior working" is satisfied by literally not touching that code path; employee gets a new `EmployeeHeader` (notification bell → `window.alert('Notifications coming soon.')`, no backend call, plus `ProfileDropdown`).
  - **Employee Dashboard (`/dashboard`) fully redesigned** for the employee branch only (`AdminDashboard` is unchanged): greeting text now reads "Hello, `{username}`" (matching the task's exact wording) with employeeId/roles as a subtitle — same "documented, not fabricated" limitation as Unit 19.3/19.4 regarding no real `employeeCode`/`fullName` being available from `/auth/me`; a 4-item quick-links row (My Attendance, Correction Request, Device Registration, Change Password — the last one a button opening `ChangePasswordModal` locally, the other three real `<Link>`s to already-existing pages); 4 summary cards (Annual/Sick Leave Balance both "Coming soon", Work Location/Current Department both "—", since no self-service employee-detail endpoint exists to source them from); a "My Requests" card reusing `listMyAttendanceCorrections({ limit: 5 })` (already existed, already used by `/corrections`) with the task's exact empty-state text ("No requests to display."); side-by-side Announcements/My Documents placeholder cards with the task's exact static text, zero backend calls; an "Attendance Last 7 Days" card reusing `listMyAttendanceSummaries({ limit: 7 })` (already existed, already used by `/attendance`) with the task's exact empty-state text ("No attendance summary calculated yet.").
  - **Profile page gained four new rows** (Employee Code, Department, Designation, Work Location), all showing `—` — **not fetched, not guessed** — with an inline comment explaining exactly why (`/auth/me` returns only `employeeId`, never a full employee record; a future self-service employee-profile endpoint, explicitly *not* the admin-gated `GET /employees/:id`, would need to exist first) and explicitly noting Civil ID/religion/nationality/manager are deliberately never shown even as placeholders, per the task's own "do not fake" instruction.
  - **Security property verified structurally, not just asserted**: no employee-facing page or component added in this unit calls `GET /employees` or `GET /employees/:id` (confirmed by reading every new/changed file) — the admin-gated employee list/detail was never an option for any of these "use employee relation if available" fields, which is exactly why they show `—` instead. Every data-driven employee section (`My Requests`, `Attendance Last 7 Days`) calls only the same `.../me` endpoints already established as safe in Units 19.3/20.
