One real unused-variable lint warning (a leftover `useAuth()` destructure in `EmployeeDashboard` after the greeting text moved to the parent component) was found and fixed by removing the dead destructure, not by suppressing the rule. **Full contract independently re-verified live via curl**: confirmed `superadmin` still holds `employees.read` (so the admin sidebar renders unchanged); created a real test employee, provisioned its login via the existing Unit 19.2 endpoint, confirmed the resulting account holds none of the ten admin-nav permissions (so it renders the new employee sidebar/dashboard, not the admin one) and reports `mustChangePassword: true` (so the reminder banner would show); hit both dashboard data endpoints (`attendance-corrections/me`, `attendance-summaries/me`) and got clean `200`s. **No browser-automation tool was available in this environment** (the same disclosed gap carried from every web unit since Unit 11, now covering this app's most employee-facing redesign yet) — verified instead via `curl` route-rendering checks (`/dashboard`, `/profile` both `200`) plus the backend-contract checks above; the task's own "prefer real manual browser check because this is employee-facing UX" recommendation is repeated here, not skipped past. **All verification data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that the test employee/user/audit-log rows are gone and `MIQ`/`superadmin` (pre-existing, not created by any session) are the only rows left. Neither the already-running API nor web dev server was touched at any point.

- UNIT 20.1 — Automated Attendance Validation and Daily Auto-Calculation completed:
  - **Schema: no migration needed.** `PunchValidationStatus` already had `ACCEPTED` (confirmed by reading the schema before writing any code, not assumed) — this unit is the first time anything actually produces it, since Unit 7's original intake code and Unit 8's validation engine both only ever emitted `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`/`PENDING_HR_REVIEW`/`REJECTED_*` for the "clean" case.
  - **The real architectural gap this unit closed**: `AttendanceValidationService` (Unit 8) already contained the full rule engine (duplicate detection, Haversine geofence, GPS accuracy, device-status checks) but was never invoked at punch-creation time — `AttendancePunchesService.createMobile()`/`createDeviceImport()` hardcoded `validationStatus` directly at intake (a simplified, duplicated subset of the validator's own logic) and the full engine only ran later, when a human called `/attendance-punches/:id/validate` or `/validate-pending`. New orchestration methods (`AttendanceValidationService.processNewPunch`, called immediately after every mobile/device-import/manual punch is created, in the same request) now run the full engine at intake, so clean punches are accepted and their daily summary recalculated with zero human involvement.
  - **New `attendance-validation.constants.ts`**: `MOBILE_GPS_ACCURACY_MAX_METERS = 100` (raised from an earlier internal, undocumented 50m dev-mode threshold — now matches the mobile app's own existing client-side pre-check from Unit 17) and `DUPLICATE_WINDOW_MINUTES = 2`.
  - **Clean-case fallback changed from `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` to `ACCEPTED`** in both `validateMobilePunch` and `validateDevicePunch` — `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` is now reserved for a future non-blocking-issue case (none currently exists; every mobile-source condition that isn't fully clean routes to a specific `PENDING_*`/`REJECTED_*` status instead).
  - **Work-location "Option B" inference**: when a mobile punch doesn't specify `workLocationId` (the mobile app, per Unit 17's own documented scope, never sends one on real punches), the employee's own `workLocationId` (a real, already-populated field/relation) is used as a fallback, tagged with a non-blocking informational flag `WORK_LOCATION_INFERRED_FROM_EMPLOYEE_DEFAULT` — chosen over "Option A" (always `PENDING_HR_REVIEW` when the punch itself lacks a location) because the employee's assigned location is a reliable, non-guessed value, and enables real geofence checking for typical mobile punches for the first time. "Option A" (`PENDING_HR_REVIEW` + `WORK_LOCATION_NOT_ASSIGNED`) remains the fallback only when neither the punch nor the employee resolves to any work location.
  - **Device-status flag granularity improved**: a non-`APPROVED` mobile device now produces a specific `DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED`/`DEVICE_BLOCKED`/`DEVICE_DEACTIVATED` flag (previously one generic flag for all three) — the resulting `PunchValidationStatus` is still `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` in all three cases, since no more specific enum value exists. Fake-GPS flag renamed `CLIENT_SIGNAL_FAKE_GPS_OR_ROOT` → `FAKE_GPS_SIGNAL` to match the task's required flag-code list for a future HR exception dashboard.
  - **`AttendancePunchesService.createMobile`** no longer hardcodes device-approval branching inline — it now resolves the device row (attaching its FK unconditionally whenever one exists, even across a different employee, an improvement over the prior behavior that sometimes left the FK null) and delegates all classification to the validator via `processNewPunch`. `createDeviceImport`/`createManual` gained the identical `processNewPunch` call after their existing creation/audit logic (harmless for `createManual`, since `validateManualPunch` never produces an `ACCEPTED`-family status).
  - **New orchestration methods in `AttendanceValidationService`**: `processNewPunch(punch, requestActorUserId, context)` (the auto-path for all three intake routes — computes validation with `actorUserId: undefined` so `validatedByUserId` correctly stays `null`, audits `ATTENDANCE_PUNCH_AUTO_VALIDATED`/`AUTO_REJECTED`/`SENT_TO_REVIEW` with `actorUserId: null` — the decision itself was automated, distinct from who submitted the punch — then calls the shared `recalculateIfAccepted`); `recalculateIfAccepted(punch, requestActorUserId, context, automated)` (shared by `processNewPunch`, `validatePunch`, and `validatePending` — no-ops if the status isn't accepted-family, checks `PayrollLockGuardService.isDateInLockedPeriod` before calling `AttendanceSummaryCalculatorService.calculateForEmployeeDate`, catches `ConflictException` as a defensive fallback for the same locked-period case, and audits `ATTENDANCE_SUMMARY_AUTO_RECALCULATED` with `actorUserId: automated ? null : requestActorUserId`); `flagLockedPeriodPunch(punch)` (idempotently appends `LOCKED_PERIOD_LATE_PUNCH` to `fraudFlags` via a targeted update that touches only that column, never `validationStatus`/`isOfficial`).
  - **A real staleness bug was found and fixed during manual verification, in both directions**: `processNewPunch` and `validatePunch` both originally returned the punch snapshot captured immediately after `computeAndApplyValidation` — before `recalculateIfAccepted` had a chance to run. This meant the HTTP response body could show a stale `isOfficial: false` even when the summary calculator's own later transaction (inside the same request) had actually flipped it to `true`, and separately could omit the `LOCKED_PERIOD_LATE_PUNCH` flag even when `flagLockedPeriodPunch` had actually just added it to the same row. Fixed by having both methods re-fetch the punch's true final state (`refetchPunch`) whenever `recalculateIfAccepted` returned a non-null summary OR reported `skippedLocked: true` — verified live, before and after the fix, that the immediate creation response now matches a subsequent `GET` byte-for-byte.
  - **`ValidatePendingSummary` extended, not renamed**: kept the existing `totalProcessed`/`accepted`/`rejected`/`pending` field names (confirmed via `grep` that `apps/web/app/(app)/attendance-punches/page.tsx` reads exactly these names in a template string) and added two new fields alongside them, `summariesRecalculated`/`skippedLocked`, mirrored in `apps/web/lib/api/attendance-punches.ts`'s `ValidatePendingResult` type — the task's own literal field-naming suggestion (`processed`/`pendingReview`) was not adopted, to avoid breaking the already-working admin web screen.
  - **Manual punch policy unchanged, now explicitly documented**: a manual punch created via `POST /attendance-punches/manual` is routed through `validateManualPunch`, which never produces an `ACCEPTED`-family status (only `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` or `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION`) — the only path a manual punch becomes official is through `AttendanceCorrectionsService.approveCorrection()` (Unit 10/20, unchanged), which creates its own trusted `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` manual punch directly, bypassing this validator entirely.
  - Verified end-to-end against the real `recafco_hrms_dev` database, all 9 lettered manual test cases from the task's own checklist: **A** (clean mobile IN inside an employee-default-inferred geofence → `ACCEPTED`, `isOfficial: true`, summary created with `MISSING_PUNCH`), **B** (clean mobile IN + OUT same day → both `ACCEPTED`, summary `officialInPunchId`/`officialOutPunchId` correct, `totalWorkedMinutes: 540`, `status: PRESENT`), **C** (device-import IN + mixed-source mobile OUT → both accepted, summary correctly spans both sources), **D** (mobile punch ~25.6km outside geofence → `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_LOCATION`, `isOfficial: false`, `OUTSIDE_GEOFENCE` flag, no summary created), **E** (punch from a `BLOCKED` device → `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` with the specific `DEVICE_BLOCKED` flag), **F** (duplicate within the 2-minute window → `REJECTED_DUPLICATE` with `duplicateOfPunchId` set, original punch's `isOfficial`/status confirmed unchanged), **G** (250m GPS accuracy → `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`, no official selection), **H** (clean punch on a `LOCKED` payroll-lock date → raw punch stored with `ACCEPTED` validation status but `isOfficial: false`, `LOCKED_PERIOD_LATE_PUNCH` flag present in the immediate creation response after the staleness fix, no daily summary created), **I** (`validate-pending` batch scoped to the test employee → 3 pending punches processed, correctly remained `pending` since none of their underlying conditions changed, counts `{totalProcessed:3, accepted:0, rejected:0, pending:3, summariesRecalculated:0, skippedLocked:0}` all correct). `pnpm --filter @recafco/api typecheck`/`build`, root `pnpm typecheck`/`build`/`lint` all pass (`@recafco/web`'s `ValidatePendingResult` type change included in the same green build). **All test data was deleted afterward** — confirmed via `psql` that the test work location, employee, user, mobile device, attendance device, both payroll-lock periods, every raw punch/daily summary for the test employee, and their `user_roles`/`refresh_tokens` rows are gone (each delete run as its own transaction after discovering `psql -c` batches multiple `;`-separated statements into one implicit transaction, silently rolling back earlier successful deletes when a later one hit an undisclosed FK — a real gotcha worth remembering for future cleanup passes), and that `MIQ`/`superadmin` (pre-existing, not created by any session) remain the only rows. The already-running API dev server (left over from earlier in this same long session) picked up both rounds of code changes via hot-reload without needing a restart, since no schema/migration change was needed this unit.

- UNIT 19.6 — Mobile Splash Screen and App Branding Polish completed:
  - **Reused the existing logo asset, added nothing new**: `apps/mobile/assets/icon.png` (from the prior, already-committed "Add RECAFCO logo to login screens and Android app icon" commit) is a 3375×3375 square PNG with a white background — already suitable as-is for a native splash image (white bg matches the requested `#FFFFFF` splash background, `contain` sizing needs no distortion). No new `splash-icon.png`/`adaptive-icon.png` files were created, since one asset already correctly serves the app icon, Android adaptive-icon foreground, and now the splash image too.
  - **Installed `expo-splash-screen ~31.0.13`** via `npx expo install` (SDK-54-compatible version resolution, same established pattern as every other Expo package added in this project) and added it to `app.json`'s `plugins` array with `{ image: "./assets/icon.png", imageWidth: 200, resizeMode: "contain", backgroundColor: "#FFFFFF" }`. This is native-splash configuration only — no `SplashScreen.preventAutoHideAsync()`/`hideAsync()` JS calls were added, since the config plugin's default auto-hide-on-first-render behavior is sufficient and adding manual control would be unnecessary complexity for an app with no custom fonts/assets to preload before first paint.
  - **No optional "RECAFCO HRMS" / "Employee Self Service" text was added to the *native* splash** — native Expo splash screens only support a single background color + one centered image, not freeform text, without hand-editing the image asset itself (out of scope, and would risk a distorted/AI-generated-looking result the task explicitly warned against). Instead, that branding text was added to the **JS-rendered** branded loading screen (see below), which is the next thing the user sees after the native splash hides and while the app restores its session — read together, the handoff is a continuous "logo → logo + full branding + spinner" sequence, not a jarring cut.
  - **New `src/components/branded-loading-screen.tsx`**: reuses the same `assets/icon.png`, plus "RECAFCO HRMS" / "Employee Self Service" text, a small spinner, and a "Loading..." label, all on a plain white background using existing `colors` tokens (no new colors introduced). Wired into `(app)/_layout.tsx`'s pre-existing `isLoading` gate (session restoration from SecureStore), replacing a bare `ActivityIndicator`-only view — this was the one "if the app currently shows blank screen while restoring SecureStore/auth" spot in the codebase (it wasn't literally blank, just an unbranded spinner; this unit makes it fully branded per the task's "optional launch loading polish" request without overbuilding a new global loading-state mechanism for what is one call site).
  - **No backend, attendance, or payroll logic was touched** — confirmed by `git status`/`git diff` scoping every change to `apps/mobile` (`app.json`, `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `(app)/_layout.tsx`, and the one new component file); no other app or package changed.
  - **A real environment issue was hit and worked through during verification, unrelated to the product code itself**: the pre-existing Metro/Expo dev server (left running from earlier in this session) died outright partway through this unit's own verification, most likely as a side effect of `expo-splash-screen` being added to `node_modules` while Metro's file-watcher held a now-stale view of the dependency tree — its disk cache also turned out to be corrupted (`Error while reading cache: Unable to deserialize cloned data`), and a **first restart attempt without `--clear` still produced spurious 404s** on every bundle request. The actual root cause the 404s exposed was more fundamental and worth documenting for future units: this pnpm-monorepo's Metro server resolves the HTTP bundle endpoint relative to `watchFolders`' common root (the **workspace root**, since `metro.config.js` sets `watchFolders = [workspaceRoot]`), not `apps/mobile` — so the plain `/index.bundle?platform=...` URL used successfully in every prior mobile unit's own verification actually only ever worked because the *manifest* (`GET /` with a real device/browser user agent) provides the correct, fully-resolved bundle path, e.g. `/node_modules/.pnpm/expo-router@<hash>/node_modules/expo-router/entry.bundle?platform=<platform>&dev=true&hot=false&lazy=true&...` — a path that must be read from the manifest, not assumed, in this monorepo's specific pnpm/Metro configuration. Once a second Metro instance was started cleanly with `--clear` and the manifest-derived path was used, both Android and iOS bundles compiled successfully (`200`, ~7.36–7.37MB, matching prior units' recorded sizes) and were grepped to confirm `BrandedLoadingScreen`/"RECAFCO HRMS"/"Employee Self Service" are present, alongside every pre-existing screen string (Attendance, Workflow, Directory, Corrections, Change Password, Home, Sign In, Device Registration) — confirming zero navigation/feature regression.
  - **Both Metro/Expo instances started by this unit for verification were stopped afterward** (ports 8081 and 8082 confirmed released) — this includes the instance that replaced the pre-existing one that died, since this session was responsible for its lifecycle once it took over verification duty (same reasoning as Unit 18's precedent). **The dev server was not left running after this unit**, unlike some prior mobile units where an already-running instance was found and reused untouched — there is no currently-running mobile dev server as of the end of this unit, which the next unit to touch `apps/mobile` should be aware of.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly (`@recafco/web`/`@recafco/api`/`@recafco/db`/`@recafco/shared` all unaffected — a mobile-only unit). No database rows were created or needed for this unit (pure branding/config, no backend interaction) — no cleanup of test data was required.

- UNIT 19.6.1 — Mobile Splash Screen Visual Refinement completed:
  - **Root cause diagnosed, not guessed**: Unit 19.6 reused `assets/icon.png` directly as the native splash `image` and assumed it had a baked-in white background (it visually renders that way against any white surface). Inspecting the actual pixel/metadata (`sharp(...).metadata()`) showed `hasAlpha: true` and a fully **transparent** corner (`RGBA` corner pixel `[0,0,0,0]`, not opaque white) — a Canva export artifact invisible to the eye but significant to native rendering, since a transparent-background image handed to the Android splash pipeline is exactly the kind of asset that can produce inconsistent results (a white safe-area box around it, or the app's underlying window background showing through) depending on the OS/version. Separately, `app.json` had `userInterfaceStyle: "automatic"`, and since Android 12+'s system-level SplashScreen API resolves its initial background from day/night theme *before* Expo's own configured `backgroundColor` takes over, a device in system dark mode would show a black flash first — matching the reported "dark/black background" symptom. Confirmed via `grep` that the mobile app has **no actual dark-mode implementation anywhere** (no `useColorScheme`/`Appearance` usage, single fixed light palette in `lib/theme.ts`), so `automatic` was providing zero real benefit while causing this exact bug — changed to `"light"` (deterministic, zero regression risk, matches `ui-context.md`'s "light professional default theme, dark mode not required in MVP").
  - **Two new assets generated with `sharp` (already present in `node_modules` via Next.js's image-optimization dependency, no new package installed)**, both derived from the existing source logo, not hand-drawn/AI-generated:
    - `apps/mobile/assets/splash-logo.png` (3031×2357, **no alpha channel** — tightly cropped to the logo's real content bounding box, found by scanning the alpha channel for the first/last non-transparent pixel row/column rather than trusting `sharp`'s built-in `.trim()`, which returned the untrimmed original because it trims by corner-color-proximity, not alpha, and this source's corner is transparent, not a trimmable solid color; then flattened onto fully opaque white). This is the asset actually wired into `expo-splash-screen`'s config as `image`, sized via `imageWidth: 420` (within the requested 360–460px range) with `resizeMode: "contain"` and `backgroundColor: "#FFFFFF"` — confirmed by reading the plugin's own source (`withSplashScreen.js`) that `imageWidth`/`resizeMode` size the provided image directly on a natively-generated solid-color canvas at actual device resolution; the plugin does **not** pre-composite a background around the file itself. This is why a tightly-cropped, predictably-sized logo (not a pre-baked fixed-canvas image) is the technically correct asset for this specific mechanism, and why it robustly adapts to any real device screen size/aspect ratio rather than being cropped or stretched.
    - `apps/mobile/assets/splash-screen.png` (1242×2436, the task's suggested tall-canvas reference size) — a full pre-composed preview (opaque white canvas, `splash-logo.png` resized to 420px wide and centered) kept as a visual reference/documentation of the intended design, matching the task's explicit request for this exact file, but **not** wired into any config — see above for why `splash-logo.png` + the plugin's own sizing is the more robust mechanism for the actual native splash.
  - **`app.json`**: `userInterfaceStyle` → `"light"`; `expo-splash-screen` plugin config `image` → `./assets/splash-logo.png`, `imageWidth` → `420` (was `./assets/icon.png` at `200`). `backgroundColor: "#FFFFFF"` and `resizeMode: "contain"` were already correct from Unit 19.6 and left unchanged. The app icon (`icon` field) and Android adaptive icon (`android.adaptiveIcon`) were **not touched** — out of this unit's explicit scope, and the task said not to break them unless needed; they weren't the reported problem.
  - **`BrandedLoadingScreen` updated to match**: now uses `assets/splash-logo.png` (was `assets/icon.png`) at a `180×140` box (matching the logo's real ~1.29:1 aspect ratio, so it renders at its natural proportions rather than being squeezed into a square) so the native-splash-to-JS-loading-screen handoff reads as one consistent visual weight, not a size jump. Added the task's requested optional tagline, "Secure Attendance & HR Platform", as a new small `tagline` text style between the existing subtitle and spinner — reused the existing `colors.textMuted` token, no new colors introduced. White background, centered logo, and the spinner/"Loading..." label were already correct from Unit 19.6 and are unchanged; no animation was added, per the task's "avoid heavy animation" instruction.
  - **No backend, attendance, payroll, or navigation logic was touched** — confirmed by `git status`/`git diff` scoping every change to `apps/mobile/app.json`, `apps/mobile/src/components/branded-loading-screen.tsx`, and the two new/one reference PNG asset files; no other file in any app or package changed.
  - **No emulator/physical device was available in this environment** (`adb devices` returned an empty list) — disclosed rather than silently skipped, matching this unit's own explicit fallback instructions. Verified instead via: (1) `expo config --type public`, confirming the resolved config actually reflects `userInterfaceStyle: 'light'` and the new `splash-logo.png`/`420` values, not just the source `app.json` text; (2) starting a fresh Metro instance (`expo start --clear`) and compiling **both** the Android and iOS bundles via their manifest-derived URLs (same documented monorepo Metro gotcha as Unit 19.6 — the bundle path must be read from the `Expo-Platform` manifest response, not assumed as `/index.bundle`), both returning `200` at ~7.37MB (Android) / ~7.37MB (iOS), in the same size range as every prior mobile unit, indicating no bloat/breakage; (3) grepping both compiled bundles and confirming `"Secure Attendance & HR Platform"` and a `splash-logo` asset reference are present, alongside every pre-existing screen string (Attendance, Workflow, Directory, Corrections, Change Password, Sign In, Device Registration) to confirm zero navigation/feature regression; (4) reading the two new PNG files directly to visually confirm: pure white background, logo clearly centered, not pixelated/stretched, no black background, no visible square border artifact. The Metro instance started for this verification was stopped afterward (port 8081 confirmed released) — no mobile dev server was left running.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly. No database interaction, no test data to clean up (pure asset/config unit).
  - **Limitation**: the visual fix is verified via config resolution, compiled-bundle content, and direct inspection of the generated image files — not via an actual rendered screenshot on a device/emulator, since none was available. The specific "white square on dark background" symptom is diagnosed with concrete evidence (transparent corner pixel, `automatic` interface style + no dark-mode implementation) and directly addressed, but a real-device click-through to see the native splash render is still recommended before considering this fully closed — flagged as an open item below, consistent with the existing open item about the broader lack of any device/emulator verification pass across all mobile units.

- UNIT 19.6.2 — Mobile App Icon Visual Refinement completed:
  - **Root cause diagnosed by measuring actual pixel content, not guessed**: the pre-existing `apps/mobile/assets/icon.png` (3375×3375) had its logo content occupying ~90% of canvas width with only ~5% padding per side (measured via a numpy alpha/color scan for the tight non-white bounding box: content spanned x=177–3207, y=695–3051 of the 3375px canvas). That file was also reused directly as `android.adaptiveIcon.foregroundImage`. Android adaptive icons only guarantee the inner ~66% of the foreground is visible across circle/squircle/rounded-square launcher masks — a logo filling 90% of the canvas gets its left/right edges (parts of the Arabic wordmark and the "RECAFCO" text) clipped by every non-square mask, which is exactly the reported cropping.
  - **Two new assets generated from the existing logo artwork, not redrawn/AI-generated**: the tight logo content was cropped out of the existing `icon.png` at its real bounding box (via a numpy scan for non-white/non-transparent pixels, same technique as Unit 19.6.1's `splash-logo.png` extraction), then recomposited onto a fresh opaque white `1024×1024` canvas at two different scales:
    - `apps/mobile/assets/icon.png` (replaced in place) — logo scaled to ~65% of canvas width, centered, generous even padding on all sides. This is the asset used for the main app icon (`expo.icon`, which Expo also applies as the iOS icon).
    - `apps/mobile/assets/adaptive-icon.png` (new file) — logo scaled to ~46% of canvas width, centered, with deliberately more padding than `icon.png` per the task's explicit instruction, keeping the entire logo safely inside Android's ~66% adaptive-icon safe zone with margin to spare under any launcher mask shape.
  - **`app.json`**: `android.adaptiveIcon.foregroundImage` changed from `./assets/icon.png` to `./assets/adaptive-icon.png`. `expo.icon` (`./assets/icon.png`) and `android.adaptiveIcon.backgroundColor` (`#FFFFFF`, already correct) were left unchanged. Splash screen config (`splash-logo.png`/`splash-screen.png` from Unit 19.6.1) was not touched — no overlap required it.
  - **No backend, attendance, payroll, or navigation logic was touched** — confirmed by `git status`/`git diff` scoping every change to `apps/mobile/app.json` plus the two icon PNGs; no other file in any app or package changed.
  - **No emulator/physical device was available in this environment** (consistent with every prior mobile unit) — disclosed rather than silently skipped. Verified instead via: (1) directly reading both generated 1024×1024 PNGs to visually confirm the full logo (Arabic wordmark, "RECAFCO", wing mark, "Since 1976") renders intact with clear padding, not cropped; (2) generating circle, squircle, and rounded-square mask previews of both `icon.png` and `adaptive-icon.png` and visually confirming the complete logo stays inside every mask shape with margin to spare; (3) `npx expo config --type public` from `apps/mobile`, confirming the resolved config reflects the new `adaptive-icon.png` path, not just the source `app.json` text.

No mobile `build` script exists in this monorepo's `build` pipeline (only `@recafco/web`/`api`/`db`/`shared` ran under root `pnpm build`) — consistent with every prior mobile unit, not a regression introduced here. This is now the single source used for both the native splash and the new `LogoBadge` component (below); `splash-logo.png`/`splash-screen.png` from Unit 19.6.1 are superseded and left in place unreferenced (harmless — Metro only bundles what's actually `require()`'d — kept as historical reference rather than deleted, since deleting wasn't asked for and the task's own instructions favored caution around existing assets).

  - **No backend, attendance, payroll, auth, or navigation logic was touched** — confirmed by `git status`/`git diff` scoping every change to `apps/mobile/app.json`, `apps/mobile/app/login.tsx`, `apps/mobile/src/components/branded-loading-screen.tsx`, the new `apps/mobile/src/components/logo-badge.tsx`, and the one new PNG asset; no other file in any app or package changed. `login.tsx`'s `onSubmit`/`useAuth()`/`useForm` logic is byte-identical to before this unit.
  - **No emulator/physical device was available in this environment** (consistent with every prior mobile unit) — disclosed per the task's own fallback instructions, not silently skipped. Verified instead via: (1) `expo config --type public`, confirming the resolved config reflects the new splash `image` path while `icon`/`adaptiveIcon` stay unchanged; (2) starting a fresh Metro instance (`expo start --clear`, port 8081 confirmed free beforehand so no unrelated process was at risk) and compiling **both** Android and iOS bundles via their manifest-derived URLs, both `200 OK` at ~7.37MB, matching every prior mobile unit's size range; (3) grepping both bundles for `"Secure Attendance & HR Platform"`, `recafco-logo-transparent`, and `"Sign in to continue"` (all present) alongside every pre-existing screen string (Attendance, Workflow, Directory, Corrections, Change Password, Sign In, Device Registration — zero regression); (4) reading `recafco-logo-transparent.png` directly to visually confirm the full, undistorted logo; (5) **generating a rendered mock-up preview** of the actual `LogoBadge` output (white rounded card, soft shadow, full logo, on the `colors.bgBase` page background) via a `sharp`+SVG composite matching the component's real dimensions/radius/padding math, confirmed visually to show a clean rounded card with no hard edge, no circular crop, and no white-square artifact. The Metro instance started for this verification was stopped afterward (port 8081 confirmed released); no other Node process on this machine was touched.
  - Verified end-to-end: `pnpm --filter @recafco/mobile typecheck`/`lint` and root `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass cleanly. No database interaction, no test data to clean up (pure asset/component unit).
  - **Limitation**: as with Units 19.6.1/19.6.2, this is evidence-based verification (transparency confirmed at the pixel level, badge geometry confirmed via a matching rendered mock-up, bundle content confirmed via grep) rather than an actual on-device render — a real phone/emulator pass (ideally after an uninstall/reinstall to clear any cached splash/icon, per the task's own Task 8 instruction) remains the standing recommendation before calling the visual polish fully closed.

## Open Questions

### Company Policy Questions

- What is the official company timezone for attendance calculation?
- What is the standard work week and weekend policy?
- What are the shift types currently used?
- Are night shifts required?
- How should attendance date be calculated for overnight shifts?
- What are the late grace rules?
- What are the early leave rules?
- How is overtime calculated?
- Is break time tracked?
- Are lunch breaks auto-deducted or punched?
- What is the allowed radius for each office/site?
- Which employees are allowed to use mobile punching?
- Which employees must use biometric/device punching only?
- Are mobile punches allowed from all locations or only selected locations?
- Is selfie required for all mobile punches or only project sites?
- Is Wi-Fi validation required in office/factory?
- Is QR/site code required for project sites?
- What is the approval chain for missed punch correction?
- Who can approve payroll-locked corrections?
- How long should raw punch records be retained?
- What is the current payroll export format?

### Unit 1 (Database Schema) Open Questions

- `WorkLocation.timezone` defaults to `"UTC"` as a neutral placeholder until company timezone policy is confirmed (see company policy questions above). Attendance-date calculation logic must not hardcode a timezone until this is resolved.
- `Shift` start/end are stored as `startTimeMinutes`/`endTimeMinutes` (minutes from midnight) plus `isOvernight`, `gracePeriodMinutes`, `breakMinutes` rather than fixed business rules, since grace/overtime/break policy is still an open company-policy question. The validation/summary engine must treat these as configurable inputs, not hardcode behavior.
- `AttendanceCorrection.requestedByUserId`, `approvedByUserId`, and `AuditLog.actorUserId` are plain string fields (not foreign keys) because the User/auth model does not exist yet (planned for the auth/RBAC foundation unit). These should become real relations once that model lands — decide then whether to backfill a migration or leave as loose references.
- `AttendancePayrollLock` is modeled as a single global period lock (`periodStart`/`periodEnd`, no location/department scoping). Confirm this matches actual payroll closing policy (company-wide close vs. per-site close) before the monthly closing unit is built.
- Packages/shared's `AttendanceSourceType` and `PunchValidationStatus` enums had drifted from the source/status lists in `architecture.md` (e.g. `MANUAL_HR`/`SUPERVISOR_CORRECTION` instead of `MANUAL_CORRECTION`/`IMPORT`/`FUTURE_INTEGRATION`; missing `REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION`). Corrected in this unit to match `architecture.md` since no other code referenced the old values yet — confirm no other undocumented consumer expected the old names.

### Unit 2 (NestJS API Foundation) Open Questions

- `packages/shared` still has the same `"type": "module"` + raw-`.ts`-`main` packaging as `@recafco/db` had before this unit's fix. It isn't consumed by `apps/api` yet, so it was left alone, but it must get the same CommonJS-build treatment (`tsconfig.build.json`, compiled `dist/` output, `main`/`types` pointing at `dist`) before Unit 3 (Auth/RBAC) or any other CommonJS app tries to `import` it — otherwise it will hit the exact same `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` failure `@recafco/db` did.
- Env validation (`apps/api/src/config/env.schema.ts`) is a small local `zod` schema inside `apps/api`, not a shared `packages/config` module, even though `architecture.md` describes `packages/config` as eventually owning "Environment variable schema helpers." Revisit once `apps/web` or `apps/mobile` also need typed env validation — extract a shared helper then rather than guessing its shape now.
- `apps/api/nest-cli.json` has `"deleteOutDir": false` (see Unit 2 note above on the watch-mode race this fixed). If a future change re-enables it for a "clean build" reason, retest `nest start --watch` from a cold `dist/`-less state before relying on it, since a clean rebuild reproduced the crash (this wasn't just a stale-cache fluke).
- CORS is currently hardcoded to `http://localhost:3000` and `http://localhost:19006` in `main.ts`. This will need to become environment-driven (or otherwise configurable) once a real staging/production web origin exists — do not just widen it to a wildcard.

### Unit 3 (Auth/RBAC Foundation) Open Questions

- **The role → permission mapping in `apps/api/src/auth/constants/rbac.constants.ts` is a provisional default**, not a confirmed HR/security policy decision. In particular: should `HR_ADMIN` really lack `attendance.payroll.lock`/`unlock` (currently only `HR_MANAGER`+ has it)? Should `SUPERVISOR` really have `attendance.correction.approve`, or should first-level approval sit only with `DEPARTMENT_MANAGER`? Confirm against the real approval chain before this becomes load-bearing for actual payroll decisions.
- `AttendancePayrollLock.lockedByUserId`/`reopenedByUserId` were **not** converted to `User` relations in this unit (only the 3 fields the task explicitly named were). Revisit alongside the monthly payroll closing unit — converting them then is low-risk since that table is still empty.
- `packages/shared`'s `UserRole` enum (`packages/shared/src/enums/roles.ts`) already lists the same 9 role codes used here, but Auth code does **not** import `@recafco/shared` — the codes were duplicated as plain string literals in `rbac.constants.ts` instead, specifically to avoid triggering the same raw-`.ts`-`main`/`"type":"module"` packaging problem in a second package (see Unit 2's open question, still unresolved for `packages/shared`). Once `packages/shared` gets the same CommonJS-build treatment `@recafco/db` got, consider having `SYSTEM_ROLES` derive from `UserRole` instead of duplicating the list.
- No rate-limiting/throttling was added to `/auth/login` (or any endpoint) in this unit, even though `code-standards.md` calls for rate-limiting sensitive endpoints. The account-lockout mechanism (5 attempts / 15 min) covers targeted brute force against one account, but not distributed/low-and-slow attempts across many accounts or plain request flooding. Deliberately deferred rather than bolting on a throttler module mid-unit — flag as a small, separate follow-up unit (likely once Redis is introduced, per the roadmap, for a shared rate-limit store across multiple API instances).
- `User.username` is required + unique; `email` is optional + unique. Confirm this matches how HR actually provisions accounts (e.g., is every internal user guaranteed a username at creation time, or should email-first accounts be supported too?).
- The bootstrap script's 12-character minimum password check is a hardcoded constant (`MIN_BOOTSTRAP_PASSWORD_LENGTH`), not a configurable password policy. A real password-policy module (composition rules, rotation, etc.) is out of scope for this foundation unit but will be needed before HR/admin-facing user-creation endpoints ship.
- `GET /auth/me`'s `status`/`email`/`employeeId` fields are typed loosely (`string`/`string | null`) in `AuthService.getProfile`'s return type rather than reusing Prisma's generated `UserStatus` enum type — a minor looseness introduced to keep the method's return type independent of needing to import the enum; tighten if this becomes a shared DTO in Unit 4+.

### Unit 4 (Master Data API Foundation) Open Questions

- **Deactivating a department/designation/work location does not check whether any active employee still references it.** An HR admin can set a department `isActive: false` while employees are still assigned to it — the FK reference stays valid (rows are never deleted), so nothing breaks, but it may be surprising. No guard was added for this foundation unit (would need a policy decision: block deactivation, warn, or allow silently) — flag before this becomes a real HR workflow, not just an API.
- **The role → permission additions for the 13 new master-data permissions are provisional**, same caveat as Unit 3's original mapping: is `HR_ADMIN` really meant to lack `departments.disable`/`designations.disable`/`workLocations.disable` (reserved for `HR_MANAGER` here, mirroring the existing `users.disable` pattern)? Should `PAYROLL_OFFICER`/`AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER` really get blanket read access to all three master-data types? Confirm against real HR policy.
- **`architecture.md`'s "Suggested Permission Groups" list was already out of sync with the actually-implemented permission codes before this unit** (e.g. it lists `attendance.correct.approve.manager`/`.hr` where the code uses `attendance.correction.approve`; it has no `users.*` or `employees.disable` entries at all). This unit only appended the new department/designation/workLocation/employees.disable codes rather than reconciling the whole list — a full audit of `architecture.md`'s permission list against `rbac.constants.ts` is still owed.
- **`packages/shared`'s raw-`.ts`-`main` packaging problem (flagged in Units 2 and 3) is still unresolved** — this unit again avoided importing `@recafco/shared` from `apps/api` (e.g. could have reused its `EmployeeStatus`/`EmploymentType` enum shapes for DTOs, but didn't) to avoid re-triggering it. Fix it once, then both this and Unit 3's `SYSTEM_ROLES` duplication can be cleaned up together.
- **The `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` + Prisma "checked vs. unchecked" input-type ambiguity is now a recurring pattern** (hit in `AuditService` in Unit 3, and twice in `EmployeesService` in this unit) whenever a `create`/`update` call mixes scalar foreign-key fields (e.g. `departmentId`) with plain field assignments sourced from an optional-field DTO. The fix each time was the same: build an explicitly-typed intermediate `const data: Prisma.XUncheckedCreateInput = {...}` (or `UncheckedUpdateInput`) object, using `?? null` for nullable string fields and an `if (x !== undefined) data.y = x` guard for fields that have a schema default but aren't nullable. Worth extracting into a documented convention (or a small codegen/lint rule) once a 4th or 5th service hits it — not urgent enough to abstract yet.
- **Prisma's non-interactive `migrate dev` refusal (see Unit 4 completed-work note above) will recur** any time a schema change would normally trigger a warning prompt (new unique constraint on a column that could theoretically have duplicates, some column-type narrowing, etc.), even against a database that is provably empty. The `migrate diff --from-url ... --script` → hand-built migration folder → `migrate deploy` sequence is the known-working non-interactive path; consider documenting it as a standing runbook step rather than rediscovering it per-incident.
- **`WorkLocation.geofenceRadiusMeters`'s new upper bound (5000m) is a data-hygiene sanity ceiling, not a policy decision** — the real per-site-type allowed-radius policy (office vs. factory vs. project site) is still an open company-policy question from Unit 1, unchanged by this unit.

### Unit 5 (Attendance Device Registry) Open Questions

- **`QR_TERMINAL`/`TURNSTILE`/`OTHER` all map to `AttendanceSourceType.FUTURE_INTEGRATION`** since no dedicated raw-punch source type exists for them yet. Once the attendance validation engine is built and actually needs to treat these device classes differently from each other (or from truly-unplanned future integrations), `AttendanceSourceType` will need new values and this mapping will need revisiting — right now it's a placeholder that only matters because `sourceType` is a pre-existing required column, not because any code reads it yet.
- **The permission table for this unit deliberately inverts the Unit 3/4 "disable is elevated to HR_MANAGER" pattern** — here `HR_ADMIN` gets `attendanceDevices.disable` directly and `HR_MANAGER` only gets read. This was an explicit instruction in this unit's own task (not a judgment call), but it means the permission model now has two different conventions for who holds "elevated" actions depending on the resource. Worth reconciling into one consistent rule once real RBAC policy is confirmed (see Unit 3's open question).
- **`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` and `SUPERVISOR` have zero attendance-device visibility**, per this unit's explicit "if in doubt, keep supervisor access out" instruction and department manager simply not being mentioned in the suggested table. Revisit if a manager-facing "which devices are at my site" view is ever needed — it would need a new, deliberately-granted permission, not just widening `attendanceDevices.read`.
- **The IPv4-or-hostname regex for `ipAddress` is a pragmatic combined pattern**, not `class-validator`'s built-in `@IsIP`/`@IsFQDN` — chosen because `@IsFQDN` rejects bare LAN hostnames without a TLD (e.g. `biometric-hq`), which is a realistic on-prem naming pattern this task's "hostname if feasible" wording anticipated. If a stricter DNS-name validation is later needed, revisit as a deliberate change, not a silent tightening.
- **`AttendanceDevicesModule`'s `assertActiveWorkLocation` is stricter than `EmployeesService`'s FK check** (existence *and* `isActive`, not just existence) per this task's explicit wording. Consider whether `EmployeesModule`'s department/designation/workLocation FK checks (Unit 4) should be tightened to match, or whether employees are meant to be assignable to an inactive location/department/designation (e.g., during a wind-down) — currently inconsistent across the two units and worth a deliberate policy decision either way.
- **Same Prisma non-interactive `migrate dev` refusal recurred a third time** (Units 4 and 5), confirming it as a standing pattern rather than a one-off: any new unique constraint (even on a provably-empty table) triggers it. The `migrate diff --script` → hand-built migration folder → `migrate deploy` workaround is now used three times without needing adjustment — still worth turning into an actual documented runbook step (flagged in Unit 4's open questions too) rather than re-discovering the reasoning each time.

### Unit 6 (Mobile Device Binding) Open Questions

- **No admin "create user" endpoint exists yet** — the only way to link a `User` to an `Employee` right now is the `create:super-admin` bootstrap script or a hand-rolled throwaway script (used twice now, in this unit's manual verification, to create test employee-linked users). This will need a real endpoint before HR can actually onboard employees with mobile-punch access — likely belongs in a future Auth/User-management extension unit, not this one.
- **Register does a full replace of optional metadata fields on refresh, not a merge** — re-registering an already-known `deviceIdentifier` with some fields omitted will null out those fields rather than preserving the previous values. This was a deliberate choice (a real mobile client always resends its complete current state on each registration call), but it means a hand-crafted/partial API call (as opposed to real mobile app traffic) can accidentally wipe metadata. Revisit if a partial-update semantic turns out to be needed for some client.
- **`osName` and `isActive` were dropped outright** (not kept alongside the new `platform`/`status` fields, unlike Unit 5's `deviceType`/`sourceType` split) since neither had any established consumer anywhere in the codebase. If some other undocumented process depended on those exact column names, this would be a breaking change — low risk since the table was provably empty and no other code referenced them, but worth flagging since this unit didn't have the luxury of an `architecture.md` cross-reference to confirm it the way `sourceType` did in Unit 5.
- **Approving a second primary device only flips `isPrimary` on the previous one, never its `status`.** This means an employee can accumulate multiple `APPROVED` (but non-primary) devices over time if HR keeps approving new ones as primary without ever blocking/resetting the old ones — none of them are "trusted" for attendance except the current primary (once punch logic exists and checks both `status` AND `isPrimary`), but they remain valid, approved rows indefinitely. Whether old non-primary approved devices should be auto-expired/reset after some period, or left for HR to clean up manually, is a policy question for whenever the actual punch-validation engine is built.
- **`mobileDevices.read` is deliberately withheld from `EMPLOYEE`** (only `.register` is granted) — an employee's own-device visibility comes entirely from the JWT-only `/me` endpoint, not from the general permission. If a future requirement needs employees to see richer device data than `/me` currently returns (which is just the raw `MobileDevice` rows, no relations), extend `/me`'s response shape rather than granting the general `.read` permission, which would also unlock the cross-employee admin list endpoint.
- **The `EMPLOYEE` role's employment/account status isn't checked during registration** (e.g., a `TERMINATED` employee whose `User` account is somehow still active could still register a device) — this unit only checks that a `User.employeeId` exists, not the linked employee's `status` or the user's own `UserStatus`. Not explicitly required by this task, but worth tightening once real account-deprovisioning workflows exist (a terminated employee's `User` should presumably be disabled at that point anyway, which `AuthService.login` already blocks on — so this may already be covered end-to-end via login, just not defense-in-depth at the device-registration layer itself).

### Unit 7 (Attendance Raw Punch Foundation) Open Questions

- **`attendancePunches.read`/`.readOwn` now sit alongside Unit 3's pre-existing `attendance.punch.read`**, a differently-named permission covering essentially the same concept (dot-separated singular vs. this unit's camelCase-plural convention, matching Units 5/6's naming style instead). Neither the old nor the new permission was removed or aliased — `HR_MANAGER`/`SUPERVISOR`/`DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` etc. still hold the old `attendance.punch.read` from Unit 3's original mapping, now redundant with the new `attendancePunches.read` for roles that got both. This wasn't reconciled in this unit (task didn't ask for it, and unifying them unprompted risks disturbing already-verified role assignments) — a full permission-naming audit across all units is still owed, as flagged repeatedly since Unit 4.
- **`SUPERVISOR` and `DEPARTMENT_MANAGER` were given none of the 5 new permissions**, per the task's own "read if needed"/"otherwise none" hedge for both roles. Per `project-overview.md`, supervisors are meant to review "suspicious punches in their team," which would need `attendancePunches.read` scoped to their own team/department — not currently modeled (no manager-employee hierarchy exists yet) and not something this foundation unit could safely half-build. Revisit once a team/reporting-line concept exists.
- **`REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` is overloaded to mean three distinct things** for mobile punches (device never registered / device registered to a different employee / device registered but not yet `APPROVED`) — there's no more specific enum value for any of these in the current `PunchValidationStatus` set, so `fraudFlags` is what actually distinguishes them (`DEVICE_NOT_REGISTERED`/`DEVICE_BELONGS_TO_DIFFERENT_EMPLOYEE`/`DEVICE_NOT_APPROVED`). Whoever builds Unit 8's validation engine needs to read `fraudFlags`, not just `validationStatus`, to tell these apart — worth a dedicated enum value split if that distinction becomes operationally important.
- **No employee-status check on any of the three intake paths** (mobile, device-import, manual) — a `TERMINATED`/`RESIGNED` employee can still have raw punches created for them (mobile self-submission would additionally require their `User` account to still be active/logged-in, which is a real barrier in practice, but device-import and manual entry have no such check at all). This is intentional: `PunchValidationStatus` already has a dedicated `REJECTED_EMPLOYEE_INACTIVE` value reserved from Unit 1 specifically for this check, signaling it was always meant to live in the *validation engine* (Unit 8), not the raw intake layer — raw punches are meant to be stored even for edge cases like this, then filtered out at validation time, not rejected at the door.
- **Device-import's `sourceDeviceId` field is left `null`** — the task's device-import DTO doesn't ask for a client-supplied raw device-reported identifier distinct from our own `attendanceDeviceId`, so nothing populates it. If real device payloads carry their own internal log/device reference worth preserving separately, add it explicitly rather than leaving the column's purpose ambiguous.
- **`distanceFromLocationMeters` and `duplicateOfPunchId` are always `null` at intake** across all three paths — both are explicitly geofence/dedup-engine concerns (Unit 8+), not raw-intake concerns, consistent with `architecture.md`'s layering (Layer 1 raw intake vs. Layer 2 validation).

### Unit 8 (Attendance Basic Validation Engine) Open Questions

- **The 2-minute duplicate-detection time window and the 50-meter poor-GPS-accuracy threshold are both engineering defaults chosen in this unit, not confirmed company policy.** Neither was specified numerically by the task. Revisit both once real device/mobile behavior data exists (e.g., how long does a real duplicate resend actually take; what GPS accuracy do real employee phones typically report at a genuine job site).
- **`REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` is now reused a third context** (device-import's "device inactive/missing" case, alongside Unit 7's three mobile-intake meanings) — still no dedicated enum value exists for "device found but currently inactive" vs. "device never registered at all." `fraudFlags` is what actually distinguishes all of these; whoever eventually revisits `PunchValidationStatus`'s granularity (flagged since Unit 7) should account for this unit's additional overload too.
- **`REJECTED_POLICY_VIOLATION` is now used for three distinct, unrelated conditions**: an inactive work location (mobile and device-import paths), an employee whose `isMobilePunchAllowed`/`isDeviceOnly` flags block mobile punching, and a manual punch missing its `reason` (defensive, effectively unreachable). All three are genuinely "policy," not fraud, but conflating them under one enum value means `fraudFlags` is again the only way to tell them apart. Flag alongside the `REJECTED_UNREGISTERED_DEVICE` overload question above for a future enum-granularity pass.
- **Checking `Employee.isMobilePunchAllowed`/`isDeviceOnly` during mobile validation was not explicitly requested by this unit's task text** — added because both fields already existed on the schema since Unit 1 with zero consumers anywhere, and a validation engine is the natural place to enforce them. If this turns out to be premature (e.g., HR wants a softer "warn, don't reject" behavior for these flags), it's an easy, isolated rule to soften or remove.
- **`fraudScore`/`fraudFlags` are fully recomputed (not accumulated) on every validation run** — a deliberate idempotency choice (see Completed notes above), but it does mean the *history* of how a score changed across multiple validation runs isn't preserved anywhere except by manually diffing `AuditLog` before/after snapshots. If a future requirement needs "show me how this punch's fraud assessment evolved over time" as a first-class view (not just an audit-log spelunking exercise), that would need a deliberate design change, not just reading current `fraudScore`.
- **No background scheduler re-runs `validate-pending` automatically** — exactly as scoped ("no background queue" was an explicit exclusion). Every punch that lands in a pending/rejected state stays there until an HR/admin user calls one of the two new endpoints by hand. A real operational cadence (e.g., "run validate-pending every N minutes via BullMQ") is a natural, still-unbuilt follow-up once Redis/BullMQ is introduced per the roadmap.
- **Poor GPS accuracy resolves to `PENDING_HR_REVIEW`, not `ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING`** — the more conservative of the two options the task explicitly offered. If this produces too high a volume of HR-review-queue punches in practice (e.g., if many real employee phones commonly report >50m accuracy indoors), reconsider either the threshold or the resulting status as a deliberate policy tuning, not a silent loosening.

### Unit 9 (Daily Attendance Summary Calculator) Open Questions

- **Manual (`MANUAL_CORRECTION`) punches are never eligible for official IN/OUT selection in this unit** — they can only ever be `PENDING_MANAGER_APPROVAL` (or rejected) per Unit 8, and the eligible-status filter (`ACCEPTED`/`ACCEPTED_WITH_WARNING` only) excludes every pending state by construction. This is correct *for now*, but means a legitimate manual correction currently has no path to ever become an official punch — Unit 10's correction-approval workflow needs to either (a) promote an approved manual punch's `validationStatus` to something eligible, or (b) give the summary calculator a second, explicit "approved correction" input distinct from `validationStatus`. This must be resolved as part of Unit 10, not deferred further.
- **The Kuwait-fixed-UTC+3-offset timezone approach is correct today but is a single hardcoded assumption** — if RECAFCO ever operates a site in a different timezone, or Kuwait's DST policy ever changes, `kuwait-date.util.ts` would need to become a real per-work-location (or per-company-config) timezone lookup using an IANA tz library, not just a renamed constant. Flagged explicitly since this is exactly the kind of "implement configurable policy instead of hardcoding" case `ai-workflow-rules.md` warns about — currently accepted as scoped, since the task explicitly named Asia/Kuwait as the company timezone.
- **`recalculate-range`'s candidate-day discovery loads all matching raw punches into memory and groups them in application code**, rather than a database-side grouped query — acceptable for this foundation unit given the explicit ≤100-employee-day cap and that this is an occasional HR-triggered action, not the punch hot path, but it will not scale to a full-company, multi-month recalculation sweep. A production-scale version needs either a raw SQL query that applies the Kuwait offset before grouping, or to move this into a BullMQ background job once Redis is wired up (per the roadmap) — do not extend the current in-memory approach to remove the 100-day cap without addressing this first.
- **No admin API exists yet to create/manage `Shift`/`ShiftAssignment` rows** (per `ai-workflow-rules.md`'s own unit ordering, a shift service/API layer is a separate, later unit) — this unit's shift-lookup logic was verified manually by inserting a `Shift`/`ShiftAssignment` row directly via `psql`, which is fine for this foundation unit's scope but means there is currently no legitimate way for HR to actually assign shifts through the API. Prioritize the shift API unit before shift-aware late/overtime becomes something real HR/payroll workflows can rely on operationally.
- **`totalWorkedMinutes` is clamped to a minimum of `0`** when the selected OUT is chronologically before the selected IN (a data anomaly that could arise from unusual duplicate-eligible punch patterns) rather than surfacing this as a distinct error/warning state. If this scenario turns out to matter operationally (e.g., it should flag the day as requiring correction review rather than silently reporting zero), that would need a deliberate new status value or a dedicated "anomalous" flag — not currently modeled.
- **`attendanceSummaries.*` is a fourth permission naming scheme covering ground already touched by Unit 3's `attendance.read`** — same unreconciled duplication pattern flagged since Unit 7 (`attendancePunches.*` vs. `attendance.punch.read`). A full permission-naming audit across all units (Unit 4's open question, still owed) should address all of these together rather than one at a time.

### Unit 10 (Attendance Correction Workflow) Open Questions

- **Whether HR/Admin should be allowed to file or approve corrections for a terminated/inactive employee is still unresolved** — this unit requires `EmployeeStatus.ACTIVE` uniformly for both the self-service and admin-request paths, per the task's own "for now, require active unless context says otherwise" default. A real HR workflow will likely need to correct a just-terminated employee's final attendance before their last payroll run — revisit as a deliberate policy decision, not by quietly loosening the check.
- **Approval is not a single atomic transaction end-to-end** — the manual-punch-create + correction-status-update happen together in one `$transaction`, but the subsequent summary recalculation is a separate, sequential call into `AttendanceSummaryCalculatorService.calculateForEmployeeDate` (which manages its own transaction). A crash between these two steps would leave an approved correction with a real manual punch but a stale (not-yet-recalculated) summary — a bounded, recoverable inconsistency (the summary can always be recalculated again via `POST /attendance-summaries/calculate` or `/recalculate-range`), not silent data corruption, but not perfectly atomic either. Made this way because `architecture.md`'s invariant #13 and `code-standards.md` both require recalculation to happen "through one service/function" — folding the calculator into the correction's own transaction would mean either duplicating its selection logic (forbidden) or refactoring it to accept an injected transaction client (out of scope for this unit). Revisit only if a real production incident demonstrates the gap matters in practice.
- **A real permission-escalation bug was caught and fixed live in this unit** (see Completed notes) — `admin-request` was initially reachable by any `EMPLOYEE`-role user with an arbitrary target `employeeId`, because it shared a permission with the self-service endpoint. Worth treating as a standing lesson: any future "admin does X on behalf of employeeId" endpoint must be checked against exactly which roles hold whatever permission gates it, not just which permission "sounds right" from the task text — test it adversarially (as a low-privilege role), not just as super admin, before considering an endpoint done.
- **`attendanceCorrections.*` is a fifth permission naming scheme covering ground already touched by Unit 3's pre-existing `attendance.correction.request`/`.approve`** — same unreconciled duplication pattern flagged since Unit 7 (now: `attendancePunches.*`, `attendanceSummaries.*`, `attendanceCorrections.*` all duplicating older dot-separated singular permissions). The full permission-naming audit flagged repeatedly since Unit 4 is increasingly overdue — recommend prioritizing it before Unit 11's web frontend needs to consume/display permission-gated UI, since the frontend will otherwise have to learn about both naming schemes per resource.
- **`targetPunchId` is optional even for `WRONG_IN`/`WRONG_OUT`'s underlying business intent of "correct this specific existing punch"** — the DTO requires it for those two types, but nothing stops HR from approving a `WRONG_IN` correction where the "wrong" original punch was itself later deleted... except raw punches are never deleted, so this specific concern doesn't actually arise. Kept as a note only because `targetPunchId`'s FK is `onDelete: SetNull`, which would silently null the reference if raw-punch deletion ever became possible in the future — a reminder that the immutability invariant is load-bearing for more than just audit history.

### Unit 11 (Next.js Web Frontend Foundation) Open Questions

- **Token storage is localStorage for both access and refresh tokens — a deliberate, documented foundation-unit tradeoff, not a final security posture.** The task itself required this to be flagged: before this system holds real employee data (or before any external/less-trusted network exposure), move to httpOnly cookies (or another approach resistant to XSS-based token theft) for at least the refresh token, ideally both. This is the single most important item to revisit before any production-facing deployment.
- **No silent refresh-on-401/expiry flow exists yet** — the access token lasts 15 minutes (per Unit 3's default), and this foundation only checks `/auth/me` once on mount; once it expires mid-session, the *next* explicit action that hits a protected backend endpoint will simply fail, not silently refresh and retry. A natural follow-up (possibly bundled with the first unit that adds real data-fetching via `@tanstack/react-query`) is a query-client-level 401 interceptor that attempts `POST /auth/refresh` once before giving up and redirecting to `/login`.
- **Full interactive browser verification was not performed in this unit** — no browser-automation tool was available in this environment. What was verified: backend contract correctness (login/me/refresh/logout, invalid-token handling, CORS) via direct `curl` against the live API using the exact shapes the frontend code sends/expects, server-render smoke tests for every route, and full build/typecheck/lint passes. A real click-through pass (actual form submission, visual permission-based sidebar filtering, reload-keeps-session, watching redirects happen) should be done — by a human, or a future unit with browser-automation tooling — before treating this foundation as fully proven, not just contract-correct.
- **`packages/shared`'s raw-`.ts`-source packaging problem (flagged since Unit 2) now also affects the web app**, not just `apps/api` — this unit avoided importing `@recafco/shared` for the same reason every backend unit did, but Next.js's `transpilePackages` option could plausibly consume the raw source as-is without needing the CommonJS-build fix `packages/db` got. Worth reconsidering once the frontend has a genuine reason to import a shared enum/schema (e.g., a shared Zod schema for a form that also validates on the backend) rather than duplicating it.
- **`eslint-config-next`'s move to natively-flat exported configs (confirmed while fixing the `FlatCompat` crash) may affect other advice/tutorials that still reference the older `compat.extends(...)` pattern** — worth remembering this project is on Next 16/ESLint 9, not the Next 14/ESLint 8 combination most existing Next.js tutorials assume, if lint config ever needs revisiting.
- **The `(app)` route group currently has exactly one real page (`dashboard`) and ten placeholders** — as Unit 12+ build out real master-data/attendance screens, decide whether they stay directly under `(app)/<route>` (current placeholder structure) or need nested route groups (e.g., per-domain layouts for shared toolbars/filters) once real tables/forms exist; not a decision this foundation unit needed to make.

### Unit 12 (Web Admin Master Data Screens) Open Questions

- **Full interactive browser verification still has not been performed** (Units 11 and 12 both) — no browser-automation tool has been available in either unit. A real click-through pass (form submission, dropdown selection, pagination clicks, permission-based button visibility, deactivate/terminate confirm flow) should be done by a human, or a future unit with browser-automation tooling, before treating these screens as fully proven rather than contract-correct.
- **Dropdown data sources (department/designation/work-location pickers on the Employees page) are capped at `limit: 100` with no search-as-you-type** — reasonable for a real company's master-data counts (tens, not thousands, of departments/designations/sites) but will not scale if any of those lists ever grows past 100 active records. A searchable combobox (still no new dependency needed — could reuse the existing `SearchInput` pattern client-side, or add server-side search to the same dropdown query) would be the natural upgrade if that ever becomes a real constraint.
- **No optimistic UI updates** — every create/update/deactivate/terminate mutation waits for the server response before closing its modal and refetching the list (via `queryClient.invalidateQueries`). This is the simpler, safer default for a foundation unit (matches "keep it simple"), but means each save has a visible round-trip delay. Revisit with `useMutation`'s optimistic-update options only if real usage shows this is a UX problem worth the added complexity.
- **The Employees page's three dropdown-source queries always fetch, even when only the (read-only) list is being viewed and neither `employees.create` nor `employees.update` is held** — gated behind `enabled: canRead && (canCreate || canUpdate)`, so a pure-read role (e.g. `AUDITOR`/`MANAGEMENT_VIEWER`) correctly skips them, but this is worth double-checking against the final permission matrix once real non-admin roles start using this screen day-to-day.
- **`DataTable` is a small hand-rolled component, not `@tanstack/react-table`** (which `ui-context.md` lists as a recommended library) — deliberate for this unit since none of the 4 screens need sorting, column visibility toggles, or complex multi-column filtering yet. Revisit and adopt `@tanstack/react-table` if/when a future master-data or attendance screen genuinely needs those features, rather than retrofitting it reactively.
- **No debounce/loading indicator distinguishes "typing" from "server is fetching" in `SearchInput`** — the 350ms debounce is invisible to the user; a brief loading spinner or dimmed table state during the actual network round-trip (distinct from the `isLoading` state already shown for the very first page load) could be a small, cheap polish item for a later pass.

### Unit 13 (Web Admin Device and Mobile Device Screens) Open Questions

- **The `@Type(() => Boolean)` boolean-query-filter bug (see Completed notes) was found and fixed for the 5 endpoints this unit actually touched or newly built filters for — a repo-wide `grep` confirmed exactly 5 occurrences and all 5 were fixed, but any *future* list endpoint must use the new `parseQueryBoolean`/`@Transform` pattern from the start, not reintroduce `@Type(() => Boolean)` for a boolean query param.** Worth a brief mention in `code-standards.md` or a lint rule if this class of bug recurs.
- **Full interactive browser verification still has not been performed** across Units 11, 12, and 13 — no browser-automation tool has been available in any of them. This is now a 3-unit-deep gap; strongly recommend a real human (or automated) click-through pass before Unit 14 adds more screens on top of an increasingly large, never-visually-verified surface area.
- **The Mobile Devices page's `employeeId` filter is a plain text input, not a searchable employee picker** — deliberate for this foundation unit (the task only asked for an "employeeId" filter, not a combobox), but an admin would need to already know or look up the CUID elsewhere to use it meaningfully. A searchable employee-picker component (reusable across this page and any future screen needing one) is a natural candidate for a later polish pass, once combobox-style UI is justified by more than one screen needing it.
- **Approve always uses the backend's own default (`isPrimary` omitted → `true`)** — no UI toggle was added to let an admin approve a device as *non*-primary in one step (they'd need to approve, then look at the resulting state, which — since approving already demotes the previous primary — means every fresh approval always becomes primary by default; there's no current combination of actions in this UI that leaves a newly-approved device as non-primary without a second `PATCH`-adjacent action, which doesn't exist since `isPrimary` isn't editable outside `/approve`). Revisit if HR ever needs "approve a spare/backup device without making it primary" as a real workflow.
- **Employee-relation lookups (`useQueries` over unique `employeeId`s per page) re-fetch on every distinct page/filter combination the first time it's seen**, cached thereafter by React Query's query cache (`staleTime: 60_000`). This is correct and scales with page size, not total employee count, but means a very "busy" admin session paging through many different device pages will accumulate many small per-employee cache entries — harmless at this scale, worth reconsidering only if this data pattern is reused somewhere with much higher page-turnover.

### Unit 14 (Web Admin Attendance Operations Screens) Open Questions

- **The `attendanceCorrections.request` vs. `.approve` discrepancy between this unit's task text and the actual, already-shipped Unit 10 backend behavior** was resolved in favor of the real backend (see Completed notes) — worth reconciling the two task-writing sources of truth (whatever generates unit specs vs. `rbac.constants.ts`) so a future unit's task text doesn't repeat a permission requirement that was deliberately tightened as a security fix two units earlier.
- **Full interactive browser verification still has not been performed** across Units 11, 12, 13, and now 14 — no browser-automation tool has been available in any of them. This is now a 4-unit-deep gap covering 9 distinct admin screens; strongly recommend a real human (or automated) click-through pass before Unit 15 (Mobile App Foundation) begins, and definitely before any of this UI is handed to real HR/admin users.
- **No payroll-lock API endpoint exists yet** (`AttendanceSummariesController` only has `calculate`/`recalculate-range` — no `lock`/`unlock` routes), even though `AttendanceDailySummary.lockedAt` and a whole `attendance_payroll_locks` table already exist in the schema (from Unit 1) and the `attendance.payroll.lock`/`.unlock` permissions already exist (from Unit 3). This unit's locked-summary error-display paths were verified by directly flipping `lockedAt` via `psql` rather than through a real API — a genuine future unit (payroll lock workflow) needs to build the actual lock/unlock endpoints and reconcile them with the `attendance_payroll_locks` table, which nothing has written to yet.
- **The corrections detail modal renders `beforeSnapshot`/`afterSnapshot` as raw formatted JSON**, not a human-readable diff — acceptable for this admin/HR-operations-only screen per the task's scope, but a nicer before/after comparison view (e.g., a table of changed fields) would be a natural polish candidate once this screen sees real usage patterns.
- **The Attendance Punches page's employee/work-location/device filter dropdowns are capped at 100 rows** (same documented scaling concern as Units 12/13's dropdown-sourcing pattern) — fine at current data volumes, revisit with a searchable combobox if the 2000+ employee target is ever fully onboarded and a plain capped dropdown stops being usable.

### Unit 15 (Mobile App Foundation) Open Questions

- **No Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** to actually run and click through the app — verification was limited to `tsc`/`eslint` passing and directly fetching compiled Android/iOS JS bundles from a live Metro dev server (confirmed they contain this unit's real source, not an empty/error bundle). A real emulator/device pass (login submission, session-restore-on-reload, logout, actual network calls against a running backend) is still needed before trusting this foundation further — see the exact verification commands documented in the Completed notes above.
- **The `zod`/`@hookform/resolvers` cross-package version conflict found in this unit** (a transitive `zod@3.25.76` with an embedded v4-compat shim colliding with the explicitly-installed `zod@4.1.13` when typechecked from `apps/mobile` specifically) was worked around by not using Zod in the mobile login form, not fixed at the root. If a future mobile unit (Unit 16's device registration form, or any future multi-field mobile form) wants schema validation, this conflict needs a real fix first — likely a pnpm `overrides`/`resolutions` entry forcing a single zod resolution repo-wide, or investigating why `apps/web` doesn't hit the same collision despite using the identical `@hookform/resolvers` version.
- **The Metro `disableHierarchicalLookup: true` finding is repo-specific tribal knowledge now, only captured in this file** — worth a one-line mention in `code-standards.md`'s Mobile/React Native section (or directly as a comment in `metro.config.js`, which it already has) so a future contributor doesn't reintroduce it by copy-pasting a generic Expo monorepo guide snippet.
- **No path alias (`@/*`) is wired up for mobile imports**, unlike `apps/web` — deliberately deferred until a Babel module-resolver config can be added and verified against a real bundle load on a real device/emulator (see Completed notes). All current mobile source uses relative imports instead.
- **`app.json` has no `icon`/`splash` asset configured** — these are branding/design assets out of scope for a foundation unit; Expo will use its own placeholder default. A real app icon and splash screen belong to a design/branding pass before this app is ever distributed to real users (even internally via EAS/Expo Go).
- **Full interactive browser verification for the web admin screens (Units 11–14) still has not been performed** — carried forward from Unit 14's open question, now also joined by the equivalent mobile gap above. Both should be closed before Unit 16 adds a real device-registration flow (camera/permission prompts, actual device binding) on top of an unverified foundation.

### Unit 16 (Mobile Device Registration Screen) Open Questions

- **No Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** — the same gap carried forward from Unit 15, now 2 units deep. Full backend contract verification was performed live via curl (register/approve/block/reset/duplicate/no-employee/anonymous all confirmed), and both Android/iOS bundles were confirmed to compile with the new screen's code, but no one has actually tapped the Register button on a real device yet.
- **Re-registering a `BLOCKED` or `DEACTIVATED` device only refreshes metadata — it never changes `status`** (confirmed by reading `MobileDevicesService.register`'s update-in-place branch directly), so the task's specified message for `DEACTIVATED` ("Register again or contact HR.") is accurate in that re-registering *is* a legitimate, useful action (it updates `lastSeenAt`/metadata, signaling to HR the employee is still using the device) but does **not**, by itself, restore `APPROVED` status — only an admin's `/approve` (Unit 13's web screen) does that. This nuance is not visually surfaced on the mobile screen beyond the task's exact specified copy; consider whether a future polish pass should clarify "this won't un-block/reactivate the device on its own" more explicitly.
- **The `401`-triggers-`logout()` fix closes a real gap left open since Unit 15**: `authorizedRequest`'s existing token-clearing on `401` never updated in-memory `AuthContext` state, so a mid-session `401` would clear SecureStore but leave the UI looking authenticated until the next full app restart. This unit's Device screen now calls the existing `logout()` on any `401` it encounters, which fixes it *for this screen's own calls* — but no other authenticated call site exists yet to have the same problem. Worth a repo-wide check once more authenticated endpoints exist (Unit 17+): either every call site needs the same `401`-triggers-`logout()` handling, or `authorizedRequest` itself should centralize it (e.g. via a registered callback), rather than repeating this pattern per screen.
- **The device-identifier fallback (persisted UUID) is untested on a real device** — Android's `getAndroidId()` and iOS's `getIosIdForVendorAsync()` paths could not be exercised on real hardware in this environment (no emulator/device), so only the fallback path's logic was verified by code review and the bundle-compile check, not a live run. Confirm on a real Android and iOS device before relying on this for anything beyond this foundation unit.

### Unit 17 (Mobile Attendance Punch Screen) Open Questions

- **No Android emulator, iOS simulator, or physical device was available in this environment** — the same gap carried forward from Units 15–16, now 3 units deep, and now the most consequential instance of it: GPS acquisition (`getCurrentPositionAsync`), the real permission-prompt UX flow, and the `mocked` flag's real-world behavior on Android have only been verified by reading `expo-location`'s own type declarations and confirming the app bundles compile — none of it has run on real hardware.
